- `BossDefeat`: shared map flags (group 1/2, `k` in 0,1,4,5,6,7, not per-player) first switched on from 20 s before to 15 s after a defeat signal: our HandleBossDefeat, the friend's `EV_BOSS(2)`, or our boss gauge going away while the boss is loaded at 0 HP and we live (minibosses, if they use the gauge) [I].
- `ObjAct`: the same map flags switched on from 2 s before to 10 s after us or the friend playing an ObjActParam `playerAnimId` (doors, gates, levers, shortcuts) [I: compared as the full TimeAct id and modulo 1 000 000, not checked live].
- **Not merged** (back to the joiner's own value): global group 0 flags (time of day 9800-9802, Demon Bell 9830, ...), group 6, and map flags that changed outside the windows. NPC quest state: merged per NPC unit since §19 (`merge_quests`; 0 = the old behaviour, back to the joiner's own value).
- Per player, kept at their current value on restore: item / shop flags, **skill flags** (new: SkillParam `unlockEventFlag` joined world.rs's per-player set, so learned skills are neither synced nor overwritten), death penalty 9500-9599. Items, sen, XP, level and skill points live in PlayerGameData, which the snapshot never touches.

**Fixed on the way.** `own_item_flags` only kept item flags in records the host had sent; a record of ours that the host had all zero was zeroed with our item flags in it. It now covers every record that is zeroed.

**Restore** (game thread): when we are no longer a connected client for 2 s (intent Solo/Host: Severed Thread, the host closed its world, title, we host now), 10 s (no session object) or 45 s (link lost; a reconnect makes a new snapshot instead), at least 5 s in the world, and the warp gate allows it (no menu, talk, death, load; retried until it does): fold, zero our shared records, write the backup + progress + per-player flags (raw, no SetEventFlag: nothing fires, nothing is sent), warp to our last idol (`game::warp_to_idol(game::last_idol())`, puppet lets go like after the join warp), delete the file, notice "Back in your own world. Progress from the shared world was kept.".

**Crash / quit.** The file is written before anything is zeroed and stays until a restore. 8 s after any load of the same slot with no client session it is restored as above; a join snapshot that comes first continues from it. Title screen mid-session: on the next load of that slot the restore runs after 5 s (intent is Solo after the title). A crash between the backup and the copy leaves a file without a baseline: it restores the backup with no progress.

**Log lines.** `own world: backed up our own world before the join snapshot: N non-zero shared records (B B), save slot S, sen total Some(X)`, `own world: baseline = the host's world (...); saved to ... (ok = true)`, `own world: join snapshot #K over the backup of T (slot S), N progress flags folded in`, `own world: N idol flags, M game progress flags [...], K ObjAct player anims [...]`, `own world: our boss defeated, ...` / `friend's boss defeated` / `boss gauge of E went away with the boss dead`, `own world: we played ObjAct animation A, ...`, `own world: flag F is session progress (BossDefeat|ObjAct)`, `own world: N new progress flags (T in total)`, `own world: RESTORED save slot S: W records written (M not resolvable), P progress flags merged {"boss": .., "idol": ..} [...], K per-player flags kept, ...`, `own world: reloading at our last idol I ...`, `own world: ... deleted`, `own world: found an unfinished session of save slot S ...`, `own world: the file of slot S belongs to another character ...`, `world: N item/shop/skill flags are per player (..., K skill flags)`.

**Live test (two PCs):**
1. B (joiner) on a save that has killed the Chained Ogre and opened some doors the host has not; A (host) on a save that has killed Gyoubu, which B has not. B joins: `own world: backed up ...`, `baseline ...`, the file appears in B's `%LOCALAPPDATA%\sekiro-coop`. In A's world: Ogre alive, Gyoubu dead (as before).
2. Together: kill a miniboss and a boss B never killed, unlock an idol B never had, open a shortcut door. Look for `flag F is session progress` lines for the boss and the door, and `N new progress flags`.
3. B uses Severed Thread: within a few seconds `own world: RESTORED ...`, an idol warp, the notice. In B's world: Ogre dead (own), Gyoubu alive (the host's old kill is not B's), the session's boss and miniboss dead, the new idol in the travel menu, the door open. Items picked up in the session still in the inventory and not lying in the world again; skills unchanged. NPC quests as before the session. The file is gone.
4. Repeat 1-2, then end B's game in Task Manager. Start again, load the same save, stay solo: after ~8 s `found an unfinished session ...`, restore, warp. Loading another save first does nothing.
5. A closes its world (Severed Thread) or quits to the title: B restores. B quits to the title mid-session and loads the same save: restore after ~5 s.
6. A death and a rest during the session still mirror as before (`world: we died ...`, `rested at idol`).
7. If `flag ... (ObjAct)` never appears for doors, the anim ids do not match the param format: compare the `own world: K ObjAct player anims [...]` list with `anim: player current animation` and fix `objact_anim`.

**Risks.** The window categories are heuristics [I]: a flag of something else switched on inside a window is merged too, and a boss/door flag switched on outside one (the host opening a door far away with no anim seen, a miniboss whose gauge we never saw) stays off. The GameProgressParam meaning and the `total_sen_earned` offset are inferred. A new character in a reused slot that has already earned more sen than the old one passes the fingerprint. A friend who connects to us as host in the 2 s before our restore gets the pre-restore world. Map flags merged on that EMEVD normally resets may stay on (they reload through the idol warp; not verified). Skill flags no longer sync between players (intended; they were synced before if their record was shared).

## 18. Native session: the game's own netcode over our link (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

Option `native_session` ([ADVANCED], default 0) and `native_session_stage` (1..3, default 2), code in `nsession.rs`, spec `SPEC-NATIVE-SESSION.md`. Both players must use the same values. With 0 nothing is installed, patched or sent (the module only keeps `OWNS_ENEMIES` false; packets of ours from a friend are consumed and dropped). Every RVA and byte was re-read in IDA on a private copy (`F:\Temp\ida_ns\db.i64`, job `o1`): all §1, §2.1, §2.2, §2.3 and §4 bytes match the spec tables. The chr-set layout for the netId check (`WCM+0x1C8` count, `+0x1D0` set pointers 24 B apart, entries 56 B apart) is from `0xA4DB20`.

**What is implemented, per spec section.**
- §1 call-site fakes: the 9 InSession sites (TRUE), the 7 IsHost/IsClient sites (by role), the two member-count calls (`mov eax, 2`), the three inline counts (`mov ecx, 0x70` + 7 nops), and on the client the `0xA4BA93` `test al,al -> xor al,al` patch when `GameMan+0xBA6 != 0`. Not patched, as the spec says: the `0xA1E140`/`0xA1E9F0` join snapshot, member join/leave handlers, `0x8E4B10`, the `0x832050` sites, EMEVD/ESD (§5), `0x8522B0`.
- §2.1 five entry detours (`hook::install_detour`, prologues relocated): `0x8E7210` broadcast, `0x8E7310` to host (client only), `0x8E6FA0` to member (our fake member, or an empty member as `0xA29DD0` gives for type 23), `0x8E6B50` recv from our fake member, `0x8E6D20` recv from host (client only). Whitelisted types are queued for the link and the original is not run (it would send nothing with an empty member vector); everything else passes through. One packet per recv call, 0 when the queue is empty, oversized ones dropped and counted (`big`).
- §2.2 fake member vector: one RWX page near the module holds a zeroed fake `S` (`+0x160/+0x168` = one empty 56-byte member) and a stub `lea rax,[FAKE_S+0x158]; ret`. Patched: the four `mov reg,[S]` -> `lea reg,[FAKE_S]` in recv 70 / recv 4 and the `call 0x8E4010` in recv 71. The page is leaked on purpose.
- §2.3 (stage 3) the any-member receivers `0x8D77B0` (21), `0x8D7320` (28), `0x8D7290` (52), `0x8D7D70` (18), `0x8D8260` (42) are detoured (not replaced, so they pass through when off) and serve our queues. Type 45 / `0x6BC504` (spec stage 4) is not done.
- §2.4 transport, no protocol change: the unreliable types (4, 5) are PlayerRaw packets of type `0x4E530000 | type` with a `u32 generation` prefix; the reliable types (22, 23, 70, 71, 21, 28, 52, 18, 42) are batched (up to 64 KB) into bulk transfers of kind `0x4E` while fewer than 32 bulk chunks are unacked. The handshake travels in the same bulk kind: `Hello{proto, stage, host, block, generation}`, `List{generation, block, [(entity id, netId)]}`, `Ready{generation, peer_generation}`, `Leave{generation, latch, reason}`. Queue depths as in the spec (4: 32, 5/70/71: 16, 22/23: 4, the small ones: 32), 1 MB cap on the reliable outbox. Only packets carrying the friend's current generation are accepted.
- §3: the remote is native.rs's N1 remote (required: `native_remote = 1`); its handle `chr+8` keys the host's ownership record. Player netIds in 21 (`+0x00`, `+0x0C`) and 28 (`+0x00`, [I]) are rewritten to "sender" `0xFFF0` / "receiver" `0xFFF1` on send and back to our remote / our player on receive. Only N = 2.
- §4: the host feeds each received 22 with `0xA1D7E0(T, remote handle)` + `0xA1C6C0(rec, n, list)` on our frame (payload checked: `len == 4(n+1)`, `0 <= n <= 1023`). Fallback rules: friend in another block (a known block only; a hidden snapshot is not "elsewhere"), no native remote for 3 s, client without a served 23 within 5 s of engaging (soft, the third time per load latches), netId mismatch (latched), friend left / restarted / switched off.
- §5 and §6 not used (EMEVD unpatched on purpose; our scaling.rs stays).
- §7: handshake -> netId check over the enemies both have loaded (duplicate entity ids left out) -> Ready -> engage. Engage installs the hooks (if not yet; they go in at the title screen when the setting is 1 at start) and, from stage 2, checks every patch site and applies all or none. Disengage (setting off, link down, load / our warp / player re-created, frame panic, the fallback rules) turns routing off first, then restores every patch byte for byte in reverse order and empties the queues. A failed byte check or a failed restore switches the mode off for the rest of the run. The detours stay in as pass-through once installed.

**Steps aside while the native session owns the enemies** (`nsession::step_aside`: from the friend's agreeing hello until a latched fallback or the setting goes off): enemies.rs's mode-2/3/4 sync (NetAI vtable / slot 37, record writes and sending, HP / death authority, damage reports, lethal hold, throw window, UpdateCoords pinning, CompletelyNoMove) and activation.rs's forced update interval near the friend. Kept: the enemies.rs scan and loot step (its scan replaces the client's host list for scaling and loot), the P3 dead list (type 5 is host -> client only, it cannot tell the host which enemies died on the client), world.rs, rest / Unseen Aid / boss rules, items, scaling, artfx (type 63 is not whitelisted: the owner needs our playerNo rewrite; the damage table is vanilla since the artfx review fix). observe.rs skips its two hooks on `0x8E7210` / `0x8E6B50` when `native_session = 1` at start (nsession counts those calls itself).

**Log lines.** `nsession: hooks 10/10 installed`, `fake S at ...`, `handshake generation G stage S as host|client in block B: N enemies ...`, `friend hello / netId list / ready / left`, `waiting: <reason>` (every 10 s), `netId check: K/K match`, `patches role=host|client applied X/X`, `ENGAGED stage S ...`, `host 22 from peer: n cand`, `23 rx: owned here C`, `sync table ... timers +0x398..` (once), every 10 s `types (10 s) <type>: tx rx srv drop big | ...`, `hook calls <hook> n (routed r) [type:count ...]` and `owners: Com A / NetAI B / other / owned-here C`, `DISENGAGED (why)`, `patches restored X/X`, `enemies are the game's own / ours again`.

**Live test, in this order (two players, same build, both `native_session = 1`):**
1. Stage 1 (`native_session_stage = 1`): connect, same area. Expect `hooks 10/10`, the handshake, `netId check: K/K match` with K > 0, `ENGAGED stage 1 ... routing off`. The game must behave exactly as with 0 (our own enemy sync still runs at stage 1). Read `hook calls`: which types the game tries to send (expected 26, 21/28 on hits, 52, 18) and that the recv calls happen every frame.
2. Stage 2: `patches ... applied 29/29` (client 30/30 with the `0xBA6` patch), `host 22 from peer: n cand`, `23 rx: owned here C` on the client with C > 0, `types` lines with 4/70/71 tx on the owner and srv on the other side, 5 host -> client, `big 0`, drops near 0. Enemies near the client must move and attack on both screens; walk apart and back: ownership moves (`owned-here` changes). The client's damage on host-owned enemies may not count yet (the damage packets are stage 3).
3. Stage 3: a host enemy hitting the client lowers the client's HP once, on the client's machine; the client's hits on host-owned enemies count. Watch 21/28 tx/rx/srv.
4. Fallbacks: one player warps to another area (both disengage, `patches restored`), comes back (new handshake); one sets `native_session = 0` mid-session (the other logs `friend left (SettingOff)` and `enemies are ours again`); every load must show `patches restored`.

**Risks.** Patches and detours are written while the game runs (FrameBegin); a game thread executing one of those bytes at that instant would crash (the spec accepts FrameBegin; the hooks go in at the title when the setting is on at start). Damage authority at stage 2 is unknown [I]: the vanilla damage table makes remote-player hits reaction-only, so enemy damage likely needs 21 (stage 3). The 5 s rule for the first 23 may be shorter than the game's own distribution timer (`T+0x398`, logged once). Behavior packets (70) can be up to 20 KB: the reliable pipe carries about 16 chunks per round trip, so a slow link queues (watch `reliable outbox`). Enemies nobody owns stay NetAI without data (DS3's own behaviour far from both players). Switching the setting on mid-session only takes effect after the next load if the friend had latched a fallback.

### 18b. Update: the native session is the main path (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

Owner decision: everything the game can do natively runs natively. Defaults are now `native_session = 1`, `native_session_stage = 3` (range 1..4). The parts of the first version above that changed:

- **Stages.** 1 counters only; 2 enemies = 4/5/22/23/70/71 **plus the hit packet 21**; 3 = + 28/52/18/42; 4 = + ObjAct 45 (experimental, not default). IDA (`F:\Temp\ida_ns` o2): the damage router `0xB6A040` picks an action from `[victim class][attacker class]` (`0xB6F4C0`: 1 local player, 2 remote player, 3/4 slot-54 chrs owned here / elsewhere, 5/6 others owned here / elsewhere). A local player's hit on an enemy owned elsewhere (action 3) is applied locally AND broadcast as 21; on an enemy owned here, a client sends 21 to the host (action 2). 21 = `{u32 victim chr+0x78, i16 HP damage, i16 posture (+484), i16 (+488), u8 flag, u8, u32 attacker chr+0x78}`; the receiver applies it through `0xA4F940` -> `0x8D77B0` -> `0xB680C0`/`0xB68FF0`. Hits of the remote player's replica are reaction only (action 0), enemy hits on the local player are computed on the victim's machine (action 1), so nothing counts twice. That is why 21 moved into stage 2 (review finding b: without it the client's hits on host-owned enemies did nothing). Our DamageReport and posture.rs reports stay off whenever the native session owns the enemies (posture.rs asks `step_aside::enemy_sync_off` itself); the first packets of 21/28/52/18/42/45 are logged as hex (`nsession: evidence tx|rx type T ...`) as the evidence the owner asked for before the custom paths are deleted.
- **Player netIds**: the damage router writes `chr+0x78`; both that and the `0xA4CE60` form are rewritten on send, the receiver gets `chr+0x78` values.
- **Own reliable lane** (review finding a): the handshake and every reliable game packet now go through `nsession::lane`, a selective-repeat ARQ over PlayerRaw (`0x4E5300FE` data, `0x4E5300FF` acks), not through coop-net's bulk pipe. Fragments of 1000 B, own sequence numbers, re-sent every 200 ms (40 tries), 128 fragments in flight of which at most 96 for behavior (70), three priority classes (ownership 22/23 + handshake first, small packets, behavior last), in-order delivery per type, a gap older than 3 s is skipped. A 20 KB type-70 packet can no longer hold back 23 or a world flag event. Unit tests: loss + reorder, overtaking, duplicates, foreign acks, a restarted peer, gap skip. `PROTO` = 2 (the old bulk handshake is ignored with one log line).
- **Patches with the game's threads stopped** (review finding c): every call-site patch and its restore is written by `freeze::write_all`: all other threads of the process suspended, their RIP checked against every target range (a thread inside one = retry, up to 20 times per frame, then next frame, up to 10 s), the old bytes checked, the new ones written, everything resumed; nothing allocates or logs while threads are stopped. A restore that stays busy falls back to plain writes (logged). The entry detours still go in with plain writes, but at the title screen (the setting is on at start by default); switching the setting on later installs them in the world (documented risk).
- **Fallback** (review finding e): any failure (netId mismatch, no 23, a check, the friend's latched fallback) disengages at once, but the enemies stay with the game's own AI on both machines for the rest of that world; our own sync (`enemy_mode`, 4 = NetAI is the tested one; modes 2/3 are deprecated) takes over at the next load, for that one world, and the world after it tries the native session again. Only an explicit `native_session = 0` (either side) switches at once. A failed byte check or restore keeps the native session off for the rest of the run (fallback from the next load on).
- **Host death** (owner decision: a host death does not reset the world): on every engagement the netId list carries each enemy's HP, and enemies alive on both machines take the lower of the two HPs on both, before routing starts. The host's death reload therefore cannot heal the client's damaged enemies through type 5; dead ones are restored by the existing P3 dead list, which marks them "no loot", so no kill is rewarded twice. Log: `HP check on engage: n enemies took the friend's lower HP`. Unit test `host_death_reload_never_heals_or_kills`.
- **Stage 4, ObjAct (type 45)**: `0x6BC1B0` (ObjAct execution: the actor chr plays its animation, the object changes state) sends `{u16 actor chr+0x78, u8 slot | flags, u8, u16 object id}` through `0x8DB7E0` when `0x6BE270()` (member count > 0, patched TRUE at `0x6BC504`) is true; `0x6BEBB0` takes them from `0x8D8750` (detoured, cap 6). world.rs stays the authority for progress: the flag the ObjAct writes on the receiver is the value world.rs brings anyway (a write to the current value changes nothing, world.rs only sends changes), so whichever arrives first applies and the other is a no-op; 45 adds the friend's animation and the object's immediate state. Type 47 (`0x8DA640`, host only, behind the unpatched global IsHost `0x8E4790`) is not routed.
- **Stage 5, SpEffects: not implemented, nothing to route.** IDA o2/o3: `0x9E9D10` (add SpEffect, callers `0x9E9C00` and the damage apply `0xB6E6A0`) has no packet: on a remote player it applies nothing (PlayerIns slot 121 `0xA239B0` only acts for the local player), on the local player it skips effects from a remote attacker, on other chrs it applies only when `0xA4A600` (the chr is in `WCM+0x3130..0x3138`) is true. Status effects come with the hits [I]: every machine computes the hits on what it owns (and on its own player), so burn / poison / buffs should follow natively from stages 2/3; whether an enemy owned elsewhere shows the effect depends on what `WCM+0x3130` holds (not checked; watch it live). Nothing of ours is sent, so our death-debuff rows (`speffect::is_own_row`) never leave the machine.
- **Bullets (type 63) stay with artfx.rs** (`step_aside::NATIVE_BULLETS = false`): both local players have the same playerNo in their own game, and our remote needs another one, so the owner field of every received record must be rewritten anyway at the call sites in `0xB1D600` that artfx.rs patches; a second copy of that would be artfx.rs again. nsession never routes 63 (unit test), so exactly one bullet path is active.
- **Scaling (§6): ours stays, no `native_scaling`.** The game's hook point (`0xC04EA4` / `0xC04FFA`, bytes re-checked) multiplies only the rate of SpEffects flagged NG-correctable (`row+0x168 & 0x40`) for every chr that has one, cannot tell bosses from enemies there (that needs another hook on `0xC01940` and a boss lookup), and only takes effect when effects are re-applied. scaling.rs sets each enemy's base max HP / posture from NpcParam with separate enemy / boss percentages, uses the host's values on both machines and is already synced. Not clearly better, so not done.
- **Pre-release review fixes.** (#5) `OWNS_ENEMIES` is now true only while the session is ENGAGED at stage >= 2: during settle, remote spawn, handshake, cooldown, a friend elsewhere and after any disengagement our own sync (mode 4) runs, so one of the two always does; enemies.rs hands its NetAI enemies back the frame after engaging. This replaces the "never mid-fight" rule above: a failure now hands the enemies back to our sync at once, the latch only keeps the native session from re-engaging until the next load (and off for the world after a latched failure). (#4) A thread that exits between the snapshot and `GetThreadContext` is let go instead of failing the write. (#2) The frozen writer is now `hook::freeze` / `hook::write_frozen`, and `hook::install_detour` writes every entry jump with the threads suspended and none inside the prologue (icons' boot-time detour included). (#6) Lane partials older than 30 s are dropped. (#7) The engage HP adoption uses the game's setter `0xBD64E0` (`posture::set_hp`). activation.rs reads enemy positions with plain loads (three ReadProcessMemory calls per enemy per frame were the host's FPS cost) and stays off only while the session is engaged.
- **Live 2026-10-23 (build 6.5): never engaged.** The host logged `patch check failed: no memory near the module for the fake session`. Every stub took its own 64 KB-granular region, searched only 1 GB downwards. Fix (hook.rs): one 4 MB RWX near arena, reserved on the first allocation at start, carved page-aligned for every stub and fake page; if it is full, a region is searched in the whole +-1.75 GB window in both directions. Startup line `hook: near arena A..B: X of 4096 KB used by N allocations, M outside it`. The fake session page is now taken at the title screen. A side whose native session cannot run (hook / patch / memory check) shows `Native session unavailable: <reason>` on the overlay once and tells the friend with a stage-0 hello. The friend then logs and shows `Native session unavailable on the friend's side` and starts no handshakes on that link, so both stay on our sync from that moment.
- **What steps aside** (`nsession::step_aside`, the single switch; when a stage is proven live, the custom code it replaces can be deleted there): enemies.rs's mode-2/3/4 sync including DamageReport, posture.rs's reports (through `enemy_sync_off`), activation.rs.
- **Live test order** as above, with stage 2 now including hits: after `ENGAGED`, the client hitting a host-owned enemy must lower its HP on both screens once (`types` line: 21 tx on the client, rx/srv on the host). Then stage 3 (default), then 4. Host death test: damage an enemy on the client, let the host die and reload: `HP check on engage: 1 ...` on the host, the enemy keeps the lower HP on both.
- **Live 7.1 (2026-10-23 13:30-13:41) and the fixes after it (NOT verified live).** Good: near arena `368 of 4096 KB, 0 outside`, `ENGAGED stage 3 as host`, 4/5/22/23/28/21/18 evidence. Three problems, root causes from the two logs and IDA (`F:\Temp\ida_ns2`, jobs o1-o9):
  - *Fallback latched after the host's death (No23 x3).* After the host's death reload the host's world update did not run for ~45 s (pause / death UI): its own `T host` position stood still, `observe: calls` showed no NetAI function at all (normally ~600 / 10 s), and the sync-table timers read `[3.0, 2.98, 5.0, 4.98]` identically at generations 4, 5 and 6 (a table created on the load and ticked once). IDA: `0xA1DD00` (23) and `0xA1CED0` (22) are only called from `0xA1D2D0` / `0xA4E520`, which only `0xA509F0` calls, WorldChrManImp's update task (`SprjEzUpdateTask`, set up in `0xA46B80`); `0xA509F0` increments `WCM+0x678` every update. `T+0x39C` is the 23 countdown (reload from `+0x398` = 3 s), `T+0x3A4` the 22 countdown (`+0x3A0` = 5 s); the client's `+0x39C` running to -200 is normal (unused there). So the host engaged with a frozen sync table, never sent 23, and the client's 5 s rule failed three times. Fixes (`nsession.rs`): (1) no handshake step while our own world update is still (`WCM+0x678` unchanged for 0.5 s; both roles; no timeout meanwhile): `waiting: our world update is paused (menu / death screen?) ...`; (2) on engage the host sets `T+0x39C = 0` (23 on its next update) and again after the first 22 of the engagement, the client sets `T+0x3A4 = 0` (22 at once): `sync table timers at engage [..]; 23 distribution | 22 send forced to the next update: true`, `host: first 22 of this engagement (n cand, t ms after engage): 23 distribution forced ...`; (3) the client's 5 s rule starts with the first packet of the host's sync (4/5/70/71 come from the same `0xA1D2D0` as 23): `the host's sync runs (N packets, t ms after engage); its first 23 is due within 5 s`, failure line `no ownership list (23) within 5 s of the host's sync packets (N, timers here [..]), failure k`; a host that sends nothing for 30 s is left without a soft fail or latch (`the host's sync sent nothing for 30 s (its world paused?)`). While engaged a stopped world is logged once (`our world update stopped while engaged (menu?) ...` / `runs again`).
  - *"The mod teleports a player constantly near the other".* Type 18 is the throw / grab / deathblow packet (`0x7FAB00` <- `0x8D7D70`, IDA o6/o7): two positions, `+0x20` defender netId, `+0x22` attacker netId, `+0x24` throw id; the receiver places both chrs at the two positions and starts the throw. It was routed without the player-netId rewrite, and both machines' own player is `0xA000`: every deathblow / throw / grab of one player put the OTHER player's own character into it at the sender's positions (live evidence: `tx type 18 ... 21 08 00 a0` = enemy 0x821, attacker 0xA000). Type 28 had the same problem: its chrs are at `+0x04` (the one the record is applied to: `0xA4F940` resolves HIDWORD(rec[0]) -> `0xB63FE0`, IDA o8) and `+0x08`; `+0` (0/1/4/8 in the evidence) is not a netId, so the old field list `[+0x00]` rewrote nothing. Now `pnet_fields`: 18 = `+0x20, +0x22`, 28 = `+0x04, +0x08` (unit test with the live bytes). `PROTO` = 3 (an older friend no longer engages). The position pin itself (`follow_native`) is the only writer of the remote's position (the UpdateCoords stub overwrites Havok's output), so it does not fight the NetworkManipulator; target jumps (late snapshots) between the normal per-frame motion (40 m/s x frame time, min 1.5 m) and 8 m are now eased (25 % of the gap per frame) instead of set at once, >= 8 m is set at once. Log: `native: pin snap D m in T ms (late snapshot?), eased`, `native: pin warp D m (the friend warped), set at once`, `native: remote moved D m off our pin by the game since the last frame` (first 30 lines), and every 10 s when anything happened `native: pin (10 s): N snaps eased (max D m), W warps, M moves by the game (max D m)`.
  - *Enemies frozen / walking in place, the Chained Ogre stuck.* (a) After every engage, before the first 23, the client owned nothing: with the chr-loop member count patched, slot 37 gave every enemy NetAI without data (frozen, walking in place); with the No23 loop above that was 3 x 5 s. Now the client applies the chr-loop patch (`0xA4DC38`) only when its first 23 arrives (`patches role=client applied 29/30, the members chr loop waits for the first 23 ...`, then `first 23 t ms after engage, owned here C: members chr loop patched, enemies not owned here go to NetAI now`); until then every enemy keeps its own AI there (our sync is off, HP still follows the host's type 5). Disengage needs nothing new: IDA `0xA4DB20` calls slot 37 for every chr every update with `forceLocal = members <= 1`, so with the patches restored every enemy is back on its own AI on the next update. (b) The Ogre fight ran in the latched fallback (mode 4): the friend was ~340 m from the host, every enemy near the friend had update interval -1 on the host (`activation: ... off (-1/-2, not touched) 1161`), so the host's copies were not updated and the client's host-driven NetAI copies had nothing to play. Mode 4 now skips enemies with `chr+0x110 < 0` on the host (`netai::dormant_here`, one check in `enemies.rs` `host_netai`): the client's copy goes back to its own AI after the stale timeout. Log: `netai host: enemy E is not updated here (interval n): not host-driven, the friend's copy keeps its own AI` (first 20), `netai host: 10 s: N enemy checks skipped as not updated here`. (c) Every 2 s while engaged each side sends `Owned{generation, netIds owned here}` (handshake message 5); every 10 s each side logs `nsession: ownerless (10 s): N alive enemies owned by nobody, K of them within 40 m of a player: <entity> netId <n> at x,y,z <NetAI|own AI|...> interval <i>, d m from us, d m from the friend; ...` (`; K claimed by both machines` if any).
  - *NVIDIA crashes* (`nvwgf2umx.dll+0xfbbc10` at 04:13, 08:54, 11:47 (no co-op, 120 fps) and 13:41): same stack every time, `sekiro+0x30C9D2` -> `0x1B5C6F0` (calls `[ctx+0x180]` = `ID3D11DeviceContext::UpdateSubresource`) -> driver; two driver worker threads fault together, each reading `rdx + 0x170` where `rdx` is 0x80-0xE0 bytes before a 64 KB boundary (0x20d54ff80 -> 0x20d5500f0 etc.): the driver reads a game-heap source buffer past its mapped end. Not in our near arena or any buffer of ours, not on the Present / overlay path, and three of the four happened before the icons patch existed: nothing of ours is plausibly involved; not changed. To test: NVIDIA "Threaded optimization" off for sekiro.exe, or another driver version.
  - Live checks: host dies while engaged -> `waiting: our world update is paused ...` until the host is back, then `ENGAGED`, `23 distribution forced`, on the client `the host's sync runs ...` and `first 23 ... ms after engage` within ~1 s, no `No23` / `fallback latched`. Host deathblows an enemy next to the client: the client stays where it is (no teleport), the host's remote plays the deathblow; same the other way round; the client's `native: pin ...` lines show only small numbers. Friend far from the host in fallback: enemies near the friend fight normally (`not updated here` lines on the host). `ownerless` lines: K = 0 near players while both fight.

## 19. NPC quests carry over into the joiner's own world (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

Owner's request "make the quests sync". During a session both players already advance NPC quests together (world.rs mirrors every shared flag, group 7 included). New: when the session ends, the quest progress made in the shared world goes into the joiner's own world (§17 restore), **per quest unit, never per flag**, so an NPC is never left with an old stage flag still on, or a flag the quest script cleared still set. Code: `ownworld.rs` (`quest_unit`, `changed_quest_units`, `take_units`, `fold_quests`, `State::quest_baseline` / `note_quest_write`, `merge` takes the quest flags), one line in `world.rs` `apply_flag` (`ownworld::note_friend_flag`), `tuning.rs` `MERGE_QUESTS`, `settings.rs` `[GAMEPLAY] merge_quests`.

**How Sekiro stores NPC quest state (evidence).** The game files are encrypted (no unpacked EMEVD/ESD here), so the evidence is thefifthmatt's Sekiro randomizer data (`SoulsRandomizers/dists/Base/events.txt` = decompiled EMEVD with comments, `annotations.txt` = shop/item data; downloaded to my scratchpad, read only) plus our own logs:
- **NPC state machines, group 0 `1000-1999`, 20 flags per NPC** (record 1000, `k = 1`, shared). Every NPC-state flag in the EMEVD dump falls into aligned blocks of 20: Inosuke's mother `1120-1139` (event 11105700: `IF HP ratio 0 -> set 1139`, i.e. the block's last flag = the NPC is dead), Kotaro `1440-1459` (`GOTO IF 1459` = gone), Doujun `1460-1479`, the Exiled Memorial Mob `1580-1599` (`Batch Set Network-Connected Event Flags 1580-1594 = 0, set 1581 = 1`: one-hot states, `1595/1596` and `1599` the end states), and the used ids 1240-1259, 1260-1279, 1280-1299, 1340-1359, 1380-1399, 1400-1419, 1420-1439, 1500-1519, 1640-1659, 1680-1699, 1760-1779 [V for the named NPCs, I for the rest]. A killed NPC = its death flag in its own block, so "NPC killed in the session" is covered by the same unit.
- **NPC dialogue / quest flags, group 7 `7AABBkxxx`, 100 flags per block** (all of group 7 is shared, `world.rs should_sync`): Isshin `711005xx` (71100500 "moved to the castle", 71100509 "assassin dead, used by Isshin"), Emma `711100xx` (71110020-71110037), Kuro `711201xx` (71120102/107/110), Anayama's shop conditions `711002xx` (71100253/254/277). In our own logs the one-hot resets (batches of `= false`) stay inside one block of 100: 71100449-71100461, 71100125-71100155, 71100204-71100235, 71109030-71109039, 70009115-70009155, 71110483-71110499 [I: the per-NPC block of 100 is FromSoftware's talk-script convention as seen in these ids, not a rule found in code]. Group 7 `k = 1/2` records (`71101xxx`, `71102xxx`) are mostly shop flags (ShopLineupParam), which are per player and never part of a unit.
- Not quest units (stay as §17): group 0 outside 1000-1999 (global state, time of day, the Demon Bell, death penalty 9500-9599; story states 8301-8306 and Kuro's charm 6911 are local flags the snapshot never touches anyway), groups 1/2 (map flags; boss/door progress stays the §17 window heuristics), group 6. Boss defeat flags 93xx are local (k = 3), untouched.

**Merge rule.**
1. Quest baseline: once per join snapshot, 10 s after the join load (60 s after the snapshot if no load came), the non-zero quest records (record 1000 + group 7) are stored (`Saved.quest_base`). The join load's own flag resets are in it, so they are not session changes.
2. Touched: every write to a quest flag after the baseline, by our game more than 5 s after a load (our SetEventFlag hook ring; the first 5 s are the game's init, same rule as world.rs) or by the friend (`apply_flag`, also inside our load settle), adds the flag id to `Saved.touched`. Per-player flags (item/shop/skill set, 9500-9599) are ignored.
3. Fold (at the next join snapshot of a reconnect, and at the restore): for each unit with a touched flag, compare the unit now with the baseline over its non-per-player flags. If any flag differs (on or off), the whole unit is taken at its current value into `Saved.quest_taken` (a later fold replaces it). A touched unit that ended as it started is not taken.
4. Restore: backup -> progress flags ORed in -> every taken unit written whole (on and off) -> per-player flags at their current value. Units not taken keep the joiner's own backup state.

**Settings.** `[GAMEPLAY] merge_quests = 1` (default): units as above. `0`: off, NPC quests go back to the joiner's own state (the §17 behaviour). `2`: coarser, whole 1000-flag records (record 1000 = every NPC state machine; each group 7 record), for when a quest still breaks with 1 because one NPC's flags span more than one block (Kuro's gourd event sets 71110694 next to his 711201xx flags: such cross-block pairs can still mix with 1). File format version 2 (`own_world_slot<N>.bin`): + quest baseline, touched ids, taken flags; a version 1 file (an unfinished session from the previous build) still reads, with no quest data.

**Log lines.** `own world: quest baseline taken T s after the join snapshot (N quest records, B B, merge_quests = M)`, `own world: quest flag F written in the session (K touched)` (first 40), `own world: quest unit A-B changed in session -> taken (N flags differ from our own world, K on)`, `own world: join snapshot #K ..., U quest units taken (Q quest flags in total)`, at the restore `own world: quests (merge_quests = M): U units taken from the session (F flags), S touched units ended as they started, T flags touched; ...` and `RESTORED ... U quest units (F flags on) ...`.

**Live test (two PCs, joiner B, host A):**
1. B on a save where an NPC quest is further than on A's, or where an NPC is alive that A has killed. B joins: after ~10 s `own world: quest baseline taken ...`.
2. Together: talk to an NPC until its dialogue moves on (e.g. Kotaro, Doujun, Inosuke's mother, the Sculptor), give an item, or kill an NPC. Look for `quest flag F written` and, when it ends, `quest unit A-B changed in session -> taken`.
3. B uses Severed Thread: `quests (merge_quests = 1): U units taken ...`, `RESTORED ... U quest units`. In B's world: the NPC from step 2 is at the session's stage (or dead), its dialogue continues from there; NPCs nobody talked to are as they were in B's own world (the NPC that is further on B's save is still further). Items/sen given or received in the dialogue are B's own as before.
4. Repeat with `merge_quests = 0`: `quests (merge_quests = 0) ... off`, every NPC as before the session.
5. Crash test (§17 step 4) after step 2: the recovery restore takes the same units (`touched` is in the file).

**Risks.** The group 7 block size is a convention read from ids [I]: an NPC whose flags span two blocks (or another record, like 71110694) can still end mixed when only one block changed and the two worlds differed there; `merge_quests = 2` narrows that, `0` turns it off. Taking a unit means the host's version of that NPC wins whole, so a joiner who was further on that NPC's quest gets the shared-world stage (the session played that stage). Flag writes that a script makes when a map loads more than 5 s after the load (or periodic talk-script resets that change a value) mark a unit as changed with no real progress; it is then taken at the shared world's state, consistent but possibly older than the joiner's own. NPC placement / cutscene flags in groups 1/2 that a quest step also sets are not taken (unless a boss/ObjAct window caught them), so an NPC's position can lag its dialogue until the game's own scripts reconcile it [I]. Not verified live.

## 20. Posture and deathblow sync (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

The friend's hits never filled the host's posture bar: the client reported only HP. Now the client's posture damage and deathblows go with the HP total in the same cumulative report, the host applies them with the game's own functions, and the host's posture comes back. Code: `posture.rs` (new), `enemies.rs` (`host_damage`, the client HP block, the resend loop, `on_frame`, `reset`), `net.rs` (inboxes, `send_posture` / `take_posture`), coop-net `protocol.rs` / `session.rs`, `tuning.rs` `SYNC_POSTURE`, `settings.rs` `[GAMEPLAY] sync_posture = 1`.

**Game side [V, IDA on a private re7db copy, `F:\Temp\ida_post` o1-o7].**
- Posture is in the chr data module (`*(*(chr+0x1FF8)+0x18)`): current `+0x148`, max `+0x14C`, base max `+0x150` (scaling.rs). It counts DOWN. The hit path `0xB6E6A0` (a5 = 0) calls `0xBD4DE0(data, -hit[+484], 1)` at `0xB6EB43`; `0xBD4DE0` = `0xBD6710(data, cur + delta, smooth)`. `0xBD6710` clamps through `0xBD4E20` (NpcParam minimum `+632` .. max `+0x14C`), keeps the lagging bar `+0x258` (smooth 1 = falls gradually), refuses a decrease while `+0x228 & 0x10` or the debug "no posture damage" byte is set, and notifies `0xBD6200`. Recovery (`0xBD60F0`, `0xBD62D0`, `0xBD6AE0`, `0xBD6DC0`) also goes through `0xBD6710`, always upwards.
- The break is a hit reaction, not a per-frame check: `0xB6E6A0` sets the hit's break byte `+0x1C6` when the posture after the hit would be <= 0 (`0xBD4E20(data, -dmg) <= 0`, `0xB6EC48`), and `0xB6BD40` (the only reader) turns it into damage reaction 1027. Posture lowered out-of-band therefore sits at 0 without a stagger; the enemy's next hit on that machine breaks it and opens the deathblow as in vanilla.
- Lives: `data+0x25C`, initialised from NpcParam `+0x126` (deathblow count, scaling.rs `NPC_DEATHBLOWS`) by `0xBD6AE0`, reset by the revive `0x9F4360`, raised by `0xA02A10` (capped by `0xBD5830`) and by case 152 of `0xB51B70` (to 1). Only `0xB6E6A0` lowers it: hit type `hit+0x28 == 5` (deathblow) and lives > 0 -> `lives - 1` and the life SFX `0x9F0410(chr)` (NpcParam `+852` / `+856`), inline at `0xB6E7C5-0xB6E811`, BEFORE the HP damage. There is no standalone "consume a life" function.
- `0xBD5DF0` ("cannot die now") is true while lives > 0 (or flag `+0x228 & 1`, SpEffects 143 / 235). The HP setter `0xBD64E0(data, hp)` (clamp to max `+0x134`) then keeps HP at 1 instead of 0, and the hit path treats HP <= 1 with lives > 0 as a break (deathblow window).
- So the RE-BACKSTAB.md § c risk was real: the old host path wrote HP raw (`set_hp`, `+0x130 = 0`), past the "cannot die" floor, and never touched the lives.

**Wire (protocol VERSION 8 -> 9).** `Damage(DamageReport { id, hp, posture, deathblows: u16 })`, 14-byte body (was 8): all cumulative session totals; the host applies each field's increase only (resends and reordering change nothing). New `Posture(Vec<PostureState { id, posture, max, lives: i8 }>)`, kind 18, 13 B per entry, at most 64 per datagram (840 B). A version-8 peer's datagrams fail the version check in `decode` and are ignored (tests `version_8_damage_is_rejected`, `old_protocol_version_datagram_is_ignored`); the Steam lobby filter compares the version too, so old and new builds never pair.

**Client** (`Posture::client_step`, per mirrored enemy and frame, BEFORE the HP report so a deathblow and the HP it took leave in one report):
- a posture drop since last frame is our hit, a lives drop is our deathblow. Not counted: first sight / new chr pointer, a changed max (scaling clamp), an enemy not seen alive for 10 frames (`alive_before`), and our own mirror writes (the baseline is re-read after each write). The native remote's hits on the client copy are reaction-only (artfx.rs), so there is no other source;
- posture totals are kept in the host's units (`convert`; only matters if the two max values differ, normally they are equal since scaling.rs uses the host's percentages);
- the host's state is mirrored with `0xBD4DE0(data, host - local, 0)` when it differs by >= 5 % of max (min 5), never upwards while our own hit is younger than 2.5 s (still on its way), never while the enemy is broken here (posture <= 0: the local stagger / deathblow window runs). Lives are copied only downwards (the host's own deathblow);
- reports are re-sent for 3 s after a posture change, like HP.

**Host.** `host_damage` handles each report: posture and deathblows first (`Posture::host_apply`), then HP. New deathblows take `min(new, lives)` lives with the game's inline steps (`+0x25C` write, `0x9F0410`). If a life is left the enemy gets up for its next life: HP to max (`0xBD64E0`), posture to max, and this report's HP and posture loss is dropped (the deathblow damage is what the refill replaces); on the last life the same report's HP loss kills it. HP is now set through `0xBD64E0` (with `sync_posture = 1`), so a client hit cannot take a multi-life enemy below 1 HP. Posture deltas go through `0xBD4DE0(data, -d, 1)`. Ten times a second the host sends the posture of the synced enemies that are below full posture, short of their first-seen lives, or changed in the last 1.5 s. Modes 2/3/4 alike.

**Native session.** `posture::enabled()` = `sync_posture` and not `nsession::step_aside::enemy_sync_off()` (native session stage >= 2: the game's own sync owns enemies; enemies.rs already returns early then). At stage 1 our HP sync stays on, so posture sync stays on too.

**Log lines.** Host: `posture: friend hit <id> +<d> (now <p>/<max>)` (first 30, then every 50th), `posture: friend deathblow <id>: lives <a> -> <b>`, `posture: <id> gets up for its next life: hp <max>, posture full`, `posture: friend deathblow <id> with no life left here (lives <n>): HP decides`, every 10 s `posture: host 10 s: friend hits N (+sum), deathblows D, refills R, states sent S`. Client: `posture: own hit <id> +<d> (here <p>/<max>)`, `posture: own deathblow <id>: lives <a> -> <b> (total <n>)`, `posture: <id> lives <a> -> <b> (host)`, `posture: <id> max host <h> here <l> (values converted)`, `posture: <id> hp refilled here after our deathblow (<a> -> <b>/<max>)` (live check 3), every 10 s `posture: client 10 s: own hits N (+sum), deathblows D, host states S, mirror writes W, held H, lives copied L`.

**Live test (two PCs, A host, B client, both this build, `sync_posture = 1`).**
1. Regular enemy (an Ashina soldier): B hits it and gets it deflected/guarded a few times without killing. B: `own hit`; A: `friend hit <id> +d`, and A sees its posture bar fill from B's hits. When B's hits emptied it, A hits it once: it breaks on A (deathblow marker on A).
2. A fills the bar: B's bar follows within ~0.1 s (`mirror writes` grows); B's bar does not jump back up while B is mid-combo (`held`).
3. Miniboss with 2 deathblows (General Naomori Kawarada, the Chained Ogre): B lands the first deathblow. B: `own deathblow <id>: lives 2 -> 1`; A: `friend deathblow <id>: lives 2 -> 1` and `gets up for its next life`. One life orb gone and full HP on both screens. Note whether B logged `hp refilled here after our deathblow` (does the local game refill by itself?).
4. B lands the last deathblow: A `friend deathblow <id>: lives 1 -> 0` and the enemy dies on A at once (`enemies: friend hit <id> ...: hp X -> 0`), loot on both.
5. A lands a deathblow on a 2-life miniboss: B logs `posture: <id> lives 2 -> 1 (host)`.
6. `sync_posture = 0` on both: no `posture:` lines; HP sync as before.

**Risks.**
- The break from the friend's posture is not instant on the host: the enemy sits at 0 posture until its next hit there (the game breaks only in the hit reaction). On the client the break plays at once (local hit).
- The refill after a non-final deathblow is inferred from vanilla behaviour (the boss gets up with full HP and posture) [I]: the game's own refill mechanism (probably the deathblow-receive TAE / ESD on the defender) was not found. If the boss's script refills differently (partial HP, a phase cutscene), the host's value wins on both machines after 2.5 s.
- A posture drop on the client copy that is not our hit (an enemy's own posture cost, an environmental effect) is counted as ours and applied a second time on the host.
- Host and client recover posture independently between corrections; up to 5 % drift is left alone by design.
- Old host + new client (or the reverse) no longer connect (version 9).
- `0xBD4DE0`, `0xBD64E0` and `0x9F0410` are called from our FrameBegin task on the game thread, like `set_hp` / ForceDead; their characters come from `chr_by_entity_id` in the same frame.

## 21. Custom icons for the co-op items, patched in memory (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

**Why not a file.** The first attempt was a me3 package that replaced `menu/hi` and `menu/low/01_common.tpf.dcx` with repainted atlases (tools/icons/build_icons.py). Live, the game crashed right after me3 served the file: `0xc0000005 at sekiro.exe+0x71B2E9` (read of 0) on a loader worker thread, stack `+0x11AC4DE`, `+0x71B444`, `+0x71B801`. IDA: `0x71B2E9` is `cmp word [rdi], 0` in the wide-string constructor `0x71B250`, called from `0x71B3F0` (FD4ResName from a TPF texture name). That is called from `0x71B750`, the per-TPF texture loop. The name pointer comes from the TPF entry iterator (`0x1424C4300`). That iterator returns null when its checks fail: TPF header byte 12 must be 0 and byte 13 must be 3, the index must be below the count at +8, and the entry's data must start with `"DDS "` and a non-zero size. Offline, the file we shipped passes all of these. The TPF round-trips byte-identical, and the DCX header is the vanilla header with only the two size fields changed. So the buffer the loader iterated was probably not our decompressed TPF (a partial or asynchronous read through the override?). The cause is not determined. Owner decision: no file override (ERSC style).

**Hook point.** `0x71B750(a1, a2, i32 a3, a4, a5)`, the TPF texture-creation loop:
- The texture count is at `a1+104`. `a2` is an array of 80-byte entry handles: handle `+8` is the TPF base (read by `0x1202A0` / `0x1424C4F50`), and handle `+48` is the TPF entry iterator (vtable `0x1430DAED8`, 20-byte entries).
- For each texture, the loop builds the name (`0x71B3F0`) and a texture object, then queues `CSGXTextureCreateFragment` (`0xF70610`, vtable `0x2B5C220`) with the handle.
- The fragment's Run (`0xF706C0`) creates the GPU texture later from the TPF bytes. So the TPF buffer is still the upload source on entry to `0x71B750`.
- Prologue: 9 bytes, `40 55 53 56 57 41 54 41 55`.
- The crash above shows that this function processes `01_common.tpf`.

**Patch (icons.rs).** The detour runs before the original:
- It reads the TPF base. If the header is `TPF\0` / platform 0 / flag2 3 / UTF-16, it walks the entries.
- For a texture named `SB_Icon*` that has a record, it checks the width/height, TPF format 102, `DX10` with DXGI 98 (BC7_UNORM), mips 1, the DDS byte size (148 + w*h), and the FNV-1a 64 hash of the vanilla blocks under the cell.
- If everything matches and the pages are committed and writable, it copies the pre-encoded BC7 rows in. Then it calls the original.
- If the hash equals the new data, the cell is already patched and is left alone. Anything else is logged and skipped. Panics are caught.

The cells are `MENU_ItemIcon_00251` (Kindred Bell, host), `00552` (Kindred Charm, join) and `00962` (Severed Thread, leave):
- These ids exist in the hi and low layouts and in all 12 icon GFX files. No param row references them (tools/icons/find_unused_icons.py).
- New icon ids would need a DefineExternalImage2 entry plus a sprite frame (frame = id % 1000) in about 12 GFX movies.
- items.rs `HOST_ICON` / `JOIN_ICON` / `LEAVE_ICON` = 251 / 552 / 962. If the patch is off or did not apply, the items show those slots' vanilla art (a blade, a droplet, a scroll).

**Timing.** The menu textures load at boot, before the task system that `init` waits for. So `icons::install_early` runs on its own thread from DllMain:
- It reads `custom_icons` straight from the settings file (`settings::early_flag`).
- It polls every 1 ms, for up to 120 s, until the prologue bytes are present, then installs the detour.
- If the hook lands after `01_common.tpf` was processed, nothing is patched and no `icons: SB_Icon` line appears.

**Data.** `crates/coop-dll/assets/icons_bc7.bin` (240 KB, `include_bytes!`) holds 6 records: 3 icons × hi/low. Hi records are 63×63 blocks in `SB_Icon` / `SB_Icon_03` 4096×2048; low records are 32×32 blocks in the 2048×2048 versions. The format is documented in the generator. To regenerate after a design change:
`py -3.14 tools\icons\build_icon_blob.py [host.png join.png leave.png]`. The defaults are `tools\icons\design\gpt-bell.png`, `gpt-charm.png` and `gpt-thread.png`. Then run `build-dist.cmd`. Previews of exactly the encoded blocks go to `F:\Temp\icons\preview_blob`.

**Setting.** `[DISPLAY] custom_icons = 1` (tuning `CUSTOM_ICONS`, default on). It is read at process start, so a change needs a restart.

**Log lines.**
- `icons: hooked after N ms, 6 cells ready (patched so far 0)`.
- Per cell: `icons: SB_Icon 4096x2048 MENU_ItemIcon_00251 blocks (576,448) 63x63: Patched`. `AlreadyPatched` or `Mismatch("…")` appear in the same place.
- `icons: custom_icons = 0, menu textures stay vanilla`.
- `icons: texture-create function at … never matched …`.
- `hook: TPF texture create (0x71B750) … detoured`.

**Live test.**
1. Start the game and look in log.txt. You should see `icons: hooked after` with a small N, and then three `…: Patched` lines for the resolution in use, with no crash. The patching is a few hundred KB of memcpy, done once at load.
2. Rest at an idol to receive the items. Open the inventory, the quick-item slot and the item pickup popup, and check that the bell, charm and thread art shows.
3. Set `custom_icons = 0` and restart: the vanilla blade, droplet and scroll show.
4. Change the in-game resolution or the menu size if possible, to switch hi/low: the other three `Patched` lines appear.

**Risks.**
- The hook might be installed too late (no `Patched` lines). The fix would then be an earlier install, or a D3D11 `UpdateSubresource` on the created texture.
- The jump is written while loader threads may already run `0x71B750` (this window exists for every entry hook).
- `0x71B750` runs for every TPF in the game. The cost is one header read per TPF, plus a name read per entry, for TPFs with the right header.
- The me3 crash cause is unexplained, so it may have nothing to do with file contents.
- `dist\sekiro-coop\icons\` is no longer used. It was left in place (deleting it needs the owner's permission). The `[[packages]]` block was removed from `sekiro-coop.me3`, and `release.cmd` no longer copies `icons\`.

## 22. Resync, own-world mirror mode, restore safety, save backups, session doctor, flag cross-check (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

From an independent review. Code: `resync.rs`, `backups.rs`, `doctor.rs` (new); `ownworld.rs` (mirror mode, file version 3, restore safety, pre-restore copy, backups tick); `world.rs` (event dispatch line, two tick lines at the end of `on_frame`, `resync_rest`, `record_tuple`, `read_all_records`); `items.rs` (`on_use`: the Bell while connected); `join.rs` (`idol_of_block`); `settings.rs` (`own_world_mode`, F3 in the template comment); `lib.rs` (three `mod` lines). Protocol unchanged (VERSION 9): one new game event kind and one new bulk kind, both additive (an older build logs `world: unknown event 40` and leaves the bulk unread). IDA: copy `F:\Temp\ida_resync\db.i64`, outputs `o1`, `o2`.

**1. Resync (both players rest).** The Kindred Bell used while connected no longer says "Already in co-op": the first use shows "Use again to resync the world (both rest).", a second use 0.7-5 s later confirms (the item's 3 s cooldown is skipped for that one use; a use less than 0.7 s after the first is the same use and ignored). With `debug_hotkeys = 1`, F3 starts it at once. Not during a boss fight (our gauge, the friend's `EV_BOSS`, the death-screen wait, the friend down): refused with "Not during a boss fight.". 15 s cooldown. The initiator sends `EV_RESYNC` (event kind 40) `(1 = request, idol)`; both sides then wait for `game::safe_to_warp` (no menu, talk, death, load, recent warp; `WarpWait`, gives up after 20 s with "Resync failed: close the menu and try again.") and rest the way the mirrored rest does (world.rs `apply_rest`):
- if the initiator's idol or our last idol is in our map (`idol / 10000` equals that of our block's join idol; [I] entity ids `AABxxxx`): both mailboxes `REST_REQUEST 0x3AFEB98` + `0x3AFEBA0` (`World::resync_rest`): refill, AllChrRespawn + objects, the player is put on the idol. `rest_from_friend_at` is set, so the rest is not echoed as `EV_REST` and our death debuff is not cleared (like any mirrored rest);
- else `respawn_all` (`0xA4C0F0` + `0x899500`): enemies and objects reset in place, no refill, nobody moves.

Each side answers `(2 = done, 1 idol rest | 2 respawn only)` or `(3 = skipped, 1 boss fight | 2 no safe moment)`. Flags are not re-sent: a resync fixes enemies / objects / map state built from the flags both already have. Log: `resync: Kindred Bell used while connected, waiting for a second use to confirm`, `resync: confirmed with the Kindred Bell` / `resync: F3 (debug_hotkeys)`, `resync: requested by us (bell|hotkey), told the friend; our idol I, block B`, `resync: requested by the friend (their idol I)`, `resync: (us|friend) idol rest at I (ok = true), T s after the request` / `... enemies and objects reset in place (...)`, `resync: the friend rested (...)`, `resync: the friend skipped it (...)`, `resync: not confirmed within 5 s, cancelled`, `resync: ... refused, boss fight`.

**2. Own world: mirror mode.** `[GAMEPLAY] own_world_mode = auto` (default) | `mirror` | `merge`. At every join snapshot (`after_join_snapshot`) our own world (every flag on in the backup, plus the progress kept so far) is compared with the host's baseline, ignoring per-player flags (item / shop / skill set, 9500-9599) and the time of day 9800-9802 (shared, follows the story [I]; no other noise could be identified from the logs). Subset = every one of ours is on in the host's. At the restore, `auto` + subset, or `mirror`, = MIRROR: the shared world stays as it is in memory (the host's world plus everything the session changed; per-player flags were never synced), nothing is written, no reload, the file is deleted, notice "Back in your own world. It now matches the shared world."; `mirror` without a subset logs a WARNING with the count of our flags given up. Otherwise MERGE as in §17/§19. The result is stored in the file (version 3), so the crash recovery uses it. Log: `own world: own_world_mode = M: our world (N flags on, per-player and time-of-day ignored) is a subset of | has K flags not in the host's world (first [...]); at the end of the session: mirror ... | merge ...`; at the restore `own world: restore mode MIRROR|MERGE: setting own_world_mode = M, our world was (NOT) a subset of the host's at the last join snapshot (K flags ...)` and `own world: MIRRORED save slot S: ... nothing written`.

**3. Restore safety.** File version 3 adds the resolve tuple of each backup record (`world::record_tuple`: group, index of the block entry the resolver picked in the group's table = the id's (area, block) as the game maps it, k slot, byte size) and the subset result. Versions 1 and 2 still read; with no tuples the check is skipped (counted as "without a stored tuple"). Before the merge write, every record whose base id now resolves to another tuple (or not at all) is refused. It is logged (`own world: record B resolved to (..) at the backup, now Some(..): NOT restored, kept as it is`, first 20) and written back with its live bytes, because `write_records_raw` zeroes every shared record first. Before any restore the co-op save is copied (`backups::request("pre-restore")`; the restore waits up to 20 s for it, then goes ahead and says so). A dry-run line comes first: `own world: dry run (merge): W records (B B) to write after zeroing Z shared records, P progress flags, Q quest flags on, K per-player flags kept, S skipped, R refused by the resolve check, U without a stored tuple (T stored); save copied first`. At backup time: `own world: T of N backup records with their resolve tuple`.

**4. Rotating co-op save backups** (`backups.rs`).
- Save folder [V IDA o1/o2]: `0x141151C60` builds `SHGetFolderPathW(CSIDL_APPDATA = 26)` + `"%s/Sekiro/%s/"` (the second `%s` from `0x1411B2B30`, the Steam id folder; this PC has two such folders). We take the `S0000.coop.sl2` (me3 `savefile`) written last under `%APPDATA%\Sekiro\*\` [I].
- Copies: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\sekiro-coop\save-backups\S0000.coop.<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>-<reason>.sl2` (local time, `GetLocalTime`). Reasons: `session-start` / `session-end` (link connected / not connected edges, host or client, checked in ownworld.rs `on_frame` = every in-world frame), `periodic` (every 10 min while connected), `pre-restore`.
- One worker thread; the game thread only queues. The copy waits while the save's mtime is less than 2 s old (an autosave may be writing), copies to `.partial`, keeps it only if the save's size and mtime did not change meanwhile, then renames. A locked file or a changed save is retried every second for up to 60 s.
- After each copy the newest 10 are kept; older files that match exactly our name pattern in that folder are deleted (nothing else is touched).
- Log: `backup: session-start copy queued (#N)`, `backup: <save> -> <copy> (n B, t tries, ms); deleted the oldest [...]`, `backup: #N (reason): copy failed (...), retrying`, `backup: #N (reason) gave up ...`, `backup: ... no S0000.coop.sl2 under %APPDATA%\Sekiro`.
- Restore by hand: with the game closed, put the copy back as `S0000.coop.sl2` in the save folder (OHJE).

**5. Session-start doctor** (`doctor.rs`, log + notice only). 3 s into each session (both sides, in the world, flag manager ready) `BULK_DOCTOR` (bulk kind 30) carries `key=value` lines: `role`, `proto`, `ng_raw` / `ng` (GameDataMan `+0x70` = the clear count; `0x10B8D10` [V] reads `clamp(v, 0, 7)` and adds 100 when `0xA8E120()` is true, which SPEC-DEBUFF-SCALING §3.4 ties to flag 6911), `f6911`, `f9830` (Demon Bell), `slot` + `sen` (the own-world fingerprint, `ownworld::fingerprint`), `native_session`, `native_stage`, `sync_posture`, `sync_art`, `enemy_mode`, `merge_quests`, `own_world_mode`, `death_debuffs`, `scale` (four percentages). Warnings with an overlay notice (up to three, 12 s): different NG cycle ("Different NG cycle (you NG, companion NG+1): enemy HP will differ."), 6911 differs, protocol differs, `native_session` (or its stage while on) differs, `sync_posture` differs, `enemy_mode` differs. Log only: 9830 (the host's applies after the snapshot), `sync_art`, `death_debuffs`, scaling (the host's are used). A key the other build does not send is not a mismatch. Log: `doctor: sent ours: ...`, `doctor: friend's: ...`, `doctor: ours now: ...`, `doctor: no mismatch` | `doctor: WARNING ...`.

**6. Flag whitelist cross-check** (`doctor.rs`, log only, no behaviour change). `IsFlagNetSynced` = `0x6C4240`, `bool (EventFlagMan*, int id)` [V IDA o1: a pure function of the id; callers SetEventFlag's wrapper `0x6C4980` and `0x8D9ED0`]: top digit 0 -> k in {0,1,5,9} (bit mask 547), 1/2 -> k in {0,1,4,5,6,7}, 6 -> k == 0, others false. 10 s into each session it is compared with world.rs `should_sync` over every flag that is on in the loaded records of groups 0/1/2/6/7 (all k, `world::read_all_records`) plus every id our SetEventFlag hook ring saw (its last 128 writes before the session and every write since); 10 min later once more over the ids first seen meanwhile. Expected differences by construction: we sync group 7 (NPC dialogue), the game does not; the game syncs item / shop / skill flags, 9500-9599 and id 0, we do not. Anything else in the class summary (`g<top>k<k>`) is a real disagreement. Log: `flagcheck: session start (N on in the loaded records, H from the hook ring): C flags compared, same S (both shared A, both local B), ours only X {reason: n}, game only Y {reason: n}, t ms`, `flagcheck: we sync, the game would not (first 50): [...]`, `flagcheck: the game would sync, we do not (first 50): [...]`, `flagcheck: flags first written in the last 10 minutes: ...`.

**7. Doc drift.** posture.rs header now cites §20 (was § 18) and protocol 9; artfx.rs header cites §14 (was § 13); the header of this file and §11 / §14 now say the protocol is 9 since §20.

**Tests** (`cargo test -p sekiro_coop --lib`): backup names, foreign files, pruning order (`backups::tests`); doctor info round trip and mismatch rules, cross-check counting and classes, the on-bit reader (`doctor::tests`); file v3 round trip, v2 still reads, damaged subset byte, subset check (per-player / noise ignored, progress counts), mirror/merge choice and `own_world_mode` parsing, resolve filter (`ownworld::tests`); the settings default-file test covers `own_world_mode`.

**Live test (two PCs, same build).**
1. Connect: both logs show `backup: session-start copy queued` and a `backup: ... -> ...save-backups\S0000.coop.<stamp>-session-start.sl2` line; `doctor: sent ours`, `doctor: friend's`, `doctor: no mismatch` (or the expected WARNING: put one side on NG+ to see the NG notice); after 10 s `flagcheck: session start ...` with ours-only only `group 7 (NPC)` and game-only only `item/shop/skill`, `death 9500-9599`, `id 0`.
2. Resync: B uses the Bell once (notice), again within 5 s: both log `resync: ... idol rest at ...` (or `reset in place`) and rest; A logs `resync: the friend rested`. With A's inventory open, A's rest waits (`warp: waiting (menu)`) and runs when it closes. During a boss fight: refused. F3 with `debug_hotkeys = 1`.
3. Mirror: B on a save that is behind A's (nothing A lacks) joins: `own world: ... is a subset of the host's world; ... mirror`. B leaves: `restore mode MIRROR`, no reload, B's world = the shared world, items kept. Repeat with B ahead of A somewhere (a boss A has not killed): `has K flags not in`, merge at the end as before. `own_world_mode = merge` forces the old behaviour.
4. Restore safety: on a merge restore, `own world: restore due; copying the co-op save first (backup #N)`, a `pre-restore` copy line, the `dry run (merge)` line with `0 refused`, then `RESTORED`.
5. 10 min in co-op: a `periodic` copy; after more than 10 copies only 10 files stay in the folder.

**Risks.**
- The resync rest at an idol the player is far from moves the player onto that idol (as the mirrored rest already did); the friend's idol may be one we have not unlocked (as with the mirrored rest).
- The map-code test for "our idol is loaded here" is inferred from the join table's ids [I]; blocks missing from that table always use the in-place respawn.
- Mirror mode trusts that the session's memory is the shared world. Time of day is ignored by design; any other flag that differs only cosmetically makes `auto` pick merge (safe direction).
- Picking the newest `S0000.coop.sl2` under `%APPDATA%\Sekiro` is a heuristic (two Steam id folders exist on this PC).
- The mtime "quiet" rule cannot see an autosave that starts mid-copy without changing mtime until it ends; the size/mtime re-check catches the usual case.
- `IsFlagNetSynced` is called from our frame task with the flag manager pointer (it does not read it). The doctor and the flag check run on the game thread once per session (the record walk is the same ~4500 resolver calls as the join snapshot).

## 23. Script item grants and boss drops reach the friend, key-item check (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

Review blocker: flags are shared, inventories are not. A talk / event gift (Healing Gourd, Mortal Blade, esoteric texts, Young Lord's Bell Charm...) is given once, in the world of the player who triggered it; the other player's NPC then has the shared "already given" flags: soft-lock. Live 2026-10-23 (host Tihvert, client HostPlayer): HostPlayer got Young Lord's Bell Charm from a talk, the host never could; HostPlayer killed General Naomori (entity 1100300) and got the Prayer Bead, the host got nothing. Code: `keyitems.rs` (new), `bossrules.rs` (`award_detour`, `install`, `on_frame`, gauge-off), `items.rs` (`goods_name`, `is_coop_goods`), `settings.rs` (`share_key_items`), `lib.rs` (mod line). Protocol unchanged (VERSION 9): three new bulk kinds 41 / 42 / 43 (30 is doctor.rs); an older build leaves them unread.

**IDA** (copy `F:\Temp\ida_grant\db.i64` of `ida_post`, outputs `o1`..`o10`):
- `0x918810 AwardItemLot(MapItemMan*, lot, bool)` callers are script code only: `0x689FF0` (EMEVD dispatcher `0x904EF0` case 4 = 2003[4]; ESD talk command 104 in `0x11C63A0`; Lua binding set at `0x8FBB0`), `0x68A010` (EMEVD case 36), `0x68A030` and `0x688360` (lot of an event chr; `0x688360` also gives boss experience, from `0x6A0980`), `0x68D240` (one fixed lot). It runs the lottery (`0x913A70` / `0x913DB0`), calls GrantItems once per result (`0x918A69`, entries `{fullId, num, -1, 2}`, last argument 1) and sets the lot's get flag (`0x6AAAB0`). So talk gifts are AwardItemLot too; ESD never calls GrantItems directly.
- `0x91C970 GrantItems(MapItemMan*, ItemBuffer*, void*, char silent, u32*, bool)`: the third argument is never read (null is safe). Other callers: `0x918550` (OnGoodsUsed `use_get_item`; lot variant `0x918690` from `0x7C0EF0` and `0x918480`), `0x91C6E0` (MapItemMan frame update `0x91A440`), `0x91D620` / `0x91FCF0` / `0x91FF30` (pickups: `0x668CC0` action handler, `0x920D20`, `0x923190`). Shops and world pickups never reach AwardItemLot. Prologue `48 8B C4 44 88 48 20` (7 bytes, no RIP-relative code).
- `0x84E7E0` (the dialog decision) only READS EquipParamGoods `first_get_event_flag_id` (row `+0x7C`); nothing in the grant path sets it.
- `0x9FFF50 Drops(chr)` (prologue `48 8B C4 57 48 81 EC A0 00 00 00`): vt[47] (`+0x178`) = NpcParam ref (`+8` row); `+0x1A12 & 1` (dropped) / `& 0x10` and `0x9F2DE0` stop it; resource lots NpcParam `+720` / `+772` via `0x918D40(MapItemMan, lot, pos, 1, ..)` (spawned at the corpse, `0x91E100` / `0x91DBA0`); then `if (!0xB5E3A0(*(*(chr+0x1FF8)+0xC8)))` (true = `byte +21 == 0 && dword +108 != -1`) `0x918480(MapItemMan, entity, ref)` -> `0x918090(.., item_lot_id_1 +44, drop_type +284, item_search_radius +340, 0)` = the item lot at the corpse; then (flag 9500 off) `0x918690(life_count_item_lot_id +836 + 10 n)`; finally `+0x1A11 |= 0x10`, `+0x1A12 |= 1`. Randomizer data (thefifthmatt `annotations.txt`): Naomori's Prayer Bead = lot 10200000, Gourd Seed = 10200001, both "Dropped by" c1020_0000 #1100300, i.e. NpcParam item lots, not AwardItemLot. Young Lord's Bell Charm = lots 51600 / 61600, flag 50006160 (Inosuke's Mother).
- Cause of the Prayer Bead miss [I, strong]: on the host our loot step called `Drops` for the friend's kill (`loot: 1100300 died without our kill, gave drops + sen+xp (bits 0x80)`), but the kill record named another killer (`+108 != -1`, presumably the friend's native remote), so `0xB5E3A0` skipped the item lot; only the resource lot dropped. The host stood at the corpse for about 60 s (T lines) and found nothing.

**1. Script grants.** `award_detour` (every call, not only the boss window): `keyitems::award_begin`, original, `award_end(lot, flags switched on meanwhile, in boss window)`; the GrantItems detour copies the nested call's entries. Frame task: boss-window lots stay with the boss reward (§10) while `boss_reward = 1`; otherwise the rule below; the kept entries go out as `BULK_ITEM_GRANT` (41) `{u8 1, u32 event id, i32 lot, u8 complete, n x {fullId, num, -1, popup}, m x per-player lot flag}`.
- Rule (`classify` / `shareable`): weapons (prosthetic tools; category nibble 0), protectors (1), accessories (2), and goods (4) that are not consumed on use (`is_consume = 0`: key items, memories, esoteric texts, Prayer Beads, Gourd Seeds, quest / upgrade materials) always; consumables only from a one-time lot (the award switched on a per-player item-lot flag, the `world::per_player_flags` set); never our co-op items or unknown goods rows. `goods_type` is logged per item, not used (its Sekiro values are not verified).
- Receiver, 5 s later when `safe_for_reward`: skipped if all of the lot's flags are on here, or our own AwardItemLot of the same lot ran within 120 s (both worlds ran the script); unique items already owned are left out (`FindItem 0x79D7E0`; unique = not goods, or `max_num <= 1`); the rest through GrantItems with the sender's entries (same popup), then the lot flags are set here (per player, world.rs never sends them). A lot given complete is remembered for 120 s: our own AwardItemLot of it then does not run (`skip_local_award`, returns 0). No loop: our GrantItems call is not inside an AwardItemLot, so nothing is captured again.
- Gauge-off fix (bossrules.rs): a gauge that goes away with its boss loaded at 0 HP and no HandleBossDefeat (minibosses) now sends `EV_BOSS(2)` (was 3), so the friend's spectate wait, ownworld's defeat window and the boss start gate (§24) treat it as a kill.

**2. Boss / miniboss drops** (`Drops` detour). For our own calls (caller outside the game image = the enemies.rs loot step for the friend's kills, `share_loot = 1`) the item lot is dropped at the corpse with `0x918480` when `0xB5E3A0` was the only reason it was skipped: the owner's "native drop at the corpse". Every drop of an NpcParam with deathblows >= 2 is logged; if its item lot dropped here, `BULK_BOSS_DROP` (43) `{u8 1, i32 entity, i32 lot}` tells the friend. The friend waits 15 s: if its own drop of that entity lay at the corpse, nothing (pick it up there); else the lot's sure goods (rows `lot`, `lot+1`.. while they exist; a row only when one slot carries all of its weight; goods by category 3 / 4 / `0x4000_0000` or a named goods row) whose lot flag is off are given with the normal small pickup log (popup 0), and the flags are set.

**3. Key-item check** (on every connection; log + notice). After 10 s in the world and 5 s connected each side sends `BULK_KEY_ITEMS` (42) `{u8 1, u8 is_host, u16 n, n x u32}`: owned named goods, not consumed on use, `max_num <= 1`. Differences are logged and shown ("You are missing: Mortal Blade (HostPlayer has it) / HostPlayer is missing: ..."). Only repair: an item the friend owns and we lack, whose `first_get_event_flag_id` is a shared flag that is on in our world, and that no item lot of ours ever gave us (all its lot flags off): given once (dialog popup) and its first lot flag set. Quest flags are not compared (the join snapshot and doctor.rs cover the world).

**Settings.** `[GAMEPLAY] share_key_items = 1` (`keyitems::ENABLED`; 0 = no script grants sent or applied, no key-item check; the boss-drop fix stays). Owner decision 2026-10-23: no generic item-give path in the mod (no grant file, no spawner); the owner's past losses are fixed by editing the save.

**Log lines.** `reward: GrantItems hook installed ...`, `hook: enemy Drops (0x9FFF50) ... detoured`, `reward: sent our script lot L to the friend (event E, ok = true): [...], lot flags [...]`, `reward: script lot L [...] stays ours (repeatable lot: ...)`, `reward: lot L [...] is a boss reward (boss rules send it)`, `reward: friend's script lot L arrived ...`, `reward: gave the friend's script lot L (event E): [goods 2470 'Mortal Blade' x1 (type .., max 1)], lot flags set [...]`, `reward: friend's lot L already received here (lot flags [...] on)` / `... already given here by our own script N s ago` / `... gave nothing new here: [... (already owned)]`, `reward: our loot-step drop of E (NpcParam item lot L, deathblows D): item lot dropped by us at the corpse (the game skipped it: another killer) (kill record +21 = .., +108 = ..)`, `reward: told the friend about the drop of E`, `reward: the friend's drop of E (lot L): our own drop lies at the corpse here (pick it up)`, `reward: gave the friend's boss drop of E (lot L, no drop of it here) with the pickup log`, every 60 s `reward stats (script lots): awards seen .., sent .., kept here .., received .., given .., local doubles skipped ..; item lots we dropped for the friend's kills N`, `key items: N key items indexed ...`, `key items: sent our N key items`, `key items: we own N key items, the friend M; we miss [...]; the friend misses [...]`, `key items: gave X once ...` / `key items: not giving X: acquisition flag F ...`.

**Live test (two PCs, this build on both).**
1. A talks to an NPC who gives a key item (Inosuke's Mother: Young Lord's Bell Charm; Emma: Healing Gourd; the Sculptor: a prosthetic tool). A: `reward: sent our script lot ...`; B, 5 s later and out of menus: the item popup and `reward: gave the friend's script lot ...`. B talks to the same NPC: no second copy.
2. B kills a miniboss (Naomori / Tenzen) near A: A logs `reward: our loot-step drop of 1100300 ... dropped by us at the corpse` and finds the Prayer Bead / Gourd Seed at the corpse. Repeat with A far away (other block): A gets them with the pickup log 15 s later (`gave the friend's boss drop`).
3. A consumable from a repeatable talk (Rice from the Divine Child): `stays ours`. A one-time consumable gift: shared.
4. Connect with one side lacking a key item the other has: `key items: we miss [...]` and the notice on both screens.
5. `share_key_items = 0` on one side: its grants are not sent, the friend's are dropped there.

**Risks.** The "only reason" test for the forced item drop is our reading of `0xB5E3A0` [I]; if `+108` means something else, the friend's kills now drop their item lot for us in cases where the game would not. The forced drop applies to every enemy the friend kills (the purpose of `share_loot`), so drops per shared kill rise to vanilla's. Consumable classification by `is_consume` and the one-time test by per-player lot flags are inferred [I]. A lot without get flags gives stackable non-consumables (Prayer Beads) again on a replay if both worlds ran it more than 120 s apart. Weapons are not named in the log (no weapon-name getter is used). The key-item repair depends on `first_get_event_flag_id`, which nothing in the grant path sets: if Sekiro's scripts never set it, the check only reports. The detours on AwardItemLot / GrantItems / Drops run on the game thread; GrantItems and Drops wait for their trampoline in the install window like items.rs.

## 24. Boss start gate (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

Review blocker: Sekiro story bosses start through cutscenes and talk scripts that set map / talk flags. Mirrored to a player who is far away, they could put that world into "fight running" with no fog gate or cutscene: a soft-lock on arrival. A boss can still be started alone; only the mirroring waits. Code: `bossgate.rs` (new); `world.rs` (two call lines in the EV_FLAG / EV_BOSS branches, `apply_friend_flag`, `write_quiet`); `bossrules.rs` (`tick` call, recorder end, gauge-off `EV_BOSS(2)` for minibosses, see §23); `settings.rs` (`boss_start_gate`). Protocol unchanged.

**Which flags are gated, and how they are identified.** Gated kinds: map flags (group 1 / 2, `1AABBkxxx`), talk flags (group 7) and the NPC state machines 1000-1999 (§19); global flags, death flags and items never. "Away" = the friend is not in our map block, or more than 60 m from us (`NEAR_M`). The start flags are not known in advance (game files encrypted), so they are learned:
- while away and no fight runs, every gated friend flag waits 4 s; a flag already learned as a start flag waits 90 s (cutscenes);
- the friend's `EV_BOSS(1)` turns every waiting flag into the fight's held flags, and gated flags we applied from the wait in the 15 s before it (the first fight against a boss, nothing learned yet) are switched back here (`World::write_quiet`: `known` is updated first, so the friend is not told) and held too; all of them are learned for that boss entity;
- our own fights: flags our world switched on from 10 s before to 3 s after our gauge appeared (the bossrules.rs recorder, `boss-flags:`) are learned;
- learned flags persist in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\sekiro-coop\boss_start_flags.txt` (`entity: flag flag ...`, max 64 per boss, 256 bosses; can be edited by hand).

**During the friend's fight** every gated friend flag is held while we are away. Held flags are applied when we come within 60 m in the same block while it runs (notice "Joining your friend's boss fight"), or with the kill (`EV_BOSS(2)`, now also minibosses; inside ownworld.rs's boss window). The fight ending any other way (`EV_BOSS(3)`, the friend died / rested / loaded = `World::friend_boss` cleared, link down) drops them: the friend's own world resets them on its reload, and those resets are never sent (world.rs ignores writes in the first 5 s after a load). Link down / new session: waiting (not held) flags are applied at once. `boss_start_gate = 0`: everything waiting is applied, nothing is gated.
