**Settings.** `[GAMEPLAY] boss_start_gate = 1` (`bossgate::ENABLED`, live).

**Log lines.** `bossgate: N learned start flags of B bosses (path)`, `bossgate: friend's boss fight E started while we are away: N waiting flags held [...] (K newly learned)`, `bossgate: boss E: N friend flags of the last 15 s switched back here and held [...]`, `bossgate: boss E: we came within 60 m of the friend's fight: N held flags applied`, `bossgate: the friend's boss fell: N held flags applied with the kill`, `bossgate: boss E: <why>: N held start flags dropped`, `bossgate: learned N start flags of our own boss E`, `bossgate: saved ...`, `bossgate: link changed: N waiting friend flags applied now`, `boss: the friend's fight is gated here: its start flags wait until we are near`, every 60 s `bossgate: waited .. friend flags, held .. in fights, applied late .., switched back .., dropped ..; learned ..`.

**Live test.**
1. A far from B (another area). B starts Lady Butterfly / Genichiro (cutscene). A: `friend's boss fight ... started while we are away`, the notice; A travels to the arena: `we came within 60 m ... applied`, and the fight state there matches.
2. B dies in the fight: A logs `... dropped`; when A reaches the arena later: fog gate / cutscene as for a fresh fight.
3. B kills it: A logs `the friend's boss fell ... applied with the kill`; A's world has the boss dead (and the reward lots via §10 / §23).
4. Second attempt at the same boss: `boss_start_flags.txt` lists it, and the 90 s hold applies to those ids.
5. A next to B at the start: no `bossgate:` hold lines, everything as before.
6. `boss_start_gate = 0`: no holds.

**Risks.** All [I]: the gated kinds include ordinary door / talk flags, which now reach a far player 4 s late (90 s for learned ones), and are dropped if they fall inside a friend's fight that ends without a kill; a talk-started boss whose flags lie outside groups 1 / 2 / 7 / 1000-1999 is not gated; switching a flag back can itself trigger a script in our world (only flags applied in the 15 s before the start, and only while still at the friend's value); a host mid-fight when the client joins still sends its fight state in the join snapshot (§2); learned noise flags only delay. Whether a player away from the arena sees any effect of the friend's start flags at all was never measured: the gate is a precaution.

## 25. Own-world restore: written during the reload's loading screen, idol activations whole (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

**Live bug (friend = joiner, v6.1 build, log `794b39j.txt` lines ~19850-20079).** The host closed its world -> `own world: RESTORED save slot 3: 16 records ... 2 progress flags merged {"idol": 2} [11100002, 11100006]` -> `reloading at our last idol 1101952` -> `warp: to idol 1101952`. Afterwards the friend could sit at an idol but was thrown out at once (no Rest/Travel menu, no `items: rest at idol`).

**What the log shows.**
- *The warp did load.* No `native: area load` / `our warp` line is expected: those only print when a native remote exists, and it had been deleted at the disconnect (`native: deleted 0x4837a880`). The evidence for the load is `debuff: applied sp 2140000001` 2 s after the warp. speffect.rs only re-applies the row when it is missing from the player, which happens when the player character is re-created by a load (the same line followed the death load at `...082`), plus an fps dip in that tick. The timing matches the join warps of the same session (`warp` at `...077`, new position at `...079`). The warp code (`0x68E870` -> GameMan `+0x14/+0x1C/+0xB50`, `0x6D7070` = GameMan `+0x10 = 1`, IDA) is the same as for the join warp. So no stuck load request is involved here.
- *The restored idol was half activated.* The friend activated idol 1101956 in the session. The host's log of the friend's flags (`friend set flag` #10-#15, 1792746130-136) shows `11100120` on, `9110` off, `11100006` on, `11100016` off, `236` on and `232` on within 3 s. The restore merged only the unlock flag `11100006`; the others went back to their pre-session values. Idol 1101952 (`11100002`) was activated the same way at 1792746886 (the friend walked to 197.3,-48.0,152.0, which is the idol, and the fold logged its second progress flag at `...887`). It is also the reload target: the friend's last idol came from the join rest there, so the friend reloaded at an idol whose unlock flag was on while its other activation flags were not. That matches "sit, then thrown out". [I] The idol ESD/EMEVD is packed game data and could not be read, so this inconsistency is the cause shown by the logs, not traced in the script.
- *Not the cause:* the v1 death-debuff SpEffect row (still in this build: `speffect: appended 5 rows`) was on the player since the death at `...082`. But the host rested at an idol (`items: rest at idol 1101950`, 1792746046) with its own v1 row on (stacks 2), so the row does not block idols.
- *Mirror mode (§22)* would have written nothing and kept the shared world's consistent idol state, if `auto` had found a subset. Whether it would have cannot be told from this log: v6.1 has no subset check and logs no flag contents. Backup and host baseline were both 15 non-zero records (1920 B), so the two saves were at the same early stage, but equal sizes do not prove a subset.

**Fix (`ownworld.rs`, `puppet.rs` one line, `items.rs` `title_count`).**
1. *Write only where a real load follows.* A due merge restore is now **armed** with its reload warp and written on the first loading-screen frame of that load whose flag manager is ready (`ownworld::on_loading`, called from puppet's no-player branch), the same idea as the join snapshot being applied before the map loads. Nothing is written in the world. The write is skipped on a loading frame that is not our reload: another save slot, or `items::title_count` changed (title trip / save load: the save's own flags load then, and the file stays).
   - No loading screen within 25 s of the warp: the arm is cancelled (`showed no loading screen within 25 s`, with GameMan+0x10 logged; the game's load request is left alone) and the warp gate tries again. The player is still in the shared world, so nothing is inconsistent.
   - A load passed without a ready flag manager: the same, counted. After 2 such reloads the fallback writes right before the warp (the v6.1 order), keeps the live records, and **rolls them back** if no load shows within 25 s. So the player is never left in an un-reloaded restored world.
   - Connected again before the reload: the arm is cancelled (and a fallback write rolled back).
2. *Idol activations whole* (`Category::IdolActivation`). When an idol's unlock flag switches on in the session (in the world, off in both the host's baseline and our backup, once per idol), every shared flag of groups 0/1/2 (not per-player, not 9800-9802, not 9500-9599) that changed **on or off** from 3 s before to 5 s after is classified. At the restore these flags take their session value (applied after the progress, like the per-player flags), so the idol is left exactly as its activation left it.
3. *Reload target.* `pick_reload_idol`: our last idol if it is unlocked in the restored world, else an unlocked idol in the same map (`entity / 10000`), else any unlocked one. Live, the last idol came from the join rest at an idol our own world did not have.

Mirror restores (nothing written) still finish at once in the world, without a reload.

**Log lines.**
- `own world: restore armed: reloading at idol I (our last idol | an unlocked idol in the same map ...); our own world is written during its loading screen`
- `own world: writing our own world during the reload's loading screen (T s after the warp to idol I)`, then the dry-run / RESTORED lines as before
- `own world: loading screen of the reload, flag manager not ready yet`
- `own world: the reload at idol I ended with nothing written (...; try N)`
- `own world: our reload warp to idol I showed no loading screen within 25 s (GameMan+0x10 load request = ...)`
- `own world: N reloads gave no loading frame with a ready flag manager: writing ... right before the warp (fallback ...)`, `fallback write rolled back ...`, `restore confirmed`
- `own world: a title trip / save load instead of our reload ...`
- `own world: connected again ..., restore cancelled`
- `own world: idol flag F switched on in the session: the flags changed around it are its activation`
- `own world: flag F is session progress (IdolActivation)`
- `own world: N idol activation flags take their session value: [(id, on), ...]`

**Live test.**
1. Joiner B, `own_world_mode = merge`. Join, then activate an idol B never had. Expect `idol flag ... switched on` and several `(IdolActivation)` lines.
2. Host closes its world. Expect `restore armed ...` and `writing our own world during the reload's loading screen`, with **no** RESTORED line before the loading screen. After the load: sit at the activated idol and at the reload idol; Rest/Travel must open (`items: rest at idol`).
3. Open the inventory right as the host leaves: the warp waits for the gate (`warp: waiting (menu)`), and nothing is written until the load.
4. If `flag manager not ready yet` repeats and `ended with nothing written` appears twice, the fallback runs: expect `restore confirmed` after its load, and a rollback line if no load comes.

**Risks.** The first loading frame is assumed to come before the new map's EMEVD reads the flags [I]. If the flag manager is never ready during loads, every restore takes two extra reloads before the fallback. The idol window can take an unrelated flag that changed in the same 8 s at its session value. Rollback restores the shared world as it was at the write, so up to 25 s of flag changes in between are lost.

## 26. Native yes/no box for the co-op items, reload on leave, native notices (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

Owner requests (like Elden Ring Seamless Co-op): the three items ask first in the game's own message box, leaving a companion's world always goes through a loading screen, the host hears when a companion arrives, and important co-op notices use the game's UI. Code: `dialog.rs` (new), `leave.rs` (new); `items.rs` (`on_use` split into `question` / `act`, answer poll in `tick`, `dialog::tick` at the top of `on_frame`); `resync.rs` (`request_confirmed`, the start notice also for it); `ownworld.rs` (`restore_pending`, boss-fight wait before the restore warp, notices through `dialog::game_notice`); `net.rs` (host-closed notice through `game_notice`); `puppet.rs` (one call after `ownworld::on_frame`); `settings.rs` (3 keys); `lib.rs` (2 `mod` lines, `dialog::install`). Protocol unchanged.

**Dialog API (IDA, copy `F:\Temp\ida_dlg\db.i64`, job outputs `o1`-`o16`, addresses with image base 0x140000000).**
- Lua script functions `ShowGenDialog` / `CloseGenDialog` are registered at `0x140092150` / `0x1400921C0` (`SprjLuaEventProxy`, `(int, int, int, bool)` / `()`). `ShowGenDialog` = `0x14068DF90`: `if CanOpen() Open(msg, buttons, type)`. `CloseGenDialog` = `0x1408C4AC0`.
- `0x1408B6380 bool CanOpen()`: MenuMan (`[0x143D67408]`) menu states 17, 180, 182 (`MenuMan + 0x20 + 4*id`) all 0 and byte `0x143D67250` 0. `0x1408C58E0` (our `game::menu_mask`) sets bit 8 when it is false.
- `0x1408C4B40 Open(u32 msg, i32 buttons, u32 type)`: buttons 0 -> FDP_Dialogues ids 3/4, 1 -> 1/2, else none; type 1 -> 7, 2 -> 8, 6 -> 9. Writes GenDlgCtrl (`MenuMan + 0x1FB0`, `NS_SPRJ::GenDlgCtrl`): `+0` msg, `+4/+8` button ids, `+0x10` = 1 (text source), `+0x18` result = -1, `+0x1C` = 0, menu state 136 = 0, MenuMan `+0x158` = -1; then `0x1408B70C0` builds the dialog job (`0x140DEA010`) on the menu system `[0x143D67410]`. `0x140DEA010`: types 1/4/7 = one button, 2/3/5/8 = two. The game itself calls `Open(10030010, 0, 2)` for a yes/no at `0x14075B9F5`.
- Text: GenDlgCtrl `+0x10` set -> `0x140DE7B10` uses `0x140C791F0` = "Event Text" through `0x1410A9E40 (MsgRepository*, u32 id) -> wchar_t*` (banks 101, 30, 231, 271, fallback "?ActionEventInfo?"; prologue `48 89 5C 24 08 57 48 83 EC 20`, the same as GoodsName). Button labels: `0x140C7A1C0` (FDP_Dialogues). Our detour returns our text for ids `1999990000..1999990007` (8 slots, leaked UTF-16 buffers); every other id goes to the original.
- Result: GenDlgCtrl `+0x18` = pressed button index + 1 (`0x140DE8100` -> `0x1408B6B90`), 0 when backed out (`0x140DEC990`, `0x140DF0620`), -1 while open. The dialog constructor `0x140DE53B0` sets state 17 to 1 or 11, so `CanOpen` is false while it is up. After the answer we call `0x1408C4AC0` (state 17 back to 0), as the Lua scripts do.
- [I] Button ids 3/4 = "Yes"/"No" and 1 = "OK" (DS3 ESD convention, not read from the FMG). `Open` is called from our FrameBegin task (game thread), not from a Lua event step.
- Not found in this pass: a non-blocking native popup with free text. The area-name banner (`0x140D87FF0`, PlaceName via `0x140C7BC00`) and the network HUD messages (`0x140E15E80`, `0x140E131F0`, ... -> menu system `+819984`) take fixed ids from the network-message dispatcher `0x140909870`.

**Default button (v8.27b, `[GAMEPLAY] dialog_default_yes = 1`, NOT verified live).** `Open` (`0x1408C4B40`) ignores its 4th arg; for yes/no it sets MenuMan menu state 84 (`0x54`, via `0x1408B6680` -> `0x1408B71F0` -> `0x1408C68B0`) = 1, and state 84 is the initially highlighted button (0 = Yes, 1 = No) [I, strong]. A write before `Open` is overwritten, so `dialog.rs open_default_yes` replays Open's steps: `0x8B7200(ctrl, msg, 3, 4)`, `0x8B71E0(ctrl, 0)` + `[ctrl+0x14] = 0`, `0x8B71E0(ctrl, 1)`, `0x8C68B0(84, 0)`, `0x8B71C0(ctrl)`, `0x8B70C0(ctrl, 8)`, `[MenuMan+0x158] = -1`. Log: `dialog: opened (text id I, default=Yes, state84 read back N)`. Test: Kindred Bell, Yes highlighted, confirm at once logs `answered Yes`. If the highlight is still No or the box misbehaves, set `dialog_default_yes = 0` (the old `Open` call).

**`dialog.rs`.** `ask(text) -> ticket`, `answer(ticket) -> Yes | No | Unavailable(why)`, `game_notice(text, Info | Important, secs)`. One dialog at a time, a queue, 0.6 s between two. Opens only when: EventText hooked, 3 s in the world, no load request (GameMan `+0x10`), `game::safe_for_reward` (alive, real world, menu mask 0), talk idle, `CanOpen`; a notice also not during a boss fight or the spectate wait. A question that finds no such moment in 10 s, or a dialog that is not up (state 17) within 1.5 s, answers `Unavailable`; a notice goes to the overlay after 8 s. Closed without a result (a load), backed out, or 5 minutes unanswered = No.

**Items (`[GAMEPLAY] confirm_items = 1`).** A use that would act asks: Bell "Open your world to a companion?", Charm "Seek a companion's world and join it?", Thread "Sever the bond and leave the shared world?" (host / hosting / host intent: "Close your world to your companion?"), Bell while connected "Resync the world? Both of you will rest." (replaces the use-again-within-5-s confirmation, which stays as the fallback). Uses that are refused anyway (no password, already open / searching / in co-op, not in co-op) give their notice at once, no dialog. Yes: the old action, with the state read again. No: nothing. Unavailable: the old action (log line). A use while a question is open is ignored; a title trip forgets the question. 0 = the old behaviour.

**Leaving (`[GAMEPLAY] reload_on_leave = 1`, `leave.rs`).** A joiner that stops being a connected client (Thread, host closed, link lost) waits the own-world grace (2 s left / 10 s no session / 45 s lost) and 5 s in the world, then the own-world restore (`restore_pending`: a merge reloads by itself, a mirror writes nothing). If no loading screen came since leaving (title, death, rest load, merge reload), it warps to `game::last_idol()` through `WarpWait`. During a boss fight (gauge or spectate wait) both this and the own-world restore warp wait (new: `ownworld` checked only the warp gate before). Reconnecting before cancels it. The host never warps. Join: unchanged, `join_reload = 1` (default) always uses the idol warp + loading screen; a block without a known idol still says "walk there yourself".

**Notices (`[DISPLAY] native_notices = 1`).** `Important` (one-button box): host "A companion has entered your world." (first `HostConnected` edge), joiner "X closed their world. Co-op has ended.", "Back in your own world. ..." (both restore modes). Everything else stays `bossrules::notice` (overlay); the status line stays on the overlay. Other call sites can switch with `dialog::game_notice(text, Importance::Important, secs)` (world.rs's idol notice was left alone: another agent edits world.rs).

**Log lines.** `dialog: EventText hooked ...` / `NOT hooked`, `dialog: question #N queued: "..."`, `dialog: waiting (why)`, `dialog: opened (text id I, buttons B, type T): "..."`, `dialog: shown after S s`, `dialog: question #N answered Yes|No after S s`, `dialog: result R (0 = backed out) counts as No`, `dialog: question #N not shown (why): the caller falls back`, `dialog: notice on the overlay instead (why): ...`, `dialog: closed without a result, counts as No`; `items: used X -> asking "..."`, `items: X confirmed (Yes)`, `items: X declined (No), nothing done`, `items: X: the dialog could not be shown (why), acting as before`, `items: used X while the question for Y is open, ignored`, `resync: confirmed in the Kindred Bell dialog`; `leave: no longer in the shared world (link L, intent I)`, `leave: waiting for the own-world restore first`, `leave: boss fight, the reload waits until it ends`, `leave: a loading screen already came after leaving ...: no extra reload`, `leave: reloading at our last idol I (S s after leaving) ...`, `leave: connected again, no reload`, `leave: a companion entered our world (host, no reload)`; `own world: restore due during a boss fight, waiting until it ends`.

**Live test.**
1. Log at start: `dialog: EventText hooked`. Use the Bell solo: the game's box "Open your world to a companion?" with two buttons; check the labels are Yes / No. No: nothing, `declined`. Yes: hosting as before.
2. Charm / Thread the same. Back out with the cancel key: No.
3. Connected, Bell: "Resync the world?" Yes: both rest (`resync: confirmed in the Kindred Bell dialog`, then the §22 lines). In a boss fight: "Not during a boss fight."
4. `confirm_items = 0`: no box, the old behaviour (Bell double use).
5. Joiner, `own_world_mode = mirror`: Thread -> Yes: the "Back in your own world..." box, then `leave: reloading at our last idol` and a loading screen. Merge mode: only the merge reload (`no extra reload`, or the restore lines). Leave during a boss fight: nothing until the gauge is gone.
6. Host: on connect the "A companion has entered your world." box, no warp. Host closes its world: the joiner gets "... closed their world" in the box and reloads.
7. If a box never appears: `not shown (the game did not show it)` and the old action; send the log.

**Risks.** Button ids 3/4/1 are inferred; if the labels are wrong, change `BUTTONS_YES_NO` / `BUTTONS_OK`. The generic dialog may not pause the game: a box during combat leaves the player standing (notices skip boss fights only; `native_notices = 0` turns them off). `Open` runs from the FrameBegin task, not the Lua step. If state 17 is set later than 1.5 s after `Open`, the dialog is up but counted as not shown, and the fallback acts while it is up (its late answer is dropped). The reload on leave uses `game::last_idol()` (in mirror mode it may be an idol rested at in the shared world, which is then unlocked in our world; merge picks its own reload idol). The own-world boss wait's log line prints once per game run.

## 27. Rest resets both, stealth deathblow window, no quitting in co-op (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

Three owner reports from the 2026-10-23 test (build 6.5: the native session never engaged, `patch check failed: no memory near the module`, fixed in 7.1, so everything below was seen on our own sync, mode 4). Code: `restsync.rs`, `quitblock.rs` (new); `world.rs` (rest notes, `respawn_all_noted`), `enemies.rs` (rest window, `on_rest`, `approach_kind` / `throw_window` / `stealth_claims`), `nsession.rs` (`on_rest`, `Reason::Rest`, type-22 claims), `puppet.rs` (`restsync::tick`, P3 rest checks), `resync.rs` (one call), `settings.rs` (2 keys), `lib.rs`. Protocol unchanged (9); nsession `PROTO` unchanged (Leave reason 9 is new; an older build logs it as `Other`).

### 1. A rest by either player resets the enemies for both

**Root cause (logs: host `log.txt` around line 18839, friend `friend-log.txt` lines ~739-800, session from 1792748192).** The hypothesis "the host is far from the idol, the mailbox does nothing" is not what happened: the host stood ~140 m from idol 1101952 and its enemies DID respawn (host T lines: `1100233` HP 0 at ...380-382, 151 at ...383; `1100214` 0 -> 112; `1100383` 0 -> 153). The friend's did not: friend T lines show `1100218` back at its spawn point (167.1,-55.9,135.4 -> 164.7,-55.1,134.6, so it WAS respawned) with HP 0, and at ...402 `enemies: HP mismatch: dead here / alive on host [1100239, ... 1100383, ...]` (18 ids). Sequence: the client's own rest respawns its enemies at once; the host's mirrored rest arrives a round trip later and the idol object respawns after its 1.333 s timer (RE-DEATH-RESET §2.1); in between the host still sends HP 0, `client_frame` wrote it onto the respawned enemies (killed again), and once the host's respawn arrived the corpse rule (HP cannot revive a corpse) kept them dead. A host rest works because the host respawns first. The same race exists under the native session (the owner's type 5 -> `0xA4B830` -> `0x9EE7E0` kill) [ARV, not seen live].

**Fix.**
- `restsync::note(kind, idol)` on every rest that respawns our enemies: our own rest (`REST_NOW`), the mirrored mailbox rest, the direct `respawn_all` (other block), resync. Not the join rest (`silent_rest`: there the host's dead SHOULD win).
- Client window `HOLD_KILLS_FOR` = 12 s: an enemy alive here and dead on the host is not written to 0; it stays on its own AI at its spawn point (not NetAI, not hooked) and counts as locally owned. After the window the host's HP decides again. A load clears the window.
- Verification (both sides, after 4 s): WCM `+0x67C` (AllChrRespawn state) seen non-zero, or enemies dead before the rest alive again. A mirrored mailbox rest (or resync) that shows neither calls `0xA4C0F0` + `0x899500` directly (`world::respawn_all`) and is verified once more.
- The frame after a rest: `Enemies::on_rest` resets the per-load kill bookkeeping (`looted`, `dead_since`, `no_loot`, lethal hold, alive counters, HP baseline). Before, `looted` kept every enemy that had died this load, so a respawned enemy the friend killed again gave the host no loot.
- Native session: `nsession::on_rest` disengages (Leave reason `Rest`, not latched) and holds the next handshake for 10 s (`REST_HOLD`), so the owner tables / type-5 HP from before the rest cannot restore the dead state; our own sync with the rest window runs meanwhile; the new handshake's netId list carries the new HPs (the engage HP adoption takes the lower of two live HPs; dead ones are not adopted).
- P3 dead list: a rest after a death means no restore. Host: no `EV_BACK` when a rest of ours came after our death; a `BULK_DEAD_LIST` arriving after a rest that followed our `EV_BACK` is ignored. Client: nothing reported when a rest came after the host's death.
- A host death still does not reset (unchanged); a wipe still resets both (unchanged).

**Log lines.** `rest: our own|mirrored (idol mailbox)|mirrored (direct AllChrRespawn)|resync rest at idol I: enemies reset here; for 12 s the friend's dead enemies are not forced on ours`; `rest: ... respawn verified|NOT seen (AllChrRespawn state seen B, dead before D, alive again A, still dead K)[ after the direct fallback]`; `rest: the ... rest at idol I respawned nothing here (idol object not loaded or not near us?): AllChrRespawn + objects called directly (ok = B)`; `rest: enemy E respawned here, still dead on the host: not killed (waiting for the host's respawn)` (first 10 per rest); `rest: window over: N enemies were alive here and dead on the host after the rest; the host now reports A of them alive, K still dead (the host's HP decides again)`; `enemies: rest: kill bookkeeping reset (N looted ids forgotten)`; `nsession: <kind> rest: disengaged, a new handshake in 10 s ...`, `nsession: DISENGAGED (... rest: enemies reset)`, on the friend `friend left (Rest)`; `death: a rest since our death reset the world for both: no restore`, `death: friend's list of N dead enemies is from before a rest: ignored ...`, `death: host is back, but a rest since its death reset the world for both: nothing to report`.

**Live test.** (a) Both in one area, kill 5+ enemies near the friend, the host walks 100+ m away in the same block. The friend rests: friend log `rest: our own rest ...`, a few `rest: enemy E respawned here, still dead on the host`, then `rest: window over: N ... A of them alive, 0 still dead`; host log `rest: mirrored (idol mailbox) ... respawn verified`; by eye the enemies are back on both screens and stay alive. (b) The host rests: the same with roles swapped. (c) The friend rests while the host is in another block: host `mirrored (direct AllChrRespawn)`. (d) If a host line says `respawned nothing here ... called directly`, the mailbox failed far from the idol: note where the host stood. (e) Native session engaged (7.1): a rest -> `nsession: ... disengaged, a new handshake in 10 s`, `ENGAGED` again ~10 s later, enemies alive on both. (f) The host dies, the friend rests before the host is back: `death: a rest since our death ...`, no kills restored. (g) After a rest the friend kills a respawned enemy: the host still logs `loot: E died without our kill`.

**Risks.** The 12 s window assumes the host's respawn arrives within it (RTT + 1.333 s + the 4 s fallback); a host that cannot respawn (in a boss fight it is expelled instead; a Remnant memory skips the respawn) kills the friend's respawned enemies again after 12 s, as before. During the window an enemy alive only here can attack the client with its own AI while the host's copy lies dead. The fallback may respawn twice if the state machine finished between two polls (it spans several WCM updates [ARV]). Disengaging the native session on every rest costs ~10 s of our own sync per rest.

### 2. Stealth deathblow window (backstab, plunge) in co-op

**Root cause [ARV + logs].** In mode 4 the client's enemies are driven by the host's AI (NetAIManipulator). The stealth deathblow gates live in the defender's local state (TAE 932 / HKS act 161 throwable state, ThrowParam SpEffects; RE-BACKSTAB.md §a/b), which only the local AI produces while it runs. The old throw window handed an enemy to the local AI only at < 5 m (3D) in its rear 120 degrees (friend log: `throw: handoff 1100702 reason behind dist 0.8 m`, `1100217 ... 0.5 m`): the local AI took over a step before the strike, still in the host-driven (often alert) behavior, so the unaware gate was not met in time; plunges from above (more than 5 m up, any direction) were never handed off. Once handed off, the enemy stayed local until the player was 6.5 m away (`re-engaged ... after 53584 / 79378 ms`: a minute of two AIs). (a) Perception of the other player's native remote happens on the machine that runs the AI: on the host the AI perceives the friend's remote, whose stealth SpEffects are not applied (`0x9E9D10` acts only for the local player, §18b stage 5), so under the host's AI the friend is noticed earlier than solo; on our own AI only the local player's (correct) state and the host's remote count. (c) Native session: the throw path has no ownership check (RE-BACKSTAB §b: nothing in the chain reads the manipulator), but an enemy owned by the other machine is NetAI here, the same problem as mode 4.

**IDA (copy `F:\Temp\ida_v8\db.i64`, jobs o24/o25): the game's ownership request path.** Type 22 = the client's candidates `{i32 n; u32 (netId | prio << 16)[n]}` from `T+0x380..0x388` (`0xA1CED0`). The host keeps one list per player (`0xA1D7E0`, `0xA1C6C0`); `0xA1D550` settles each pair of players with `0xA1C590`: for a netId both list, player A keeps it unless B's prio is higher, or equal with fewer candidates in total; the loser's entry is removed (`0xA1B700`); `0xA1DD00` builds the owner lists (23) from what remains. Higher prio wins [VAR].

**Fix.**
- `stealth_window_m` (default 15, 0 = the old rule): `approach_kind` = "behind" within that many metres horizontally in the rear 120 degrees (the rear half and 5 m more while held), or "above" 2-25 m higher within min(window, 12 m) horizontally (plunge); the facing comes from the learned `Facing` mapping.
- Mode 4 (`throw_window`): hand off on such an approach, on an active throw node, or on an empty posture bar (both within 8 m; RE-BACKSTAB patch A step 2.2, the host's next behavior record used to undo the local stagger); it stays while any of them holds and re-engages 3 s after the last one (was: 1 s and > 6.5 m away).
- Native session (`stealth_claims`, both roles, while engaged at stage >= 2): enemies on such an approach are claimed until 3 s after it ends. Client: our outgoing 22 gets prio 0xFFFF for them (appended if missing). Host: the friend's 22 entries for the host's claims are removed before `0xA1C6C0` (unless the friend claims them too). Ownership then moves through the game's own distribution (23), and slot 37 puts the enemy on our ComManipulator (owned here). The alert state stays with the owner (the AI that runs).
- Unit tests: `enemies::tests::stealth_window_geometry`, `nsession::tests::stealth_claims_raise_our_prio_and_strip_the_friends`.

**Log lines.** `throw: handoff E reason behind|above|node|posture dist D m height H m (window W m, facing mapping M)`, `throw: re-engaged E after T ms`; `stealth: claiming enemy E (netId 0x.., behind|above, height H m) through the game's ownership list`, `stealth: enemy E runs on our own AI now (owned here after the claim)`, `stealth: claim on enemy E dropped (approach over; it was (never) owned here)`, every 10 s while engaged `nsession: stealth claims (10 s): ours now N; candidate lists sent with a claim P, last with K claimed entries` (client) / `friend's candidates for them removed S` (host).

**Live test.** (1) The friend crouches up behind an unaware patrolling soldier from 20 m: `throw: handoff ... reason behind dist ~15`; the red deathblow marker shows and the backstab plays as solo. (2) From a roof above an enemy: `reason above`, the plunging deathblow works. (3) Get noticed and fight face to face: `re-engaged ... after` a few seconds (not a minute). (4) Posture-break deathblow on a soldier: `reason posture`, the deathblow plays out. (5) Native session (7.1): the same with `stealth: claiming enemy`, then `runs on our own AI now` within ~1-2 s; host log `friend's candidates for them removed` when the host sneaks. (6) `stealth_window_m = 0`: the old behaviour. Compare the window with solo.

**Risks.** A handed-off / claimed enemy can be fought by two AIs for a few seconds (the host's copy keeps its own state). The local AI resumes from its own stale state; that it is "unaware" at once is not verified [ARV]. On slopes "above" also triggers on an enemy that simply stands lower. Ownership moves only at the host's next distribution (`T+0x398` timers), ~1 s [ARV]; the prio layout is from the code, the prio range the game itself uses was not checked (0xFFFF assumed highest). The host's AI still perceives the friend's remote without its stealth SpEffects.

### 3. No Quit Game while in co-op

**IDA (copy `F:\Temp\ida_v8\db.i64`, jobs o1-o23).** The InGameStep update `0x140A71190` reads `0x1408BF330` = MenuMan menu state 135 (`MenuMan+0x20+4*135`, setter `0x1408C68B0`); when set it fades out, sets its exit reason (`+193 = 1`), waits for saves and finishes, and its parent `0x140A81930` builds `TitleStep(1)` (`0x140A89DA0`): the TitleStep is far too late to cancel. Writers of state 135: `0x140DD9AC0` (the `_Do_call` of lambda `12343e8a...`, vtable `0x142B0B018`: `state135 = 1; byte_143D8A044 = 1`) and `0x1408BF370` (EMEVD dispatcher `0x140904EF0`, a script's own return). The lambda's job is made by `0x140DD7C30`, called from `0x140DD6920` / `0x140DD6C40` (job chains with dialog texts 110500 / 110501, a wait while `byte_143B11A90` (saving) is set, then the ReturnTitle save `0x1408322E0`) and from `0x1408C2B30` (a MenuMan system-message flow). `0x140DD6920` / `0x140DD6C40` are only called from `0x140CC37D0`, a menu job factory reached from a menu item's lambda (`0x140CD3CF0` -> `0x140CB0900` -> `0x140CB3690` -> `0x140CAE890`) [ARV: the pause menu's Quit Game]. Other title paths, not touched: the GameMan save/load requests `+0xB60..0xB6E`, the Lua `RegistReturnTitle` (`0x140679EE0`, network sign-out), the cheat-detection module.

**Fix (`quitblock.rs`, `[GAMEPLAY] block_quit_in_coop = 1`).** A detour on `0x140CC37D0` (12-byte prologue of pushes) only arms (time) and runs the original; a detour on `0x140DD9AC0` (prologue `48 83 EC 28 BA 01 00 00 00`) returns without setting state 135 when armed within 60 s, the link is HostConnected / ClientConnected, the option is on and the mod is alive; else the original runs. Each call consumes the arm. Not blocked: no companion connected (hosting / searching / off), the EMEVD return, the system-message flow (never armed), deaths / loads / warps (other paths). The frame task logs it and shows `dialog::game_notice("Use Severed Thread to leave co-op before quitting.", Important, 8)`. Unit test `quitblock::tests::blocks_only_an_armed_pause_menu_quit_while_connected`.

**Log lines.** `hook: quit menu flow (0xCC37D0) ... detoured`, `hook: return-to-title request (0xDD9AC0) ... detoured`, `quit: 2/2 hooks installed` (or `...; Quit Game is NOT blocked in co-op`), `quit: the pause menu's return-to-title flow started (0xCC37D0), link L, block_quit_in_coop B`, `quit: Quit Game / return to title cancelled while in co-op (link L): menu state 135 not set; notice shown`, `quit: return to title from the pause menu allowed (link L, ...)`, `quit: return-to-title request (0xDD9AC0) not from the pause menu's flow (game's own), allowed`, `quit: menu state 135 (return to title) is set now, link L`.

**Live test.** (1) Start: `quit: 2/2 hooks installed`. (2) Connected (host, then client): pause menu -> Quit Game -> confirm: `quit: ... flow started`, `quit: ... cancelled`, the box "Use Severed Thread to leave co-op before quitting.", the game stays in the world and keeps working (menus, items, rest). (3) Severed Thread, then Quit Game: `allowed`, title screen. (4) Solo: Quit Game works. (5) Report if after a cancel the screen stays black or the menu is stuck (then the block must move to `0x140CC37D0` itself), or if no `flow started` line appears for the pause menu (then `0x140CC37D0` is not the pause menu's quit, and `menu state 135 is set now` shows that the request took another path).

**Risks.** `0x140CC37D0` = the pause menu's Quit Game is inferred from the call graph and the texts [ARV]. The rest of the menu chain still runs after the cancel (the ReturnTitle save and its "saving" wait): harmless as far as read; the visible state after it is not known. A game-forced return through the same lambda within 60 s of opening the quit flow would be blocked while connected. The detours run on the game thread; the menu factory has C++ EH frames (an exception through our Rust frame would abort, as for every detour).

## 28. Driver crash nvwgf2umx.dll+0xfbbc10: material constant over-read guarded (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

Corrects §18's (Live 7.1 notes) "nothing of ours is plausibly involved; not changed": the faulting read is identified and the over-read is now removed at its source.

**Evidence: every crash and what the mod did in the ~40 s before it** (host `log.txt`; friend `friend-uploads/log-HostPlayer-20261023-134311.txt` = `Downloads/794b39j.txt` for 10-22/10-23 morning). All have the same stack `[..]+0x1b5c732 [..]+0x30c9d2`, the fault at `rdx + 0x170` (once `+0x180`) and `rdx` 0x20-0x180 bytes below a 64 KB boundary; often two render threads with the same `rdx`.

| Crash (local) | Machine | Fault / rdx | What came before |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10-22 09:23:55 | friend | 0x1edbc0000 | warp to the host's idol (area load -7 s), teleport -4 s, ghost CREATED + dressed -3 s |
| 10-23 04:13:49 | host | 0x29fc0090 / 0x29fbff20 | join snapshot -13 s (after the join load), native CREATED + dress (10 slots) -7 s, 60 fps |
| 08:54:28 | host | 0x441500b0 / 0x4414ff40 | own death -29 s, reload, death restore asked -23 s (friend's 6: killed 0 here), loot / scale lines only; 70-80 fps (unlock) |
| 09:05:15 | friend | 0x237d90150 / 0x237d8ffe0 (+ 2nd thread) | ~20 s at 120 fps with mod 0.03 ms (menu / load), world resumes -4 s; nothing from the mod |
| 11:47:20 | host | 0x20d5500f0 / 0x20d54ff80 (2 threads) | SOLO, speffect param rebuild phase 2 active, a gap in the 10 s stats (load / menu) then `debuff: applied sp 2140000002` -5 s (= back after a death) |
| 13:41:12 | host | 0x1e13f0090 / 0x1e13eff20 (2 threads) | "new world" -8 s, native CREATED + dress -1 s (the friend also created + dressed its remote at 13:41:11) |
| 13:43:05 | friend | 0x2031b0000 / 0x2031afe80 (2 threads) | host gone 13:41, own-world reload (warp) 13:42:07, load hitch (fps 63) -3 s; no mod action |
| 14:31:02 | host | 0x20e640090 / 0x20e63ff20 | own death -7 s, area load -6 s (remote forgotten), ForceDead on 6 enemies -1 s; icons patch active since ~13:00 |

Common factor: every crash came 1-60 s (mostly 1-8 s) after a load / respawn / character creation, i.e. while new models and their materials are being allocated. Three of eight had no mod action at all in the window; one was solo; three predate the icons patch. fps (60 / 70-120), GPU OC, Threaded optimization and RTSS differ between them.

**The resource [IDA `F:\Temp\ida_gpu`, jobs o1-o6].** `0x30C9D2` is the return address of `call 0x1B5C6F0` at `0x30C9CD` inside `0x30C750`; `0x1B5C6F0` = `ctx->UpdateSubresource(*(wrap+0x18), 0, NULL, src, 0, 0)` (`[ctx+0x180]`). Chain: `GXFlverMaterial` vtable `0x14325B990` slot 2 (`0x2FEC80`) -> `0x30B870(material+208, ...)` -> `0x30C750(block, cbset, ctx, n, -1)`. `block+0x28/+0x30` is a vector of float4 material constants. If the material has texture scroll (`cbset+0x5C` mask != 0) the vector is copied into a 400-byte (25 x float4) stack buffer first; otherwise (the crash path) `src = block+0x28` itself. With a NULL box D3D11 reads the constant buffer's whole ByteWidth (400 bytes; the fault offsets +0x170/+0x180 are the last 32-byte chunk of 400). Materials with fewer than 25 constants therefore over-read the heap; it faults only when the vector ends at the end of committed memory. This is the vanilla `GXFlverMaterial` OOB read that me3 documents and tries to absorb with mimalloc on a 6 GB pre-committed heap (FEASIBILITY.md 5.3/5.4); our extra model / material churn (native remote create + dress, ghost, own-world reloads) only adds chances. Not texture streaming, not FaceGen, not the icons (texture init data, a different path: ruled out), not the overlay or the vsync patch (Present path).

**Fix (`gpuguard.rs`, `[DEBUG] gpu_guard = 1`, read at start).** The 5 bytes `e8 1e fd 84 01` at `0x30C9CD` (checked, plus `mov rax,[rdi+28h]` at `0x30C9AB`) become a call to a near stub that copies stack args 5..8 and passes `rdi` (the material block) as argument 9 to `guarded_update`. That reads the vector's begin/end, `GetType` / `GetDesc` of the buffer; if `src == begin`, no box, and the vector is shorter than ByteWidth (<= 64 KB), it uploads a zero-padded copy (stack up to 4 KB) instead, else calls the original unchanged. Written with all threads suspended (`hook::write_frozen`). Diagnostics only on the render threads (atomics); when the over-read would have crossed a 64 KB boundary it probes the next byte with ReadProcessMemory and counts an averted crash. The frame task logs once a minute and at once after an averted crash.

**Load-window mitigations (live settings, [DEBUG]).**
- `native_spawn_delay_s = 10` (2..120; was a fixed 6 s): native remote and auto-ghost are created only this long after the world loaded. Log: `native: remote created X s after the load (native_spawn_delay_s N)`.
- `native_dress_delay_ms = 3000` (0 = the old immediate dress): the remote is dressed once it is in the player chr set, the delay has passed and the world is older than `native_spawn_delay_s`. Logs: `native: dress of 0x.. deferred N ms ...`, `native: deferred dress of 0x.. now, N ms after creation, X s in the world (model ..)`, `native: not dressed (native_dress = 0) ...`. The remote shows the CharaInitParam 9000 outfit for those 3 s.
- `death_restore_delay_s = 8` (2..60; was 4): host's dead-enemy restore (ForceDead) after its own death reload. Log: `death: back after our death (X s after the load, death_restore_delay_s N), ...`.
- `overlay_hook = 0` (read at start): `ui::install` (hudhook) is skipped completely; log `ui: overlay NOT installed (overlay_hook = 0) ...`. For A/B only: no status line, names or menu.

**Log lines.** Start: `gpuguard: ON: material constant upload at 0x14030c9cd -> stub 0x...` (or `OFF (gpu_guard = 0)` / `unexpected bytes ... not installed`). Every minute: `gpuguard: 60 s: N material uploads, M padded (shortest vector B, buffer up to B), E ended on a page edge, A would have read unmapped memory`; after a hit `... = DRIVER CRASH AVERTED (last source 0x...)`.

**Live test.** (1) Start: `gpuguard: ON`; play 1 min: the minute line shows many thousands of uploads, most of them padded, and buffer up to 400 B (expected), no visual change (materials, scrolling textures, water, armour colours). (2) Mod cost: `coop: tick ... mod avg` unchanged; frame rate unchanged at 120 fps. (3) The crash scenarios: join + native create, deaths with the friend alive (ForceDead), own-world reload after leaving: no `CRASH? ... nvwgf2umx`; any `DRIVER CRASH AVERTED` line is the proof. (4) A/B: `gpu_guard = 0` on one machine for comparison; `overlay_hook = 0` to exclude hudhook.

**Risks.** [I] The shader may read material registers beyond the defined constants: it now gets zeros instead of heap garbage (the scroll path already gave it stack leftovers); a visual difference would show on the first test. Two virtual calls and a <= 400-byte copy per material bind: a few thousand per frame, estimated well under 0.5 ms spread over the render threads, not measured. The stub address is in the near arena; `guarded_update` must never panic (no indexing / unwraps on that path). Other callers of `0x1B5C6F0` (about 200) are not guarded: no crash so far came from them.

## 29. Lives / death sync under the native session, the idol-found banner, notices without OK, no pausing in co-op (2026-10-23, NOT verified live)

Owner reports from the 7.2 test (host `log.txt` from line 22418, friend `friend-log.txt`; the friend's clock runs about 5 s ahead of the host's). Code: `livesync.rs` (new), `nopause.rs` (new), `nsession.rs` (Lives message, `PROTO` 3 -> 4, throw notes, small pub helpers), `dialog.rs` (status message, banner, the OK box removed), `world.rs` (one line), `enemies.rs` (`force_dead_one`), `posture.rs` (`data_of` / `add_posture` pub(crate), `life_sfx`), `puppet.rs` (one call), `lib.rs`, `settings.rs` (2 keys, 1 comment). coop-net protocol unchanged (9); nsession `PROTO` 4: both players need this build (an older friend does not engage the native session).

### 1. Miniboss lives desync, the corpse that still hits

**Root cause [VAR from the logs + the IDA notes of §18b / §20].** Enemy 1100301 (netId 0x83f, 2 deathblows). Lives are local (`data+0x25C`); only the hit path `0xB6E6A0` lowers them (hit type 5), and no native packet carries them: 21 has HP / posture only, and type 18 (the throw; every deathblow is one) is applied on the receiver only when that machine owns the defender (the damage router gives a remote player's hit on an enemy owned elsewhere action 0). Sequence: friend 14:28:27 (its clock) `tx type 18 ... 3f 08 f0 ff e0 26 a8 00`; the friend owned 1100301 after its stealth claim at :25/:26 -> friend 2 -> 1. Host `rx type 18` at 14:28:23; its copy was NetAI (the host's own claim at :22 "never owned here") -> still 2. Host 14:29:01 `tx type 18 ... 3f 08 f0 ff` (host owned it since :55) -> host 2 -> 1. Friend `rx type 18` at :06, owned again after its claim at :03/:04 -> the received throw took its last life: dead (friend `reward: the game's drop of 268451903 (... deathblows 2)` at :12), alive on the host. posture.rs (our lives sync) is off under the native session (`posture::enabled`). The friend's dead copy kept the host's HP (type 5) and the behavior of the living host copy (70 / 71): at 14:30:37-14:31:37 the friend still listed `1100301 ... own AI` among the alive enemies: the "corpse deals damage" report.

**Fix (`livesync.rs`, `[GAMEPLAY] native_lives_sync = 1`; only while the native session owns the enemies, `nsession::engaged_key() != 0`).**
- Every 150 ms each side scans its enemies (`nsession::enemy_chrs`, netId `chr+0x78`, the data module in one read) and sends every enemy below its NpcParam deathblow count (`+0x126`) or dead as `(netId, lives, dead | fresh)` in the new nsession handshake message `Lives` (MSG 6, lane key 0: reliable, in order; at most 1024 per message): changes at once, all of them every 2 s. `fresh` = the death was seen (alive -> dead) during this engagement. A new engagement (load, rest, fallback) starts over.
- The friend's states are merged by minimum (lives only go down, death is final). `livesync::plan`: our copy dead -> nothing (never revived); the friend's copy dead -> ForceDead (`0x751F20(entity, 1)`, no game reward) after writing lives 0 (so the "cannot die" floor cannot hold it); enemies without an entity id through the HP setter `0xBD64E0(0)`; fewer lives there -> lives written + the life SFX `0x9F0410`, and when a life is left the vanilla get-up: HP to max through `0xBD64E0`, posture to full (`0xBD4DE0`).
- No double deathblow: a type-18 throw on that enemy in the last 3 s that this machine applies itself (we sent it, or we own the defender, `0xA4A650`) holds the merge (`nsession::recent_throw`; the defender netId `+0x20` is noted in the send hook and in `serve`). When the hold ends, a life the native throw already took makes the plan "nothing". A death waits 0.4 s for the native kill (type 5) first.
- Rewards: ForceDead pays nothing itself; a fresh death gets our own drops / sen once from `loot_step` ("died without our kill"); an older one (from before a load) is marked no-loot, like the P3 dead list. Boss / miniboss item lots stay with keyitems.rs (lot flags, the 120 s double guard).
- The trailing hit (21) of the friend's deathblow can floor the refilled copy at 1 HP: for 2 s after a refill, HP <= 1 with lives left is set to max again.
- A dead copy stays a corpse: HP raised again without a revive (the revive resets lives to the NpcParam count, a HP write does not; only for deaths with a life used up) is written back to 0. The living copy on the other machine dies through the sync, so its behavior stops coming.

**Log lines.** `livesync: on for this engagement (friend generation G)`, `livesync: enemy E netId 0x.. : lives a -> b (the friend's deathblow), gets up: HP and posture full`, `livesync: enemy E netId 0x.. died on the friend's side: killed here too (ForceDead, no game reward, our own loot once | an old death: no loot, ok = B)`, `livesync: enemy netId 0x..: the friend has lives L (dead), ours l; a native throw on it applies here first`, `livesync: enemy netId 0x.. at H HP right after its get-up (the deathblow's own hit): HP full again`, `livesync: enemy netId 0x.. is dead here, HP h came back (the friend's copy alive?): kept at 0` (first 40 lines, then every 50th); every 10 s and at the end of an engagement `livesync (10 s): sent S states in M msgs, received R, lowered L, killed K (k with loot), held H, get-up refills guarded G, corpses kept dead C; here N enemies short of lives, D dead`. After a fresh kill `loot: E died without our kill, gave ...`.

### 2. "SCULPTOR'S IDOL FOUND" banner

**IDA [VAR, copy `F:\Temp\ida_v73\db.i64`, jobs o1-o20].** Banners are `0x1408C75F0(u32 type 1..22, bool)` (EMEVD 2007[2] = case 2 of `0x140909870`; Lua `SetTextEffect` `0x14068DF80`): with the new menu system it stores `{type, flag}` at menu system `+16948` (`0x140D882D0`), else in MenuMan's ring `+10992`. The texts are per-language images (`engUS_TextEffect` ...), so no custom text. The idol: `SprjLuaEventScriptImitation` registers `OnEvent_BonfireFirstLvUp` (`0x14069AF20`: waits for event 4086 of the idol, then schedules `OnEvent_BonfireFirstLvUp_TextEffect`), and that one (`0x14069AFF0`) is `SetTextEffect(13)`. So type 13 = the banner of a newly lit idol. (`0x140D87FF0` is the area-name banner, PlaceName.)

**Fix.** world.rs's "the friend unlocked idol flag F for us too" now calls `dialog::idol_banner`: on the game thread, 3 s in the world, no load pending, the menu system present: `0x1408C75F0(13, 0)`. Several idols within 10 s get one banner; refused, or not possible within 8 s -> the old overlay text. Log: `dialog: the game's banner 13 shown (idol found)`, `dialog: the game refused banner 13: overlay notice instead`, `dialog: banner 13 not shown (why): overlay notice instead`.

### 3. Notices without an OK box

Owner: no text box that needs OK; the yes/no questions of the co-op items stay. `dialog::game_notice(.., Important, ..)` (a companion entered your world, X closed their world, back in your own world (3 places), Quit Game blocked) now uses the game's status message (EMEVD 2007[3], case 3 of `0x140909870` -> `0x14068CEC0`): `0x140E13C30(u32 msg, bool)` runs a NewMenuSystem lambda (vtable `0x142B1DE00`, slot 2 `0x140E186F0`) -> `0x140D87E40(menu, msg, flag)`, which copies the Event Text of `msg` (`0x140C791F0` -> our EventText detour, ids 1999990000..7) into the menu system (`+17016` / `+17024`, flag `+17072`; the area-name banner `0x140D87FF0` writes the same way at `+16960`). No menu handle, no input, no pause. At least 4 s between two; it waits up to 8 s for the world (3 s in the world, no load, the menu system present), then the overlay. The one-button GenDialog path is gone (only questions open the box). `native_notices = 0` = overlay only. Not used: `0x140E15BD0(kind, wchar*)` (the EMEVD 2007[4] case: free text into the priority message queue `0x143D8A4B0`, type 99) [I: which HUD element shows it is not known]. Log: `dialog: notice as the game's status message (text id I, after S s): "..."`, `dialog: notice on the overlay instead (why): ...`.

### 4. No pausing in co-op

**IDA [VAR, o1-o8].** `NS_SPRJ::CSPauseManagerImp` (instance `0x143E9DA30`, built in `0x140A6F060`) holds `CSPauseIns` objects: `_Instant` (one frame), `_MenuHandle` (made by `0x141173830` for every menu opened with "pause": in-game menus `0x140D884F0`, EMEVD / Lua message boxes and system messages `0x140E13740`, our GenDialog), `_DebugBool`. `0x141173910`, called once per frame by the InGameStep update `0x140A6D490` (`0x140A6DAF6`), sets `+8 = any IsPause()` (vtable slot 1). The InGameStep then clears its "world runs" byte `+0x318` when `+8` is set (`0x140A6DB35`); that byte gates the world / chr / map updates (`0x140A76BB0`, `0x140A75790`, `0x140A75180`), hence `WCM+0x678` standing still in menus (§18b). `+8` is also read by MenuMan `0x1408CB250`, MapItemMan `0x14091A440` (pickups), `0x140D603B0`, `0x140E8D0E0`, `0x1411CC0B0`. `CSPauseIns_MenuHandle::IsPause` = `0x141172EE0`.

**Fix (`nopause.rs`, `[GAMEPLAY] no_pause_in_coop = 1`).** A detour on `0x141172EE0` (15-byte prologue `40 57 48 83 EC 60 48 C7 44 24 20 FE FF FF FF`, nothing RIP-relative) runs the original and returns false while the link is HostConnected / ClientConnected, the option is on and the mod runs. Menus, the inventory, item pickup popups, the idol menu and our yes/no boxes then leave both worlds running (FromSoft's online behaviour). Instant / debug pauses untouched; solo unchanged. Log: `hook: menu pause (CSPauseIns_MenuHandle::IsPause 0x1172EE0) at ... detoured`, `nopause: menu pause hooked ...` / `NOT hooked ...`, `nopause: a menu / message box is open, the world keeps running (link L)`, `nopause: a menu pauses the world (link L, no_pause_in_coop B)`, `nopause: menu closed`, `nopause: the world is paused by something other than a menu (an instant / debug pause) while connected`.

### Live test (both PCs this build, native session stage 3)

1. Start: `nopause: menu pause hooked`; after `ENGAGED`: `livesync: on for this engagement`.
2. A 2-deathblow miniboss (General Naomori Kawarada, the Chained Ogre, 1100301). B takes the first life: B's orb goes; on A within ~0.5 s (up to 3 s while A's own throw logic is held) `livesync: enemy E netId 0x.. : lives 2 -> 1 (the friend's deathblow), gets up` and one orb gone. A takes the last: it dies on both; on B `livesync: ... died on the friend's side: killed here too (..., our own loot once ...)` (or it died natively: no line), one `loot:` line, no second Prayer Bead. Then roles swapped.
3. Watch for `the friend has lives L ..., ours l; a native throw on it applies here first`, and that this copy then lost exactly one life (no double).
4. A dead enemy near the other player never hits; `corpses kept dead` normally 0 (> 0 = type 5 raised one: report it).
5. Kill regular enemies on each side: `livesync (10 s)` shows `killed` only where the native kill did not come; `held` small.
6. Idol: B lights a new idol (A elsewhere): A sees the big "SCULPTOR'S IDOL FOUND" banner, no box; `dialog: the game's banner 13 shown`.
7. Notices: the host sees "A companion has entered your world." as an on-screen message that fades (no OK); the same for "closed their world", "Back in your own world", Quit Game blocked. If nothing shows but the log says `as the game's status message`: set `native_notices = 0` and report.
8. Connected: open the inventory / pause menu / pick up a key item: the world keeps moving on both screens (`nopause: ... keeps running`), enemies can hit you. Solo: menus pause as before (`a menu pauses the world (link Off ...)`). nsession's `our world update stopped while engaged (menu?)` should no longer appear for menus.

**Risks.** The throw rule (the receiver applies a throw only when it owns the defender) comes from one live sequence [ARV]; if a native throw takes the life after the 3 s hold, the copy loses two. The 0.5 s target holds for kills / life losses without a throw held here; a held one takes up to 3 s. The get-up (full HP / posture) is inferred from vanilla [I], as in §20. A copy dead here whose HP type 5 raised is kept at 0 HP; whether that alone stops a NetAI corpse from hitting is not known [I]: the real fix is the other copy dying. An enemy alive on one side with lives 0 (seen right before its death) is lowered to 0 on the other, not killed (the death follows). Cost: ~7 Hz, about 5 reads per enemy (~230 enemies), not measured. Banner 13 is read from the event's name and code path, not seen [VAR/I]; the idol's own light / sound at the idol object does not play (we are not there). The status message [I]: that `0x140D87E40` is what EMEVD 2007[3] draws on screen is from the code path, not seen; its duration and position are unknown (no reader of `+17016` found by displacement; the area-name banner's `+16960` has none either, both are read through another base). No pausing: in co-op a player in a menu is open to attack (like online play); the world keeps running in the idol menu too; game logic that relied on the pause during a menu (shops, the skill tree) now runs with the world moving [I].

### Review fixes (v7.4)

- **F3 `enemies.rs` `stealth_claims`:** rate-limited to every 100 ms (`claims_at`); before it read the position of all ~230 known chrs (3 ReadProcessMemory each) every frame. `CLAIM_LINGER` (3 s) behaviour unchanged.
- **F1 `settings.rs` / `quitblock.rs`:** `block_quit_in_coop` now defaults to 0 (untested): use the Severed Thread before Quit Game. Code kept; `= 1` turns it on.
- **F2 `nopause.rs`:** the menu-pause detour passes the game's own result through while there is no player, the player's HP is <= 0, or a load is pending (`game::load_pending()`, GameMan+0x10, shared with `safe_to_warp`). Log once per change: `nopause: pass-through (dead/loading)`.
- **F5 `gpuguard.rs` `guarded_update`:** the `block+VEC_BEGIN` / `block+VEC_END` reads are now fault-safe `game::read::<usize>`; an unreadable block passes through to the original and counts in the "passed through" counter.
- **F7 `nsession.rs`:** `REST_HOLD` 10 s -> 13 s (restsync `HOLD_KILLS_FOR` is 12 s; the handshake must start after the kill hold ends).
- **F8 `restsync.rs` `tick`:** the direct `respawn_all` fallback is skipped during a boss fight / spectating (`rest: fallback skipped, boss fight`).
- **F10 `gpuguard.rs`:** a failed frozen write now resets `ORIG` to 0 (it cannot be stored only after the write: render threads resume inside `write_frozen` and could call the stub with `ORIG` unset).
- **F4 `enemies.rs` `loot_step` (diagnostic):** the skip on `chr+0x1A12` bit 0x10 logs `loot: <id> skipped, no-drop bit set (bits 0x..)` once per enemy, to see whether ForceDead(noReward=1) sets it. No behaviour change.

## 30. Doors open for both, NPC introductions per player, nameplate at any distance, scaling defaults (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Owner reports from the 7.4 test (2026-10-24 01:30-02:05; host `log.txt` from line 24318 = session 1792795666, friend `friend-log.txt`). Code: `doors.rs`, `npcintro.rs` (new); `world.rs` (calls only), `ui.rs`, `tuning.rs`, `settings.rs`, `lib.rs`. coop-net protocol unchanged; one new game event kind (8, below): both players need this build.

### 1. A door one player opened was closed for the other

**Root cause (IDA, copy `F:\Temp\ida_world`, jobs w1-w6).** The player's own ObjAct (`0x6BA7B0` -> `0x6BC1B0`, which sets `info+114 = 1`) calls the state object's slot 4: for SprjObjActDoorState / SprjObjActOneDirOpenDoorState that is `0x6B6470` = `SetFlag(f, GetFlag(f) == 0)` with `f = state+0x10`: **a toggle, not "set open"**. A flag that arrives from the friend (world.rs, SetEventFlag) changes only the flag; the door object stays as it is until the block is loaded again. Sequence that matches the report: the friend opens door D (flag on, sent to us, our door object still closed); we open D (our door opens, the toggle switches the flag OFF, sent to the friend); at our next load (the host's death reload at 1792796333 in this test) D comes back closed here, while the friend's door object stays open until it reloads. Log evidence is thin: world.rs logs only the first 30 and every 100th flag, so the door flag itself is in neither log. The friend's `own world: we played ObjAct animation 10700250` (1792796129, friend clock, ~6 s ahead) and `the friend played ObjAct animation 10700250` (1792796242 = the host, ~1792796236 host clock, 100 s before the host's death) are two executions of the same ObjAct animation by both players, as the toggle sequence needs [I: same animation id, the door entity is not logged]. Suspects checked: (a) `0x899500` (the object respawn after a rest, vanilla and our direct fallback) only resets objects whose `+0x10` part has a `+0x30` pointer (`0x870C20` / `0x10A8860` -> `0x874A10(obj, 4)`, breakables); ObjAct infos are not touched, so a rest does not close doors [IDA]. (b) bossgate only delays / holds friend flags (`waited 395, held 9, applied late 9`, `switched back 0`, `dropped 0` in this session): no door flag was switched off by it. (c) is the cause, together with the toggle.

**How the game replays a friend's ObjAct** (type 45, sent only with the native session at stage 4): `0x6BE740` (WorldObjActMan update) drains the 45 queue (`0x6BEBB0` -> `0x8D8750`; packet `u16 actor netId, u8 block slot (5 bits), u8 state, i16 ObjAct index`) and calls `0x6BA910(block, index, actor, state)`: slot 5 sets the flag to `state` (no toggle: `info+113 = 1, +114 = 0`), then the ObjAct executes with the object's animation. The debug menu "RepolicateObjActInfo by event flg" (`0x6BB860` case 5 -> `0x6BDE80` -> `0x6BAC30` -> `0x6B2E30` -> `0x6BAAA0`) re-executes ObjActs whose flag is on, but skips infos with `+0xAC != 0`, so it is not used.

**Fix (`doors.rs`).** (1) A door flag the friend switched ON whose door is loaded here and still closed is replayed 300 ms later with `0x6BA910(block, index, -1, 1)` (no actor: our remote plays nothing, the door opens and its flag stays on). "Closed" = the object is not at the animation the execution would play (request builder `0x6BCBC0`, `req[6]`; object `0x899310(ObjMan, info+0x50)`, `obj+0x138 == anim && obj+0x128 & 4`, the game's own check `0x8777F0`); an info that is executing (`+0x54 == 3`) is skipped. (2) Guard: our own write switching OFF a door flag the friend switched on this session is put back ON at once and not sent (world.rs ring branch -> `write_quiet`). (3) 2.5 s and 7.5 s after every rest of ours (own, mirrored, direct, resync; restsync `last_rest`) every loaded door with the flag on is checked and replayed if closed. Doors in another block need nothing: the block load sets them up from the flags. Layout: WorldObjActMan `*(base+0x3D55EC8)`: blocks `+0x30` x `+0x28`, 224 B; block `+0x10` loaded, `+0x78` infos x `+0x80`, 304 B; `*(block)+0x20` slot; info `+0x08` index, `+0x50` entity, `+0x54` run state, `+0x88` state; door state vtables `0x298B628`, `0x298B798`, flag `+0x10`.

**Log lines.** `doors: the friend opened a door: door flag F is on but its door (entity E, block slot S, index I) was closed here: opened (0x6BA910 ok = B, N so far)`, `doors: our ObjAct toggled door flag F off, but the friend had opened it: kept on (N so far)`, `doors: after the rest: D doors loaded, K with the flag on, R were closed and opened again` (R should be 0), `doors: door flag F (...): object not found here, not replayed` (first 60 lines in all).

**Live test.** A and B near the same closed door (same block). B opens it: on A within ~0.5 s the door swings open by itself (`doors: the friend opened a door ...`). A walks through, dies, reloads: the door is open. Then a door B opened while A was in another block: A arrives, the door is open (no `doors:` line needed). Rest at an idol next to an opened door: `after the rest: ... 0 were closed`. A `kept on` line means the replay came too late (report it).

**Risks.** `0x6BA910` is called from our frame task, not from WorldObjActMan's own update [I: the same function runs there for type 45]. A door that the game closes on purpose with the flag still on would be reopened after a rest (none known). Only the two door state types are replayed; levers / one-shot ObjActs (SprjObjActOneState, OneLoopState) still sync only their flag. With the native session at stage 4 the game's own type 45 replays too; the second replay finds the object open and does nothing.

### 2. NPC first dialogue skipped for the other player

**Why no clean per-flag split.** Sekiro's talk scripts choose their lines with the same group-7 flags (`7AABBkxxx`, one block of 100 per NPC, §19) that hold the quest decisions. The game files are encrypted here (no unpacked talk ESD), the randomizer data (`events.txt` = EMEVD, `annotations.txt` = shops) has no talk scripts, and a GitHub code search found no public Sekiro talk-ESD dump; so "this flag only selects a line" cannot be read from data. Approximation, per NPC and per conversation (`npcintro.rs`, `[GAMEPLAY] shared_npc_intros = 0`; 1 = the old behaviour):
- the sender marks the group-7 flags its game writes during a conversation (`game::talk_idle()` false, plus 1.5 s after) and sends them as game event 8 `EV_TALK_FLAG` (same payload and versioning as `EV_FLAG`);
- the receiver applies them at once, unless the NPC's block of 100 is all off in its own world (it never talked to that NPC): then the friend's talk flags of that block are held;
- when we talk to that NPC ourselves, we hear its introduction from our own state; our writes to the held block are not sent; when our conversation ends, they are undone (`write_quiet`) and the friend's held flags are applied (`apply_flag`): the NPC continues from the friend's decisions, and both worlds agree;
- shared as before: NPC state machines (group 0, 1000-1999), map flags, everything written outside a conversation, and the join snapshot (a joiner whose host already met an NPC gets the host's block in the snapshot: no introduction for the joiner there).

**Log lines.** `npc intro: the friend talked to an NPC we have not met (flags B-B+99): its talk flags wait until we talk to it ourselves`, `npc intro: we talk to the NPC of flags ... for the first time: our flag F = v stays here`, `npc intro: we heard the NPC of flags ... ourselves: U of our talk flags undone, K of the friend's applied (held T s); its decisions are shared again`, `npc intro: session over: N NPC blocks with K of the friend's talk flags were still held ...: dropped`.

**Live test.** B talks to an NPC A never met (e.g. Kotaro, Doujun, the Exiled Memorial Mob): A logs `npc intro: the friend talked to an NPC we have not met ...`. A talks to the same NPC: A hears the first conversation; after it, `we heard the NPC ... K of the friend's applied`; talking again, A gets the dialogue that follows B's choices. B's world is unchanged by A's conversation. With `shared_npc_intros = 1`: no `npc intro:` lines, old behaviour.

**Risks.** All [I]: a block with any flag already on (an EMEVD flag set before the first talk) is "met" and shared at once (old behaviour). A talk-started effect read by EMEVD (an NPC moving away after the friend's first talk) waits in our world until we talk to that NPC; if we never do, the friend's progress with it never reaches us (for the joiner nothing of it is merged into its own world). A choice we make in our own first conversation is undone in favour of the friend's (the first talker decides). Group-7 writes of EMEVD that fall inside the friend's conversation window are tagged as talk flags too. `talk_idle()` is false for item prompts too [I]. An older build drops event 8 (`world: unknown event 8`): update both.

### 3. Friend's nameplate at any distance

`ui.rs`: the plate faded out between 100 and 150 m (alpha 0 beyond 150 m). Now it is full up to 100 m, then fades slowly to at least 85 % (never hidden), and from 40 m shows `name  N m`. The text is drawn at the font's fixed pixel size, so it never shrinks with distance (the font size is set once from the screen height, 16-40 px). Off screen the existing edge arrow with name and distance is unchanged. Still limited to the same map block (puppet.rs: positions are block-local, another block has no common origin): a friend in another block shows no plate or arrow.

### 4. Scaling defaults

`enemy_health_scaling` 35 -> 60, `boss_health_scaling` 100 -> 150, `enemy_posture_scaling` 50 -> 60, `boss_posture_scaling` 50 -> 80 (tuning.rs, settings.rs reader + template, and the four lines of `dist\sekiro-coop\sekiro_coop_settings.ini`). The host's values are used on both machines (unchanged). doctor.rs's test fixtures still use the old string `35/100/50/50` (fixtures only).

### 5. Flag hook ring overflow during rests (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Live 7.4: world.rs `lost` jumped by ~420 on both machines in the rest window (host 379 -> 799 between 1792796222 and 1792796282, the friend's rest at 1792796226; friend 455 -> 882): the rest's event restart writes hundreds of flags within a frame or two, the SetEventFlag hook ring held only 128, and every write that fell out was never sent (doors, idols, quest flags written around a rest were lost for good). Fix:
- `hook.rs`: ring 128 -> 2048 entries (32 KB data pages + the code page, `FR_ALLOC` 0xA000 from the near arena; the stub masks with `and eax, imm32` now, the imm8 form is sign-extended and only reaches 127). Compile-time check: power of two and below the code page. ownworld.rs / doctor.rs read the same constant.
- `world.rs` `diff_resync`: once a load has settled (5 s) our shared records are kept as a baseline (`shadow`, `read_shared_records`); after any overflow (300 ms after the last lost entry) and 3 s after every rest of ours (restsync `last_rest`), the records are read again, and every flag that changed since the last check, passes `should_sync`, and differs from what the friend was told (`known`) or is queued (`dirty`) is queued to send; the baseline is then replaced. Flags the friend set, our suppressed NPC-intro writes and flags already sent are equal to `known` and skipped; the doors.rs guard applies here too. A load resets the baseline (load init writes are not news). Bit layout as SetEventFlag (unit test `diff_finds_exactly_the_changed_flags`).
- Log: `world: flag ring overflow, resynced N flags by diff (C changed since the last check, R records, T ms; ring lost L in total)`; after a rest the same line with `after a rest` when N > 0. Live check: rest at an idol, the `lost` counter in the 60 s `world: flags sent ...` line should stay flat now; if it still grows, the overflow line shows how many flags the diff caught.
- Risks: a flag that changed and changed back between two checks is not seen (nothing to send). A record diff costs one shared-record walk (~4500 resolver calls, not measured live: see `T ms`). A shared record missing from the baseline counts as all off, so its on flags are compared with `known` and may be sent once.

## 31. Phase B kept in the world, kills paid once per rest cycle, dead set and boss records, ownerless enemies get an owner (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Owner reports from the live 7.4 test (2026-10-24 ~01:30-02:05; host `log.txt` from line 24318, friend `friend-log.txt`, the friend's clock ~5 s ahead). IDA on a private copy `F:\Temp\ida_v75\db.i64`, jobs o1-o10. Code: `bossrules.rs`, `enemies.rs`, `livesync.rs`, `persist.rs` (new), `nsession.rs` (`ownfill`, block churn), `puppet.rs` (one call), `settings.rs` (one key), `lib.rs`. coop-net protocol unchanged; persist.rs uses a new bulk kind 50 (an older friend never takes it); nsession `PROTO` unchanged. Built, unit tests pass; nothing below has run in the game.

### 1. Phase B ended 4 s after the host's death

**Evidence.** Host: `spectate: start (phase B, no death screen) boss 1100800` at 1792795992, `world: sent flag 600 = true` the same second, `nsession: our world update stopped while engaged` + `native: area load` + `DISENGAGED (area load)` at ...996, then `spectate: release alive again after 6 s` with the host at its idol (P = -29.4,-74.2,-31.6, the load position of the session) while the friend fought on (`boss: defeated 1100800` at ...018 came from our livesync ForceDead). No `a reload came during phase B` line: the held death reload `0xA8F7E0` was never called. None of our warps logged anything (no `warp:` / `join:` / `expel:` / `own world:` line).

**IDA.** The InGameStep update `0xA71190` leaves the world (`v71`) on GameMan (`0x143D5C0D0`) `+0x10`, `+0xB6C`, `+0xB6E`, `+0xDB1`, `+0xD9A`, menu state 135 or a CSDeadStep (`+800`). `+0x10` (the load request, `game::load_pending`) is written by `0x832EE0` (only from `0x6D7070` and an InGameStep clear), `0x8333B0` (from `0xA72640`, a GameMan-requested warp) and `0x8324B0` (two vtable functions). `0x6D7070` is the shared tail of every warp: the death reload `0xA8F7E0`, EMEVD WarpPlayer `0x68E7F0` (two sites in the dispatcher `0x904EF0`), the Lua event warps `0x68E850` (ten `OnEvent_*` handlers) / `0x68E870` (= `game::warp_to_idol`), `0xEBA2C0`. So a script warp (most likely EMEVD reacting to the death, flag 600) reloaded the host without a CSDeadStep [ARV: the exact caller is not in the log; the new stack line names it next time].

**Fix (`bossrules.rs`).** A detour on `0x6D7070` (prologue `48 89 5C 24 10 57 48 83 EC 20`, nothing RIP-relative): while we wait dead (`SPECTATE` or `WAITING`, main player HP <= 0, mod alive) the load request is not run (returns 0); our release (state `RELEASING` / `OFF` before it calls the reload) and our warps (they need a living player) pass. The dead player stays in the world with the camera on the friend until the boss falls, a wipe, a rest, the friend leaves / the link drops, or the 15 min timeout; then the normal death (CSDeadStep, penalty, reload) follows as before. Safety nets kept: `0xA8F7E0` held in `SPECTATE` -> phase A wait; `on_loading`; `hand_back`; the panic reset. New: the call stack of a held request is logged; a load pending in phase B that did not come through `0x6D7070` is logged once with the GameMan request bytes. New end condition: a rest of ours (own or mirrored) since the start. Guards from the research: a death within 10 s of a boss gauge counts as a death in the fight (the game clears the gauge before the death step; not after the boss fell); the friend's "boss defeated" waits up to 20 s for the defeat in our world (our HandleBossDefeat, or the boss dead / gone here) before the release.

**Log lines.** `bossrules: 3/3 hooks installed, phase B true, spectator camera true, load requests held while spectating true`, `hook: load request (0x6D7070) at ... detoured`, `spectate: N load request(s) (0x6D7070) while we wait dead, not run (state 3): callers [0x..., ...] (0x68E7F0 = EMEVD WarpPlayer, ...)`, `spectate: a load is pending during phase B by a route we do not hold (GameMan +0x10 .., +0xB4A .., ...)`, `spectate: the friend's boss B fell; waiting for its defeat in our world before the release (up to 20 s)`, `spectate: boss B defeat seen here | (boss dead / gone here) | not seen here (20 s) after T ms, releasing`, `spectate: release rest after N s`.

**Live test.** Die in a boss fight with the friend alive: `spectate: start (phase B ...)`, then (if the script fires) `load request(s) ... not run` with the callers, NO `area load` on our side, the camera stays on the friend until `boss: defeated` / the wipe; then the death screen and the reload. If `a load is pending during phase B by a route we do not hold` appears, report the line (another route).

**Risks.** Holding every load request while dead also holds a script warp the game meant for the dead player (the death reload after the release goes to the idol anyway). The stack walk (`RtlCaptureStackBackTrace`) runs on the game thread inside the request, only while we wait dead.

### 2. No sen / XP / drops for the friend's kills under the native session

**Root cause (two).** (a) Classification: 28 of 29 friend kills were logged `an old death: no loot`; every one right after an `ENGAGED` line: the friend had killed them while the session was disengaged (friend in another block, the host's death reload), so its first scan of the new engagement saw them dead with no earlier sight -> no FRESH flag -> "old". (b) The one fresh kill (1100800) paid nothing either: `loot: 1100800 skipped, no-drop bit set (bits 0x10)`. IDA o1/o9: ForceDead `0x751F20(id, noReward)` queues `ForceDeadCmd` (vtable `0x1429AC6C8`, its slots call the chr's vfuncs +280 / +288 with the flag); the drop `0x9FFF50` and the sen/XP `0x9FFD50` both return early through `0x9F2DE0` when `chr+0x1A12 & 0x10` is set: our "pay once" call after ForceDead(noReward=1) could never pay.

**Fix.** The rule is now "paid once per enemy per rest cycle": `persist.rs` keeps `rewarded` (session-wide, across loads, cleared by a rest or a wipe); `loot_step` adds every id the game paid itself (bits 1|2) and every id it paid. A friend's kill (livesync, the P3 restore `kill_ids`, the dead set) pays us when the id is not in it, whatever the engagement. `force_dead_one(id, loot = true)` remembers the id (`force_loot`); `loot_step` clears bit 0x10 for those ids before calling the two functions. The FRESH flag is only logged now.

**Stats miscount.** `here 188 enemies short of lives`: the lives field `data+0x25C` reads 0 on ordinary enemies (deathblow count 1). Lives are now tracked only for multi-life enemies (`livesync::multi_life`, NpcParam count >= 2): only those are sent while alive (before: ~200 states every 2 s), lowered and counted.

**Log lines.** `livesync: enemy E netId 0x.. died on the friend's side (during this engagement | earlier): killed here too (ForceDead, no game reward, our own loot once (not paid here yet) | already paid here: no loot, ok = B)`, `loot: E died without our kill, gave drops + sen+xp (bits 0x.., after our ForceDead)`, `loot: E already paid this rest cycle, nothing more`.

**Live test.** The friend kills enemies while the host is in another block, then the host comes over: host `livesync: ... (earlier) ... our own loot once` and `loot: ... after our ForceDead`, the host's sen / XP go up; skill points stay level over a session. The host kills, dies, reloads: no second payment (`already paid here`).

### 3. Dead set (ordinary enemies) and boss records (owner rule: only a rest or a wipe resets)

`persist.rs`, every 250 ms in the world while connected (after livesync):
- **Dead set.** An ordinary enemy (deathblow count 1, not the gauge boss) seen alive and then dead in this load is added and sent to the friend (bulk 50: `{u8 1, u8 1, u16 n, i32 ids}`); the friend's are merged. An id in the set that is alive 3 s after a load is killed (ForceDead, at most 4 per scan), paid once if not in `rewarded`. So killed enemies stay dead when a player walks far away and back (whichever machine had them unloaded) and after a single player's death reload, and both machines know the other's kills even when they were never engaged.
- **Boss records** (`[GAMEPLAY] boss_persist_on_death = 1`, default on; 0 = vanilla, as in the other seamless co-op mods): for bosses / minibosses (deathblow count >= 2 or the gauge entity) the lowest state seen this rest cycle (fewer lives first, then HP; here or from the friend, bulk 50 `{u8 1, u8 2, u16 n, (i32 id, i32 hp, i32 max, i8 lives, i8 lives0)}`). The first time a boss shows up 3 s after a load with full HP and every life, a damaged record is written onto it (lives `data+0x25C`, HP through `0xBD64E0`, clamped to its max). A dead boss's record is dropped (its defeat lives in the game's flags).
- Cleared by a rest of ours (own or mirrored, `restsync::last_rest`) and by a wipe (both players dead within 90 s: our HP, the friend's snapshot HP or EV_DEATH; or the boss wipe release); the friend's messages are ignored for 13 s after a clear.
- Log: `dead-set: E killed on load (died earlier this session; not paid here yet: our own loot once | already paid here: no loot)`, `persist: boss E restored hp X lives Y after a death reload (record ours | from the friend, ok = B)`, `persist: rest | wipe (...): dead set (N), paid kills (R) and boss records (B) cleared`, every 30 s `persist (30 s): dead set N (died here a, from the friend b), killed on load k (l with our loot), paid kills r, boss records B (restored m)`.
- Live test: (a) kill 5 enemies, walk to another block and back: they stay dead (`dead-set: ... killed on load` only where the game had brought them back). (b) The friend fights a boss alone, takes a life, dies: after its reload `persist: boss ... restored hp .. lives 1 ...`, the boss shows one orb less and the lower HP. (c) Rest: `persist: rest: ... cleared`, everything respawns. (d) `boss_persist_on_death = 0`: (b) gives a full boss.
- Risks: an enemy the game revives by script without a rest (if any) is killed again on load; ForceDead on a chr loaded 3 s ago is untested; a boss record's HP is clamped to a changed max (scaling), not rescaled; a boss that heals by design is never healed in the record.

### 4. Enemies running in place / ownerless, block churn

**Root cause [VAR from the logs + IDA o10].** Friend: `ownerless (10 s): 200 alive enemies owned by nobody, 3 of them within 40 m ... 1100237 ...
