NetAI interval Some(-1), 7 m from us` (and 5-7 such enemies at 14-38 m in other lines). The game gives ownership only from the candidate lists (22, built by `0xA4E520` from enemies updated here) and the host's distribution (23); an enemy that is NetAI here is not updated here (interval -1), so one nobody listed when the engagement started never becomes a candidate: NetAI without records on both machines = animation without movement. The host owned only 2-6 enemies (`owners: Com 10 / NetAI 222 / owned-here 2`).

**Fix (`nsession::ownfill`).** A detour on `0xA4A650` IsOwnedHere(WCM, netId) (prologue `48 83 EC 28 48 8B 89 30 06 00 00`; used by EnemyIns slot 37 `0xA14B50` to pick ComManipulator / NetAI, by the sender `0xA1C100` for 4/70/71 and by the damage router `0xB6A040` / `0xB6F4C0`) answers "owned here" for netIds in a bitmap. Every 250 ms while engaged (stage >= 2; the client after its first 23): an alive enemy the game owns nowhere (not owned here by the game, not in the friend's Owned list) within 40 m of us is taken by the nearer player (host on a tie within 5 m; no friend position: the host); it runs our AI, its state goes out as for any enemy we own, and it becomes our candidate for the game's next distribution. Given back when the game gives it to us, the friend lists it (client at once, host after 3 s), it dies / unloads, or the rule turns clearly (hysteresis: the host up to 10 m farther, the client while nearer, 50 m max). Cleared on disengage. The hook goes in with the other nsession hooks (title screen); not fatal if it fails.

**Churn.** In Idle no handshake starts while the friend's snapshot is in another block (live 7.4: generations 4 -> 12 in 5 min, each handshake timing out on "blocks differ"); engaged, the friend's block must differ for 3 s before `friend in another block` disengages (our own block change still disengages at once).

**Log lines.** `ownfill: enemy E netId 0x.. owned by nobody, D m from us, d m from the friend (interval i): runs here now`, `ownfill: enemy E netId 0x.. given back after T ms (the game gives it to us now | the friend owns it | the friend is nearer / we are far | dead or unloaded)` (first 30), every 10 s with the ownerless line `nsession: ownfill (engagement): N ownerless enemies run here now, taken T, given back G`, `nsession: the friend is in another block (ours .., friend ..): no handshake until we share one`, `friend in another block (ours .., friend .., for T ms)`.

**Live test.** Walk into a group the session did not list at engage: `ownfill: ... runs here now` within ~0.5 s, the enemies move and attack on both screens; the `ownerless` line shows `0 of them within 40 m`; a backstab works only on a really unaware enemy. Go to another block for a minute: one `no handshake until we share one` line, no new generations until back.

**Risks.** Two AIs for up to ~2 s when both machines take the same enemy in a race (the Owned list period); the game's own 23 may move it right after (normal). A taken enemy that stays dormant on our side too (interval -1 with Com) may still stand still until the game's LOD wakes it [ARV].

## 32. Auditor: what one player got and the other did not (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Owner request: find, without the owner noticing it first, every case where the friend gets something and the host does not (live 7.4-7.6: Gyoubu's boss drop and the Shinobi Esoteric Text missed on the host, a one-time enemy drop (Herb Catalogue Scrap) reaching the host only on a re-kill, a miniboss below half HP, flag 9100 ping-pong). Two parts: an in-mod auditor that only observes, logs and reports (no item give, no flag write, no character change), and an offline analyzer of both logs on the host PC. Code: `audit.rs` (new); one-line hooks in `puppet.rs` (`audit::pump` after the net frame, `audit::tick` after persist), `lib.rs` (mod line), `settings.rs` (two keys), `world.rs` (two `flag_write` calls), `keyitems.rs` (sent / received / resolved of script grants and boss drops), `bossrules.rs` (`note_award` in `award_detour`, sent / received / resolved of boss rewards), `persist.rs` (read-only `boss_record`), `net.rs` (no "bulk transfer kind 60 received" line for our 2 Hz samples). New crate `crates\coop-audit` (bin + lib). Scripts: `build-dist.cmd` builds both, `test.cmd` tests coop-audit too, `release.cmd` copies `coop-audit.exe` to `dist\tools\` (host only, never in the zip), `serve.ps1` runs it after each friend log upload.

**Protocol.** coop-net unchanged (VERSION 9), nsession `PROTO` unchanged. One new bulk kind **60** (`audit::BULK_AUDIT`, body `{u8 wire 1, u8 msg, ...}`; messages 1 digest, 2 detail request, 3 detail, 4 boss HP samples, 5 reward acks). Safe with a v7.6 peer: an unknown bulk kind is logged once (`net: bulk transfer kind 60 received`) and stays unread in its 24-entry inbox (oldest dropped first); our side sends a v7.6 friend exactly one digest per connection (the hello) and nothing else once 90 s pass without an answer (`audit: no digest from the friend within 90 s ...`). So no version bump.

**1. Digest** (`Digest`, `build_digest`). Inventory: every key-item and normal-item slot (EquipInventoryData read raw, offsets from the vendor structs; a unit test checks `PlayerGameData.equip_game_data == 0x278`, the FindItem constant), (full id, count). Skills: SkillParam `unlock_event_flag` on. Lot flags: every ItemLotParam get flag (slot flags and the row flag) that is on, with the lot's source as this side knows it. Shared flags: one FNV checksum per shared record over the ids that are on and pass `world::should_sync` (item / skill / tutorial flags, 9500-9599, 9100/9110 left out). Reward counters (3 pipes x sent / received / resolved / not given). Sent 45 s after a connection (hello), 25 s after a rest (`restsync::last_rest`), 30 s after a boss / miniboss died here, every `audit_every_s` (host; the client 15 s later), and 60 s (client 75 s) after a comparison that left differences pending; the receiver compares the friend's digest with its own state of that moment and answers with it (`reply`), so both logs get the comparison; a round of the friend's covers one of ours due within 30 s. At most one digest per 15 s.

**2. Classification** (`compare`, `lot_class`; a difference must last 45 s (`Tracker`) before it is reported, each key once per game run). Lot source, most specific of: NpcParam `item_lot_id_1` (+44) rows L..L+7 = enemy drop (deathblows >= 2: boss / miniboss drop); AwardItemLot seen at run time (`note_award`; in the 15 s boss window or our replay of the friend's boss reward = boss reward, else NPC talk / event gift), remembered across game starts in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\sekiro-coop\audit-lots.txt`; rows < 1 000 000 except 61400 / 63610 / 63660 = script lot (§33's rule). A lot flag on one side only: map pickup / unknown = expected (audit.txt only); unknown with a key item = info; enemy drop = SUSPECT sev 2 ("or it still lies at the corpse"); boss drop, script gift, boss reward = SUSPECT sev 3. Items one side owns and the other not, not explained by a lot difference: key items (weapons / tools, non-consumable goods max 1) SUSPECT sev 2, non-consumable stackables info, consumables never. Skills on one side only: SUSPECT sev 1 (each player buys their own; a bug when the other could not). Reward counters: we sent more than the friend received: SUSPECT sev 3. Shared records with different checksums (at most 64; more = one SUSPECT "worlds far apart"): the friend is asked for the ids of up to 16 of them and every differing flag is reported (sev 2; 7xxxxxxx NPC talk flags sev 1, npcintro.rs holds some on purpose).

**3. Boss / miniboss HP** (`boss_scan` every 0.5 s; bosses = NpcParam deathblows >= 2 or the gauge entity). While a boss is within 60 m of us, the friend is in our block (snapshot not hidden) and has the auditor, and the bulk queue is idle: HP / max / posture / max posture / lives at 2 Hz (`MSG_HP`, at most 8). SUSPECT when HP shares differ by > 10 % for 3 s (sev 3), lives differ for 3 s (sev 3), posture shares by > 30 % for 5 s (sev 1); when a boss is first seen 6 s after a load, or its gauge appears, below 99 % of its max with no damage seen this rest cycle (our HP drops after 6 s in the world, the friend's samples below max): sev 3 without a persist record, sev 2 with one (a record from a fight while apart, or a wrong record: §35's "restored hp 1").

**4. Reward pipeline** (`Rewards`, `run_checks`). Sender: every script grant (event id), boss drop (entity, lot) and boss reward lot (boss, lot) we send. Receiver: acks receipt (`ST_RECEIVED`), and 3 s after resolving (90 s for a boss drop: it may lie at the corpse) checks the lot flags (for boss drops: the flags of the lot's sure rows) are on, or every non-co-op item of the off flags' lots is owned (consumables count: Herb Catalogue Scrap is one); not = SUSPECT sev 3 here and `ST_NOT_OK` to the sender (SUSPECT there too). Sender: not resolved 30 s after the friend's receipt (90 s after sending without one) = SUSPECT sev 3 ("a menu or a load delays it"); resolved later = info. Only for grants sent while the friend's auditor was known.

**5. Flag ping-pong** (`PingPong`, from world.rs: our sends and the friend's flags as they arrive): the same shared flag in 3 alternating runs (us / friend / us) within 60 s = SUSPECT sev 3 "per-player flag?" (sev 2 for 7xxxxxxx NPC talk flags). This is exactly what 9100 did in 7.5.

**6. Output.** Every SUSPECT once as `audit: SUSPECT (sev N) text`, info as `audit: info: text` in log.txt; `%LOCALAPPDATA%\sekiro-coop\audit.txt` rewritten (at most every 2 s when something changed): header (role, friend, totals), the latest comparison line, SUSPECT newest first, then info / expected. Other lines: `audit: index built: ...`, `audit: on for this connection ...`, `audit: sent digest #N (reason, B bytes, ok = true): ...`, `audit: comparison #N with the friend's digest #M (...): ... K difference(s) pending, R new report(s)`, `audit: shared flag detail of N record(s): ...`, `audit: boss / miniboss E died here, digest in 30 s`.

**Settings.** `[DEBUG] audit = 1` (default on while this is a test build; 0 = off, the bulk kind is drained and dropped) and `audit_every_s = 180` (30..3600).

**7. Offline analyzer** (`crates\coop-audit`, `coop-audit <host log> <friend log> [-o report.txt] [--all] [--offset S]`, no dependencies). Host lines outside the friend log's span +-10 min are ignored (`--all` keeps them). Friend minus host clock is the median of matching flag lines (`world: sent flag` on one side, `world: friend set flag` on the other). Findings, ranked 5 critical .. 1 info, each with side, UTC time and `side:line`: CRASH? (with the regs line) and panics (5); `audit: SUSPECT` (sev + 1, deduped with a count); reward hand-overs matched across the logs: `sent our script lot / sent queued grant lot ... (event E)` against the other side's `arrived (event E` and `gave ... (event E)` / `friend's lot L already ... / gave nothing new / dropped` / `the friend acked lots ... (events [...])`, `told the friend about the drop of E (lot L` against `the friend's game dropped lot L for E` and `the friend's drop of E` / `gave the friend's boss drop of E`, `sent N lots for boss B` against `reward: received` within 2 min and a `gave lot` / `already owned` / `already handled` / `not given` within 10 min (never arrived / arrived and never given = 4, dropped = 3; only when the other log covers the time); version (`an older build's handshake` 4, `testsync: host has build ... run play.cmd` 3, `... asking for it` 2, password rejected 3); flag ping-pong in the world.rs lines (4, NPC flags 3) and flapping (one side 4 changes in 60 s, 2); `key items: ... we miss [...]` per item (3); `lost` / `given up` / `dropped` / `gaps skipped` / `too big` counters above 0, one per module and counter with the largest value (`world lost`, `given up` 3, `dropped old` 1, others 2); disconnects (2); fps 6..19 for 10 s or more (2; loading screens at <= 5 fps are not counted); `persist: boss ... restored hp` (1; at hp <= 1: 3); `audit: info` (1). The report is Finnish + English headings, at most 25 lines per (severity, category). Run on the real 7.6 logs it found the 9110 ping-pong of 2026-10-23 (friend log line 17368, before v7.6 made 9100 / 9110 local), the missing Shinobi Esoteric Text, NPC talk flags 71100127 / 71100128 ping-ponging (host 33199-33305) and §35's `restored hp 1`.

**serve.ps1.** After a friend's log upload is saved and answered 200 OK (connection closed), `Invoke-Audit` runs the first of `dist\tools\coop-audit.exe`, `target-dist\release\coop-audit.exe`, `target\release\coop-audit.exe` against `%LOCALAPPDATA%\sekiro-coop\log.txt` and the upload, writing `friend-uploads\report-<name>-<time>.txt` (newest 30 kept). Missing analyzer, missing host log or any error: nothing, the upload is not affected.

**How to read.** In the game: audit.txt (SUSPECT first). After a session: the newest `friend-uploads\report-*.txt`; start at the top (5 / 4), each line names the log and line number to open. A SUSPECT is a lead, not a proof: check the lines around it in both logs.

**Live test (both PCs on this build).** 1. Connect: both logs `audit: on for this connection`, ~45 s later `audit: sent digest #1 (hello ...)` and `audit: the friend runs the auditor too`, then `audit: comparison #1 ...` on both, audit.txt appears. 2. One player picks up a map item: after the next round `expected:` in audit.txt only. 3. Stand near a miniboss together: no `HP differs` while both screens agree; take a life on one screen only (if it ever happens) gives a SUSPECT within ~3 s. 4. The friend talks to an NPC who gives a key item while the host is in another block: if the grant does not arrive, `SUSPECT (sev 3) the friend has lot flag ...` on the host about a minute after the next round. 5. Friend on v7.6: one `net: bulk transfer kind 60 received` line there, `audit: no digest from the friend within 90 s` here, nothing else. 6. Upload the friend log: `report-<name>-<time>.txt` next to it.

**What it cannot detect.** Items both players lack (nothing to compare); a lot whose source neither side saw run and that is not below 1 000 000 nor an NpcParam lot (reported as an expected map pickup); stackable count differences (Prayer Beads 3 vs 2) and consumables owned on one side without a lot difference; anything while the two are not connected, or before the hello (first 45 s), or while one side is on a loading screen; HP of bosses not loaded on both machines or more than 60 m away; posture under 30 % / 5 s, short (< 3 s) HP disagreements; per-player flags that both games write to the same value (no traffic); flags held on purpose (bossgate, npcintro) show up as differences after 45 s; the analyzer only sees what is logged (world.rs flag lines are rate-limited: first 30, then every 100th), and the friend's clock is estimated. The auditor never repairs anything: §33 (reconcile) does that, and the auditor then shows whether it worked.

**Risks.** Digest building reads ~5000 lot flags and every shared record on the game thread (a few ms, at most every 15 s; a failed read retries in 5 s). The inventory layout comes from the vendor crate (checked against one known offset only); a wrong layout gives empty or odd item lists, never a write. The 2 Hz HP samples share the reliable bulk queue (sent only when it is idle). Classification heuristics are [I]: a boss drop lot that is also referenced by a non-boss NpcParam is labelled by the higher category.

## 33. Reward reconcile: pending grants, lot ledger, boss-lot doubles (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Live findings (7.6 logs): (1) `keyitems::outgoing` dropped a captured one-time grant when nobody was connected: the friend got Herb Catalogue Scrap (lot 13604000) 62 s after the host left, the host only got it by killing that enemy itself. (2) The key-item check only indexes goods not consumed on use with max 1, and its one repair needs an acquisition flag (`0 with an acquisition flag`), so materials / memos (Mechanical Barrel, Flame Barrel Memo lot 61700, Herb Scrap) were never reconciled; the friend got the Flame Barrel Memo while its old settings had `share_key_items = 0`, the host never did. (3) Boss reward double: `apply_reward` gave lot 2499 (Regenerative Power x2, no lot flags) although the friend's own world had already given it. Code: `keyitems.rs`, `bossrules.rs` (`apply_reward` only). Protocol unchanged; two new bulk kinds 44 / 45.

**1. Pending queue (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\sekiro-coop\pending_grants.txt`).** Every captured one-time grant (a lot that switched on per-player lot flags, after the sharing rule of §23) is added to the queue and appended to the file (one line `event-id lot complete fullId:num:x:popup,... flag,...`; a lot already queued is not added again; at most 128, oldest dropped). It is sent at once when connected, else on the next connect (in the world 10 s, connected 5 s), and again on every later connect until the friend acks it: `BULK_GRANT_ACK` (44) `{u8 1, u16 n, n x u32 event id}`, sent by the receiver for every grant it decided (given, already ours, nothing new, dropped by `share_key_items = 0`, or a repeat); "item manager not readable" is not acked. Acked lines are removed (file rewritten through a temp file; deleted when empty). Before a queued grant goes out it must still be ours: its lot flags on in this save, every entry an item of rows `lot`..`lot+7` of our own ItemLotParam, every flag one of those rows' flags; otherwise it is kept and not sent (another character loaded). So the file cannot be used to give arbitrary items: only lots the game really gave us. Repeatable lots (no lot flag) are never queued: the receiver could not tell a repeat from a new grant. The receiver's `apply` (event-id set, lot flags, `owned_check`) keeps a resend from giving twice.

**2. Lot ledger.** Each side sends `BULK_LOT_LEDGER` (45) `{u8 1, u8 reason (0 connect, 1 rest), u16 n, n x i32 row}` = the reconcile rows whose lot flag is on here: on every connect (in the world 10 s, connected 5 s) and 5 s after every rest (ours or the friend's mirrored here, `restsync::last_rest`). 8 s after the friend's ledger arrived (queued grants land first) and when `safe_for_reward`, every row that is on there, a reconcile row in our own table, and off here is given once: its sure goods through GrantItems (`grant_now`, popup 0 = the small pickup log, at most 16 entries per call, at most 64 rows per pass, the rest with the next ledger) and its flag set here. A unique item (not goods or `max_num <= 1`) we own already only gets the flag set. Rows sharing a flag (one gift, several lots: Young Lord's Bell Charm 51600 / 61600, flag 50006160) are given once. Every flag must be readable first (unreadable = wait), so "off" never means "unknown". Owner decision: lots the friend earned outside a session count too (they always play together), so past misses (Flame Barrel Memo) come over on the next connect.
- **Classification** (`classify_lot`; `build_lot_index` reads the params once). Row flag = the sure slot's `get_item_flag_idNN`, else the row's `get_item_flag_id` (the flag `sure_goods_of_lot` already used). No flag: repeatable, never. A flag a ShopLineupParam row also uses: shop, never. A row named by an NpcParam `item_lot_id_1..6` (or a row after it while rows exist, up to 8: one lot reads rows L, L+1..): enemy drop, yes (13604000 Herb Catalogue Scrap, 10200000 Prayer Bead, 13200xxx Treasure Carp). An id below 1 000 000 that is not 61400 / 63610 / 63660: script / talk / boss lot (EMEVD 2003[4], ESD 104, boss `0x688360`: 2000 Mechanical Barrel, 2010 Slender Finger, 51600 / 61700 talk / NPC-death lots), yes. Everything else (map lots `AAxxxxx` per map from 1 000 000 up: treasure, chests, corpses; 8-digit lots no NpcParam names): map treasure, never; each player picks up their own (§23, world.rs item flags). Source of the ranges: thefifthmatt's randomizer `annotations.txt` (529 treasure lots: 506 seven-digit, 22 eight-digit enemy-model "treasure" (Treasure Carp, NpcParam drops anyway), the 3 five-digit ones above; 217 enemy / NPC lots: 4-, 5- and 8-digit; 14 scripted: 4-digit, 62200 Mortal Blade, 2000060 Illusive Hall Bell (7-digit, so NOT reconciled; the live grant path still shares it), 50500000 Great White Whisker (also a treasure, not reconciled)). Then only rows with one sure goods slot (one slot carries all of the weight; goods by category 3 / 4 / `0x4000_0000` or a named goods row, as for §23 boss drops), never our co-op goods (`items::is_coop_goods`: Kindred Bell / Charm / Severed Thread), and the §23 sharing rule (`shareable(.., one_time = true)`). Weapons (Mortal Blade) and lotteries are left out.

**3. Boss-lot doubles (fix 3) and "flags on" (fix 4).** `local_award_age(lot, window)` is `pub(crate)`; our own AwardItemLot calls (that gave something, never our replays) are kept 10 min (was 120 s; 128 entries). `bossrules::apply_reward` asks `keyitems::boss_lot_check` per lot: our own award of that lot within 10 min = ours, skipped (lot 2499 has no flags, so this is the only way to see it); flags on = ours only if we own every unique sure goods item of the lot, else only the missing unique items are given (dialog popup) and the lottery is not run again; flags off = AwardItemLot as before. After our AwardItemLot of a friend's boss lot, `note_replayed(lot)`: our own world's award of the same lot in the next 120 s (its copy of the kill) is skipped. The same rule in `apply` for script grants (`owned_check`): our own award within 120 s, or flags on with every unique entry owned = already received; flags on with a unique entry missing = only the missing unique entries (stackables are not given again).

**Older peer.** A v7.6 peer keeps bulk kinds 44 / 45 unread in its inbox (cleared on every new link, at most 24 kept, oldest dropped first) and otherwise ignores them; it still applies a resent `BULK_ITEM_GRANT` with its own lot-flag check, but never acks, so our queue resends on every connect (harmless: `already received here`). Its own captured grants are not queued and it sends no ledger. Both PCs need this build for the full effect.

**Settings.** `[GAMEPLAY] share_key_items` (default 1) governs all of it: 0 = nothing sent (the queue stays in the file), no ledger sent, the friend's ledger ignored and its grants dropped (acked). A friend whose old settings file has `share_key_items = 0` must set it to 1, or its memos never come over.

**Log lines.** `reward: script lot L [...] not sent (no friend connected): queued in pending_grants.txt (event E), sent on the next connect`, `reward: N queued grant(s) in pending_grants.txt for the friend: lots [...]`, `reward: sent queued grant lot L to the friend (event E, ok = true): [...], lot flags [...]`, `reward: queued grant lot L (event E) not sent: ...; kept`, `reward: acked the friend's events [...]`, `reward: the friend acked lots [...] (events [...]), removed from pending_grants.txt (N left, saved ok)`, `reconcile: N lot rows can be reconciled (S script / boss, D enemy drop; ...); left per player: T map treasure, H shop, R repeatable (no lot flag), X lottery / not goods, C co-op; NpcParam read (K lots named)`, `reconcile: sent our ledger (N of M one-time lots on here, after connecting | a rest, ok = true)`, `reconcile: the friend's ledger arrived (N one-time lots on there, after ...)`, `reconcile: got lot L [goods ... 'Flame Barrel Memo' x1 (...)] that the friend has, lot flag F set (true)`, `reconcile: lot L [...] that the friend has: already owned here, lot flag F set`, `reconcile: friend's ledger done: N lots on there, G off here and given / marked, U unknown or per player here`, `reward: lot L for boss B not given: our own world gave it N s ago` / `...: lot flags on here and its unique items owned`, `reward: lot L for boss B: lot flags [...] on here but we lacked [...]: gave only those`, `reward: friend's lot L already received here (lot flags [...] on, its unique items owned)`, `reward: friend's lot L has its lot flags [...] on here but we lack some of its unique items: giving only those`; the 60 s stats line ends `queued for the friend N, reconciled here R`.

**Live test (both PCs on this build, the friend's `share_key_items = 1`).**
1. Connect: both logs `reconcile: N lot rows can be reconciled ...` and `sent our ledger`; the host `reconcile: got lot 61700 [goods ... 'Flame Barrel Memo' ...] that the friend has` (and Herb Catalogue Scrap / other past misses); a second connect gives nothing new (`0 off here`).
2. Friend alone (host quit): the friend kills an enemy that drops a one-time item or gets a talk gift: `not sent (no friend connected): queued in pending_grants.txt`. Host joins: `sent queued grant lot L`, host `gave the friend's script lot L`, friend `the friend acked lots [L]`; `pending_grants.txt` is gone.
3. Kill a boss together whose reward has a flagless lot (Regenerative Power, lot 2499): the receiver logs `reward: lot 2499 for boss B not given: our own world gave it N s ago` when its own world awarded it; one copy only.
4. Rest: 5 s later `sent our ledger (..., after a rest ...)` on both.

**Risks.** The id-range classification comes from the randomizer data, not from the game files [I]: a 4- or 5-digit treasure we do not know would be shared (one extra pickup; its flag then hides it here); a 7-digit script lot (Illusive Hall Bell) missed outside a session is not recovered. A lot flag that is also a quest flag is now set here when the friend has it (our own dialogue follows "already given"). Reconcile items show the small pickup log, not a dialog. The ledger carries every reconcile row whose flag is on (a few hundred i32, one bulk). That our own AwardItemLot gives nothing for a row whose lot flag is on is the game's lot-flag rule as §23 already assumes [I].

## 34. Deathblows on an enemy both players fight: contested claims, colliding throws (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Owner report (live 7.6 = 45d0c92, 2026-10-24 ~06:35-06:41): the host "couldn't deathblow properly" on the miniboss 1120300 (netId 0x182e, 2 lives). Host `log.txt` [1792813100..813260], friend `friend-log.txt` (clock ~6 s ahead). Code: `nsession.rs` (throw ring, `serve`, claims, `host_candidates` / `contest`, MaxHp message), `livesync.rs` (rest baseline). `PROTO` unchanged (4); the new MaxHp message (MSG 7) is dropped by a 7.6 friend as undecodable, which is harmless.

**Causes (log evidence).**
1. *The friend always won the enemy.* Both players were near it the whole fight (host `ownerless` line at 813150: 13 m from us, 8 m from the friend, "claimed by both machines"), so "behind" held for whichever player the miniboss did not face. The friend's claim kept being renewed (friend 813163..813262: 12 claims, every one `owned here`). Of the host's 10 claims, 8 ended `never owned here`, because when both claim, `strip_22` kept the friend's prio-0xFFFF entry and the game's rule (higher prio wins) gave it to the friend. The host's copy was NetAI for the whole fight. On a NetAI copy the deathblow window comes from the owner's sparse behaviour records, and NetAI keeps moving the defender during a throw (RE-BACKSTAB §b.3/b.4). The host started a throw only twice in ~100 s (`types` 18: tx 2 at 813220-230, tx 4 at 813240-250).
2. *Two deathblows at once cancelled each other.* At host 813226 the friend's throw 1200 on 0x182e arrived (`evidence rx type 18 ... 2e 18 01 a0 b0 04`) and the host's own followed within the second (`tx ... 2e 18 f0 ff b0 04`), then both 1205. At 813244 the same happened in the other order. Both packets in each pair carry a bit-identical defender angle (`b2 42 71 bf`, `9a 5a 39 c0`), so the second throw started on the defender just placed by the first. A received 18 puts the defender into the remote player's throw, whose hit is reaction-only on the receiver, and cuts off the local player's running throw. After the first pair no copy lost a life: livesync showed `1 enemies short of lives, 0 dead` on both before and after. In the second pair the host's throw completed on its NetAI copy (host `loot: 1120300 died without our kill` at 813252). The friend's copy kept its life (`the friend has lives 0 (dead), ours 1; a native throw on it applies here first`) until livesync ForceDead it 2 s later.
3. *Lives that did not reset after the rest* (coordinator report, the same enemy): the friend's copy took the host's pre-respawn lives during the 13 s of our own sync after the rest (friend `posture: 1120300 lives 2 -> 1 (host)` at 813124, fixed in posture.rs by §35). Then the 813130 engagement lowered the host's fresh copy from it (`lives 2 -> 1 (the friend's deathblow)`).
4. *Engage HP check across different max HP* (coordinator report): `hp_adoptions` compared raw HP while scaling had not been applied on one side yet (1120353: 558 on the host vs max 349 on the friend).

**Fixes.**
- *Colliding throws* (`serve`): a received type 18 is not given to the game when the friend's player is its attacker and our own player started a throw on the same enemy within 3 s (`throw_collides`). Each player's deathblow then runs on their own machine, and livesync merges the lives by minimum (never two lives for one deathblow). Dropped throws are not noted in the throw ring.
- *A deathblow claims its enemy.* Our player's throw on an enemy (our 18, attacker = our player) claims it for 4 s, on top of the stealth claims (`claims_now`). The client sends it as prio 0xFFFF; the host strips the friend's entry.
- *Contested claims are settled by the host* (`host_wins_contest`, every 100 ms while both claim one enemy). A player's deathblow comes first; when both players threw, the earlier throw wins, and the host wins a tie. Without a throw, the nearer player wins (horizontal distance, 1.5 m in favour of the current holder). `strip_22` removes the friend's 0xFFFF entry when the host wins.
- *Faster ownership moves.* A new claim makes the client send 22 on its next update (`T+0x3A4 = 0`). The host settles the friend's last 22 again at once (stored `last22`) and distributes 23 on its next update (`T+0x39C = 0`), also when the friend's 22 brings a new claim. Before this, a claim needed up to 5 s (22) + 3 s (23).
- *Rest baseline* (`livesync.rs`): when an engagement starts within 30 s of our rest, the friend's lives in its first states (the first 3.5 s) are a baseline. Only fewer lives than that count as a deathblow (`rest_baseline_blocks`). A deathblow made during the engagement still applies at once.
- *Engage HP check* (`hp_adoptions`): each side sends `MaxHp {entity, max HP}` after its List. A lower HP is taken only if our max is known, neither HP is above our max, and both maxes agree within 1 %. With a 7.6 friend (no MaxHp) only the "above our max" guard applies.
- Unit tests: `two_deathblows_at_once_do_not_cancel_each_other` (live bytes), `contested_claims_go_to_the_deathblow_then_the_nearer_player`, `stealth_claims_raise_our_prio_and_strip_the_friends` (won case), `engage_hp_check_skips_enemies_whose_max_differs`, `messages_roundtrip...` (MaxHp), `livesync::stale_lives_after_a_rest_are_a_baseline`.

**Log lines.** `nsession: the friend's throw T on enemy netId 0x.. not applied here: our own player's throw on it started N ms ago (two deathblows at once cancel each other)`, `stealth: our player's throw T on enemy netId 0x..: claimed for 4 s (the deathblow runs on our AI)`, host `stealth: both claim enemy E netId 0x..: the host|the friend runs it (our deathblow N ms ago | the friend's deathblow ... | both deathblowed it ... | us D m, the friend D m)`, `nsession: host: our claims changed: the friend's last candidates (N) settled again`. Every 10 s: `nsession: stealth claims (10 s): ours now N; ... contests kept W / given to the friend L; our deathblows claimed T, the friend's throws on them not applied X`. livesync: `this engagement follows a rest: the friend's first lives states are a baseline, not deathblows` and `enemy E netId 0x..: the friend's lives L in its first state after a rest (ours l): not taken`. Engage: `HP check on engage: K of R lower friend HPs not taken (max HP differs or HP above max ...)`.

**Live test.** (1) Fight a 2-life miniboss together; the host breaks its posture and deathblows it: host `stealth: our player's throw ... claimed for 4 s` (and, if the friend claimed it too, `both claim ... the host runs it (our deathblow ...)`), one orb gone on both screens (friend `livesync: ... lives 2 -> 1`). (2) Both press deathblow at once: one `the friend's throw ... not applied here` on each side, exactly one life gone on both. (3) Both stand behind it: one `both claim ... (us D m, the friend D m)` line per change, not a new owner every second. (4) Rest after taking a life: every orb back on both screens, the host logs `the friend's lives 1 in its first state after a rest ... not taken` if the friend's copy was stale. (5) Watch whether the host's deathblow marker appears while the FRIEND owns the enemy. If the host still cannot start a posture deathblow there (no `our player's throw` line while the bar is broken), the next step is a posture-broken claim like mode 4's `posture` reason.

**Risks.** A throw claim moves ownership mid-throw, so for a moment the other machine's copy turns NetAI under the remote's animation (cosmetic). The nearer-player rule can move a contested enemy between the AIs when the players' distances cross by more than 1.5 m (before this, the friend's claims moved it about as often). Dropping a colliding throw hides the friend's deathblow animation on our screen for that enemy; the friend's player anim (hkbsync) still plays. That a received throw cuts off a running local throw is inferred from the two log pairs [ARV]; the throw ids 1200/1205 are taken as the two stages of one deathblow [ARV]. Re-feeding the friend's last 22 hands the game a list up to 5 s old (the same one it already holds, minus our claims).

## 35. Boss records, rest window and scaling: the miniboss that came back damaged (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Owner report (live 7.5/7.6, 2026-10-24 ~06:30-06:40): the miniboss 1120300 (netId 0x182e, 2 lives, host max 5373 = base 2149 x 2.5) had less than half HP when it should have been full, and after a rest its lives orbs did not reset. Host `log.txt` ~33100-36300, friend `friend-log.txt` (friend clock ~6 s ahead).

**Causes (log evidence).**
1. *Get-up HP became the record.* Friend stealth deathblow (host 1792812752 `lives 2 -> 1 ... at 1 HP right after its get-up ... HP full again`): persist recorded the 1 HP moment; the friend's lone death reload restored `hp 1 lives 1` (friend 857).
2. *Wipe latch.* `wipe (both players died)` fired once (host 935: friend died 846, we 935); the `wiped` latch blocked the real wipe (we 949, friend 953). The still-loaded boss was re-recorded at 1544 / 1 life and restored on both reloads (host 954, friend 964) although `death: both died (wipe): no restore` (959).
3. *Scaling on one machine only.* The host scaled from enemies.rs `known` (45 s rescan, 166 of 224 characters until 1102): 1120300 stayed at max 2149 on the host for minutes while the friend's copy was scaled; when scaling finally applied the game healed it by the growth (1519 + 3224 = 4743, the host's HP at 1117). Records compared raw HP across different maxes.
4. *Rest.* Both cleared their records, but in the client's first seconds after its rest the host's pre-respawn state was mirrored onto the fresh copy (`posture: 1120300 lives 2 -> 1 (host)`, `hp clamp 1120300 host 4743 max Some(2149)`), and at the 1130 engagement livesync lowered the host's fresh copy from it (`lives 2 -> 1 (the friend's deathblow)`). Persist also re-recorded the host's pre-respawn boss (1 life, 4743) within a second of the rest clear.

**Fixes.**
- `persist.rs`: `BossRec::lower` compares HP as a share of each record's max; `restore_plan` puts the record's share onto the copy's max (was a clamp); `observed` + `GETUP_GRACE` (6 s): after a lost life (seen here or below the record) the HP counts as full; `wipe_pair`: deaths are stamped on alive -> 0 and every new death pair clears again (no latch); a wipe while we are dead freezes recording, sending and the friend's messages until our reload (`hold_load`, also `note_wipe`); `frozen()`: nothing is recorded for 13 s after any clear (the respawn may come later). 3 new unit tests.
- `posture.rs` (client lives mirror): no lowering of lives from the host while `restsync::hold_kills()` (12 s after our rest).
- `enemies.rs` (client HP authority): the rest hold also covers a full copy here with a lower HP on the host (not only host HP 0).
- `puppet.rs` (scaling): every loaded enemy (`nsession::enemy_chrs()`, entity id > 0) on both roles, once per second; `loaded_enemies` only as a fallback when that list is empty. `scaling.rs` stays idempotent per (chr, entity) and resets on loads.
- Proposed to the owners of the files: livesync.rs (no `Plan::Lower` ~30 s after a rest; the engagement starts after the 12 s window), nsession.rs `hp_adoptions` (compare shares of max, not raw HP).

**Log lines.** `rest: enemy E respawned here, HP X (from before its respawn?) on the host: not lowered (waiting for the host's respawn)`, `persist: reloaded after the wipe: records start over from the fresh world`.

**Live test.** (a) Stealth-deathblow a miniboss, die alone: after the reload `restored hp <max> lives 1`, not hp 1. (b) Both die: no `persist: boss ... restored` after the reload, the boss is full with every life. (c) Rest after taking a life: both screens show every orb and full HP; no `lives 2 -> 1 (host)` on the client and no `(the friend's deathblow)` on the host right after the engagement. (d) Host T lines: a miniboss's HP is the scaled max within ~1 s of loading (no 2149-type values once linked), and no `hp clamp` on the friend for it.

**Risks.** A real hit by the host in the 12 s after a rest is not mirrored onto the client until the window ends; a boss HP recorded while its max is still changing is scaled by share, which is right unless the game heals by the growth afterwards (now rare, since scaling covers every enemy); `enemy_chrs()` walks all chrs once per second (cheap, persist already does it every 250 ms).

## 36. Quit Game greyed out in co-op, with a "leave co-op first" label (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Owner request (like Elden Ring Seamless Co-op): while a companion is connected the pause menu's Quit Game is greyed out, cannot be chosen, and its label says why. Code: `quitblock.rs` only (two more detours, `should_grey`, `is_quit_command`, `quit_label`). Same switch as §27: `[GAMEPLAY] block_quit_in_coop = 1` (default 0; the owner's test ini has 0, so nothing changes until it is set). The §27 state-135 cancel stays as the safety net.

**IDA (copy `F:\Temp\ida_quit\db.i64`, jobs o1-o8).** [VAR] The quit item's job lambda (vtable `0x142AC7520`, `_Func_impl<lambda_f5822cd0...>`, `MenuJob(CommandSelectDialog&)`) is created only in `0x140CB5E30` (`AppMenu::CSCommonOptionUnit` ctor, "OptionTop"), `case 5` of its item loop; the label struct comes from `0x140CC64A0` / `0x140CC6440` = FDP_MenuText 110500 (help 110501 / 110500; the variant depends on `0x140C7DDA0(23)`). The item is an `AppMenu::CommandSelectDialog::Command` (ctor `0x140C5F7F0`): `+192` job std::function (impl pointer `+248`), `+392` std::function<bool()> (impl pointer `+448`), flags `+456` (ctor sets bit 2, the menu sets bit 4). The default predicate (vtable `0x142AA6500`, `_Do_call` `0x140C63670` = `32 C0 C3`, `xor al, al; ret`, only xref that vtable slot) is kept by the quit item; cases 6-8 install their own through `0x140CC71F0`. Render `0x140DC9820`: `0x140C53ED0(widget, pred() || !(flags & 2))` sets the widget state "Grayout" or "Normal". Select `0x140DCA850` (CommandSelectDialog vtable `0x142AC5AA8` slot 17): pred() true -> menu sound (600, 2, 41) via `0x140C7DEC0`, no command; false -> `0x140DCB580`. Label text: `0x140C7B910` -> `0x140C7CE90` -> `0x1410AA310(MsgRepository*, bank, id, default)` for banks 200, 232, 272 in turn, copied into the item at build time (`0x140C7DAA0`). [ARV] that the "OptionTop" unit is the in-game pause menu's system list (the f5822 lambda leads to the §27 quit flow, so it is the Quit Game item wherever it is shown); `+248` as the job's impl pointer follows the MSVC std::function layout (the ctor zeroes `+248` right before the next std::function at `+256`, whose impl pointer is `+312`).

**Fix.** (1) Detour on `0x140C63670` (5 relocated bytes: the 3 code bytes and 2 int3 of padding; the original is not called, it always returns false): returns true only when `should_grey` (option on, mod alive, HostConnected / ClientConnected) and the predicate is stored inline in a Command (`Command+448 == this`, `Command = this - 392`) whose job impl's vtable is `0x142AC7520`. So the item renders "Grayout" and pressing it only plays the menu's refusal sound. A press is recognised by the detour's return address inside `0x140DCA850` and gets the overlay notice "Use Severed Thread to leave co-op before quitting.". (2) Detour on `0x1410AA310` (prologue `40 53 48 83 EC 20 49 8B D9`), installed only if (1) is: for (bank 200/232/272, id 110500) while `should_grey`, the game's own text plus " (leave co-op first: Severed Thread)" (a leaked UTF-16 string, rebuilt only when the original pointer changes). Unit tests `quitblock::tests::greys_only_while_connected_with_the_option_on`, `recognises_only_the_quit_command`, `quit_label_text`.

**Log lines.** `hook: menu command disabled predicate (0xC63670) ... detoured`, `hook: menu text lookup (0x10AA310) ... detoured`, `quit: 2/2 grey-out hooks installed` (or `; Quit Game greys out in co-op but keeps its label` / `; Quit Game stays selectable in co-op (the return is still cancelled)`), once per session `quit: Quit Game label replaced while in co-op (link L)`, `quit: Quit Game shown greyed out while in co-op (link L)`, `quit: greyed-out Quit Game pressed while in co-op (link L): refused, notice shown`.

**Live test.** (1) `block_quit_in_coop = 1`. Start: `quit: 2/2 grey-out hooks installed`. (2) Connected (host, then client): open the pause menu's system list: Quit Game is grey and reads "Quit Game (leave co-op first: Severed Thread)"; press it: refusal sound, the overlay notice, `... pressed ... refused`, no yes/no box. (3) Severed Thread, close and reopen the menu: normal label, Quit Game works. (4) Solo / title screen: unchanged. (5) Report if the label changes but the item is not grey (then the render path differs), or if the grey state lags one menu opening behind the link state.

**Risks.** The label is copied when the menu is built: if that unit is built once and kept, the label (not the grey state, which is read at render and press time) can lag behind connecting / leaving until the menu is rebuilt. Whether render `0x140DC9820` runs per frame or only on rebuild is not known [ARV]; the press check in `0x140DCA850` is evaluated per press [VAR]. The text-lookup detour sits on a hot path for all menu texts (one compare per call when not the quit id). 110500 is also the title of the quit flow's yes/no box, which shows our label too if it is ever reached while connected (only through a path that skips the grey check).

## 37. Doors: WorldObjActMan walker re-derived, never-close guards (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

**Problem.** `doors.rs` never found a door: 32/32 live checks logged `doors: after the rest: 0 doors loaded`. So guard 2 (our ObjAct toggle switching a friend-opened door flag off) and the replay never ran. Live: a trapdoor the friend opened stayed closed for the host; the Hirata gate past Estate Path (m10_00, idol 1001951, candidate flag 21005024) ended closed and unopenable for the host after both opened it.

**Layout (IDA copy `F:\Temp\ida_doors`, jobs d1-d7).** [VAR] = read in the IDA output, [ARV] = inferred.
- `*(base+0x3D55EC8)` is the `SprjWorldObjActMan` singleton: `0x6B8400` / `0x810BB0` pass it straight to its update `0x6BE1B0` -> `0x6BE740` [VAR].
- Manager `+0x28` block count, `+0x30` inline array of 224-byte blocks [VAR]. Block `+0x00` descriptor (`+0x20` slot), `+0x10` loaded (`0x6BC0A0`: 1 after setup `0x6B9FE0`, 0 after unload `0x6BC8E0`), `+0x78` infos / `+0x80` count, 304 bytes each [VAR].
- Info (ctor `0x6B02C0`, setup `0x6B2430`): `+0x08` MyListIdx (= array index), `+0x50` ObjHandle (the argument of `0x899310`, not an entity id), `+0x54` run state, `+0x88` state, `+0xA9` state type, `+0xAC` sub index, `+0xB0` primary info [VAR]. A type-1 door has four infos with the same flag (sub 0..3) [VAR].
- State factory `0x6B64E0`: type 0 `OneState` (vt `0x298B570`, no flag: slot 16 is a nullsub), 1 `DoorState` (`0x298B628`), 2 `OneLoopState` (`0x298B6E0`), 4 `OneDirOpenDoorState` (`0x298B798`). Types 1, 2 and 4 keep the MSB EventFlagID at `state+0x10`. Slot 4 toggles it (`0x6B6470` / `0x6B64A0`) [VAR].
- The old offsets were right. The old walker only accepted types 1 and 4 and every sub info. Since it never found one, the doors seen live are most likely `OneState` ObjActs whose flag is set by EMEVD, not by the state [ARV]. The first scan now logs a count per state class to settle this. (Elden Ring tutorials show the same split: hand doors StateType 4, lever doors StateType 0 + common event 90005650, soulsmodding.com.)

**Change.**
- `scan(mem, base)` is pure over a `Mem` trait. It reads each info in one read and accepts types 1, 2 and 4, primary (sub 0) only. It returns a `Report` and remembers every walked door flag (`door_flags`).
- A walk runs once on the first settled tick after each load.
- Fallback door flags (`candidates`): a friend ON map flag that arrives 0-6 s after the friend's snapshot animation became an `ObjActParam.playerAnimId` (or up to 1.5 s before it).
- Guard 2 (`keep_open`) needs `friend_on` plus a known door flag (walked or candidate). It no longer re-walks per write.
- Guard 3 (`ignore_friend_off`, world.rs `apply_flag`): a friend's OFF for a known door flag that is ON here is dropped and ON is re-sent to the friend. This holds on both host and client.
- Both guards are off for 15 s after any rest. Non-door flags are untouched.

**Log lines.** Once: `doors: scan: WorldObjActMan 0x..., N blocks, M loaded (U unreadable), K ObjAct infos (state: one-state a, door b, one-loop c, one-dir-door d, none e, other f); S door sub infos skipped, B bad; D doors with a flag [...]`. This logs again the first time D > 0. On failure, once per step: `doors: scan failed: <step> (...)`. Also: `doors: flag F counts as a door flag (...)`, `doors: our ObjAct toggled door flag F off, but the friend had opened it: kept on`, `doors: the friend switched door flag F off (its ObjAct toggle); ignored, it stays on here`, `doors: the friend opened a door: door flag F ... opened (0x6BA910 ok = ...)`.

**Live test.** (1) Load in, both players. Read the `doors: scan:` line and report the per-class counts and D. (2) The friend opens a door or trapdoor. Look for `the friend opened a door: ... opened` (walked door) or `counts as a door flag` (fallback). (3) The host opens the same door: `kept on`, never `sent flag F = false`. (4) Hirata gate 21005024: both open it, then reload; it stays open on both machines. The friend's log must show `... ignored, it stays on here` if its toggle fired.

**Risks.** For OneState doors there is no flag-to-info mapping, so the friend's opening is not replayed. The door object only opens here after a reload. The guards still keep the flag on. A non-door map flag that the friend sets inside the ObjAct window becomes a candidate: it can no longer be switched off by a toggle or by the friend, except within 15 s after a rest.

## 38. No New Game+ in co-op, ending flags stay local, cross-cycle gate (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

**Problem.** NG+ resets every event flag and reloads the world (`0x140A8D130`): in a connected pair that desyncs both worlds. Also `should_sync` shared ids 20..=30 (ending / NextLap requests), so the companion would get the ending too.

**RE (IDA, [VAR] seen, [ARV] inferred).** InGameStep `0x140A71190`: flags 20-23 -> RequestToEnding (GameMan+0xB6C) + kind to GameDataMan+0x74; flag 30 -> RequestToNextLap (GameMan+0xB6E); both cleared. Post-credits prompt (texts 450000/450001): YES lambda `_Do_call` `0x1411DADA0` = `C6 05 F9 63 CC 02 01 C3` writes `byte_143EA11A0` (RVA `0x3EA11A0`) = 1; NO `0x1411DAE10` writes 0. State 7 `0x140A8B650` reads it: set -> state 8 -> commit `0x140A8D130` (flags reset, GameDataMan+0x70 += 1, map `0x0B020000`); clear -> `0x140A8D030` (vanilla "No": clear NextLap, map `0x0B000000`).

**Change.**
- world.rs `should_sync`: ids 20..=30 never shared. Record-level paths (snapshot of whole records) are not changed: that record also holds other flags.
- `ngblock.rs`, `[GAMEPLAY] block_ng_in_coop` (default 1). `should_block(enabled, mod_alive, link, latched)` = enabled && alive && (connected || latch).
  - (a) YES lambda detour. `install_detour` needs 5..=32 bytes: the 8-byte function is replaced whole (5-byte jmp + 3 int3), the trampoline (RIP-relative copy) is never called; the detour writes `base+0x3EA11A0 = 1` itself when not blocked, else nothing, so the game takes its own "No" path.
  - (b) commit detour (safety net): blocked -> calls `0x140A8D030(a1)`; else the original via the trampoline.
  - (c) "No" path hook, only clears the latch (so a block does not outlive the ending). The latch is set when the prompt answer or the commit runs while connected; the link dropping during the long credits is NOT covered (no hook point found before the prompt).
  - Notice: "New Game+ is off in co-op. Leave co-op, then use the Dilapidated Temple idol."
- doctor.rs `[GAMEPLAY] allow_cross_cycle` (default 0): both NG cycles known and different and the setting off -> `doctor: REFUSED different NG cycle (ours a, friend b)`, notice, and the CLIENT calls `net::set_intent(Intent::Solo)` (Severed Thread path). Host only gets the notice. With the setting on: the old warning. Pure `refuse(ours, theirs, allow)`. Needs no protocol change (cycle already in BULK_DOCTOR); an older build without the key is "unknown" = allowed.

**Log lines.** `ngblock: 2/2 hooks installed; declined-path hook on`, `ngblock: NG+ refused (link ...)`, `ngblock: NG+ step allowed`, `ngblock: the declined path (0xA8D030) ran, latch cleared`, `doctor: REFUSED different NG cycle (...)`.

**Live test.** Connected at the ending, answer YES to NG+: notice, back to the vanilla No path (Dilapidated Temple). Solo: NG+ starts as before. Pair NG vs NG+1: client leaves with the notice; with `allow_cross_cycle = 1` on both only the warning.

**Risks.** The commit's and "No" path's return types are unknown (both treated as returning a register value). Doctor runs ~3 s after joining, so the client is briefly in the host's world before leaving. The reload-on-leave path restores its world.

## 39. One deathblow per enemy: first-come lock (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Owner request: two players must not deathblow the same enemy at once; whoever starts first does it alone, the other's attempt simply does not happen (no double animation, no cancelled deathblows). Keeps v7.11 (a type 18 releases the native pin 4 s), v7.10 `own_getup_due` and the §34 claims (`ns_throw_claims` / `ns_throw_collide_drop` stay as they are, default 0). Code: `nsession.rs` (`dblock` module, `serve`, `route_send`, `install_hooks`, `tick`, `Frame::rtt_ms`), `puppet.rs` (passes the link RTT). No protocol change, no new message, no ini key (`const DEATHBLOW_LOCK = true`).

**What the game checks before a player's throw [VAR, IDA copy `F:\Temp\ida_dblock\db.i64`, jobs o1/o2; RE-BACKSTAB.md §a].** Input `0xBE0E50` -> ChrFinder per chr `0xBDF930` (hostile team, `0x7DCE60` active) -> `0xBDE5C0` -> candidates `0xBE2A30` -> per ThrowKind the defender gate `0xBE3810(atk chr, def chr, kind row)` (only caller `0xBE2A30`, used by both `0xBDE5C0` and `0xBDE1C0`) and the pair check `0xBE3A90` -> `0xBE2D60` starts it (input) or applies the red marker SpEffect (no input). `0xBE3810` rejects on: def vtable slot 170 (`chr+0x1A11 & 0x20`, `chr+0x1A13 & 4`, TAE action flags), action flags `0x10/0x20`, a ThrowNode without throwable state, throwable state `ThrowModule+0x7A/+0x7B` (TAE 932 / HKS act 161) vs the kind's category, the target lock (kind `+0x13`), and `defAliveCheck` (kind `+0x15`: 1 = not HP-zero, 2 = HP-zero, boss deathblow). Live §34: a defender already placed in the remote's throw still passed (bit-identical angles in both packets), so the game itself has no "already being deathblowed" gate [ARV]. Writing any of those state bits would fight the TAE/HKS every frame and change other behaviour [ARV]; the gate is the narrow point.

**Fix.**
- *Lock.* Our player's type 18 on an enemy (attacker = our player, defender an enemy netId) = our lock for 4 s after its latest packet; the friend's 18 (attacker = the friend's player) = the friend's lock. Each side already receives the other's 18, so nothing new goes on the wire.
- *Suppression.* `0xBE3810` is detoured (16-byte prologue `48 89 5C 24 18 55 56 57 41 54 41 55 41 56 41 57`, checked): the original runs first (its side effect on the attacker's ThrowModule `+0x151` stays), then a "yes" becomes "no" when the attacker is our own player and the defender's netId (`chr+0x78`) is locked by the friend (atomic bitset, set in `serve` the moment the friend's 18 is handed to the game). No candidate = no marker, no throw. Enemy grabs (an enemy attacking) and the friend's throws (applied through `0x7FAB00`, not this gate) are untouched. Installed with the native-session hooks at the title screen; not fatal if it fails.
- *Collision* (both started inside the latency window, before the other's packet arrived): `dblock::first_come`. The friend's start = arrival - RTT/2 (link RTT, 100 ms until measured); the earlier start wins, within 120 ms the host's. The winner does not give the loser's 18 to the game; the loser applies the winner's, which cuts its own throw off before its hit (§34) and plays the winner's deathblow on the loser's screen too. One copy loses the life, livesync lowers the other (`plan` holds while our own throw is recent, then `Lower` with the get-up refill). With RTT under ~200 ms every reachable collision is a tie, so both machines pick the host whatever the estimate error; at longer latency a wrong estimate can at worst let both run (never both cancelled when the RTT is underestimated). Unit test `both_machines_agree_on_one_winner` simulates both machines with their own clocks.
- With `DEATHBLOW_LOCK = false` the old one-sided `ns_throw_collide_drop` path is back.

**Log lines.** `deathblow: enemy 0x.. locked by us (our throw T, 4 s; ...)`, `deathblow: enemy 0x.. locked by peer (the friend's throw T; ...)`, `deathblow: our attempt on 0x.. suppressed (peer first): its deathblow marker / throw is off ...` (once per lock, from the gate), collisions: `deathblow: enemy 0x.. locked by us: the friend's throw T not applied here (ours started ~N ms earlier | both within 120 ms (+N ms): the host's goes first, rtt R ms)` / `deathblow: our attempt on 0x.. suppressed (peer first): the friend's throw T applied, ours gives way (...)`, a missed veto `deathblow: our throw T on enemy 0x.. started although the friend holds its lock (...)`, hook `hook: throw defender gate (0xBE3810) detoured ...`, every 10 s when anything happened `deathblow: 10 s: locks ours A / the friend's B, gate vetoes V, the friend's throws not applied D, ours gave way G, ours started during the friend's lock M (gate hooked)`.

**Live test (both PCs this build, native session stage 3).** (1) Host log at start: `throw defender gate (0xBE3810) detoured`. (2) The friend deathblows a soldier, the host stands next to it and presses deathblow during the animation: no marker, nothing happens on the host; host `locked by peer`, then `our attempt on 0x.. suppressed (peer first)`; one animation on both screens. Swap roles. (3) 2-life miniboss, break its posture together and both press at the same moment (count down by voice): exactly one deathblow plays on both screens, one orb gone on both, one side logs `not applied here`, the other `ours gives way`; no `posture` stuck low (the get-up after the orb as in v7.10). (4) Stealth backstab by one while the other walks up behind: the second gets no marker. (5) The host deathblows a friend-owned enemy (§34 case) with the friend nowhere near: it works, `locked by us` only. (6) Pin: `native: remote in a throw, pin released for 4 s` still appears on the loser's / onlooker's side.

**Risks.** The veto keys on the defender netId; an enemy whose `chr+0x78` is 0xFFFF (no netId) is never locked. In the latency window the loser's player animation starts and is cut (its first frames show on both screens via hkbsync) [ARV]. A friend still on 7.13 applies every 18 of ours, so a collision we lose cancels both there (the §34 bug) until both run this build. 4 s after the friend's latest 18 a second deathblow on that enemy is blocked for us; a boss getting up takes about that long anyway [ARV]. The gate runs for every throw candidate check of every chr (atomics only, a netId load only for our own player's "yes").

## 40. Dead-stuck fix: replay held load, dead watchdogs, rest releases (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Live bug (Genichiro, v7.14): the friend died in phase B, a load request `0x6D7070` (Lua event warp `0x68E850`) was held and never replayed; after the host's death the release came, but no death screen (menu state 250) or reload followed, the 30 s RELEASING timeout set OFF while still dead, and a rest did nothing for a dead player (160 s stuck). Code: `bossrules.rs`, `world.rs`.

- *Replay.* The detour keeps the arguments of the last held `0x6D7070` (`LOAD_HELD_ARGS`, `LOAD_HELD_PENDING`). Phase-B `release()` with a held request (`phase_b_release`) calls it via `LOAD_REQ_TRAMP` (state is RELEASING, so the detour lets it pass): `spectate: replayed held load request`. Without one the game's death step runs as before.
- *RELEASING watchdog* (`releasing_action`, replaces the 30 s timeout): dead, no load pending (`game::load_pending`), no menu 250: 15 s -> forced reload (`RELOAD_TRAMP`, the same call phase A uses), 15 s later -> `game::warp_to_idol(last_idol())`, 30 s after the reload -> OFF. Alive -> OFF. Load pending / death screen up -> wait.
- *Global watchdog* (`dead_watchdog`): dead, not spectating, state not RELEASING, no load, no menu 250 for 45 s -> `spectate: dead for N s with no death screen: forcing the reload` (then the RELEASING watchdog escalates to the warp).
- *Rests.* `rest_while_dead()` (friend rest in `World::apply_rest`, our own rest): waiting -> release "rest"; dead and OFF/RELEASING -> the frame task forces the reload (at once in RELEASING, else after 3 s dead idle). Alive and not waiting: unchanged mailbox rest.
- *Hard triggers* in `spectate_step` come first and cancel a pending "boss defeated" wait: EV_DEATH/EV_DOWN/rest from `RELEASE_REQ`, link lost / friend left block, `SPECTATE_MAX` (now 5 min), our restsync rest.
- *Wipe* by HP needs 5 s of continuous 0 HP (`DEAD_AFTER`, was 1 s); HP above 0 resets the timer. EV_DEATH still wipes at once.

Pure fns with tests: `releasing_action`, `dead_idle_due`, `should_release`, `spec_timeout`, `friend_wipe`, `phase_b_release`.

**Risks.** The replayed call's arguments may be stack pointers that are stale by then (the Lua warp passes plain values as far as seen, not verified). A forced reload in phase B skips the CSDeadStep (no death penalty). Menu state 250 = death screen is from one live log; if it is wrong the 15 s / 45 s watchdogs may fire while a death screen is up (reload would then just be what the screen does anyway). `warp_to_idol` ignores `safe_to_warp` (dead player).

## 41. Spectator camera: orbit and lock-on (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

While dead and spectating (`spectate_camera` 1/2) the player can now turn the camera and lock onto enemies. Code: `spectate_cam.rs` (orbit state, targets, pure math), new `spectate_input.rs` (input + pure helpers), call site `bossrules.rs` (`cam.update(.., boss_hint)`), keys in `tuning.rs` / `settings.rs`. Hook, restore and release are unchanged; only the published pose differs.

- *Orbit.* yaw / pitch / dist (4.5 m); cam = look + dist*(cos p cos y, sin p, cos p sin y), pitch clamped -35..+60 deg. Initialised from the render camera's direction on engage. `spectate_orbit = 0` runs the old fixed code (`step_fixed`) unchanged.
- *Source A* (`spectate_orbit = 1`, default): each frame the ChrCam forward row (`*(*(base+0x3D5C0A0)+0x30)`, row +0x30 [ARV/VAR]) -> yaw = atan2(z, x), pitch = asin(y); the wrapped yaw delta is added to the orbit yaw, the pitch delta is SUBTRACTED from the orbit pitch (looking up = the camera swings down; the spec text said "add"). Frames with |delta| > 45 deg are ignored. First motion logs `spectate: orbit input from the game camera`. 5 s without any motion logs `spectate: game camera frozen while dead, using XInput` and adds the stick; A stays read, so it still works if it wakes up later.
- *Source B* (`spectate_orbit = 2` or the fallback): XInput controller 0 right stick (xinput1_4.dll, fallback xinput9_1_0.dll), dead zone 8000, 150 deg/s x `spectate_orbit_speed`; retried once a second while no pad answers. Mouse not implemented (A already carries the mouse if the game camera turns). Keys and stick are read only while the game window is foreground.
- *Lock* (`spectate_lock`): rising edge of `spectate_lock_key` (default 4 = middle mouse) or R3, 250 ms debounce. Candidates every 0.5 s: boss from `boss_hint` (wait_boss, else last gauge entity; `chr_by_entity_id`) first, then `nsession::enemy_chrs()` with hp > 0 within 40 m of the friend, nearest first (boss up to 60 m). Lock with none = boss, else nearest. Framing: aim = target + `spectate_lock_height`; camera behind the friend opposite the target, look = lerp(friend look, aim, 0.35), orbit pitch = elevation of the aim -> friend line + 10 deg, smoothed with DIR_RATE / POS_RATE. Orbit input is ignored while locked except cycling: source A flick (> 20 deg of screen yaw within 0.3 s), stick x past 0.7 on its edge; next target = smallest screen-yaw offset to the right/left of the current one, wraps to the far end.
- *Drop*: target hp <= 0, unreadable or > 60 m from the friend -> nearest other candidate, else unlock and keep orbiting from the shown yaw / pitch. Also dropped on release / engage. Logs: `spectate: lock on <entity> (boss|enemy)`, `lock switch to ..`, `lock lost (<why>)`.
- *Recenter*: `spectate_recenter_s` > 0 swings the orbit yaw behind the friend (Facing learner) after that long without steering; 0 = never.
- *Failures*: NaN / failed read keeps the last pose; ChrCam unreadable logs once and A gives no input (the 5 s rule then enables the stick).

Pure fns with tests: `orbit_cam`, `orbit_angles`, `apply_orbit` (clamp, wrap), `lock_goal`, `build_targets`, `cycle_target`, `drop_reason`, `recenter_due`; in `spectate_input`: `fwd_angles`, `angle_delta`, `stick_axis`, `stick_orbit`, `stick_edge`, `debounce_ok`, `frozen_after`, `Flick`.

**Risks.** Everything live is unknown: whether ChrCam still turns while the player is dead (else only the stick works, after 5 s); sign of yaw for the stick depends on `hand` (derived, not seen); the target Chr pointer is held up to 0.5 s between rebuilds (reads are fault-safe, a freed slot could briefly show another enemy); an enemy or boss on the friend's line makes no collision check for the camera; GetAsyncKeyState sees the middle mouse even if the game ignores it.

## 42. Get-up window, single-life bosses, persist restore gate (7.15, NOT verified live)

Evidence: live 7.13 (Genichiro -> Way of Tomoe), 5 clips + both logs. livesync.rs / persist.rs only.
- **Drop before dead.** The scan that sees lives 2 -> 1 can also read HP 0; `dead` was true, `own_drop` never recorded, the host's own deathblow on a NetAI copy never got up. Now `lives_drop` / `getting_up` run BEFORE the dead test (HP <= 0 with a life left inside a drop or the window = getting up). A NetAI copy (`!owned_here`) is refilled at once; an owned copy gets `OWN_GETUP_AFTER` (1.5 s), then `own_getup_due` also fires when HP <= 1 % of max (not only posture). Log: `lives a -> b (seen here), getting up`.
- **Get-up window** `GETUP_HOLD` 6 s replaces `REFILL_GUARD` 2 s: HP <= 1 is re-refilled on either side (owned copies only after 1.5 s). The friend's stale low type-5 HP is applied in nsession.rs and is NOT gated; the immediate re-refill (scan 150 ms) counters it instead. The "host HP above 1 for 1 s" gate is not implemented.
- **Lives never rise.** `livesync::set_lives` is the only writer used (Lower, Kill, persist restore): a raise is logged, refused, `debug_assert`.
- **Single-life bosses** (`boss_tracked`: gauge entity or `bosses.rs` final entity): death on either side already went through `plan` (Kill / Hold on a recent throw) since `entry` sends deaths of any enemy; pinned with tests and a log tag. Lower stays multi-life only (field reads 0).
- **persist.** Boss records are restored only on a load after a committed death of ours (`DEATH_COMMITTED` edge counter vs count at last load) and only for the load's initial set (first member of the encounter seen <= 5 s earlier), never for a phase entity at the gauge swap. The wipe pair counts committed deaths only (ours: `DEATH_COMMITTED` edge; friend: EV_DEATH); HP 0 during a resurrection no longer counts. Risk: a restore after a walk-away reload without a death is gone by design.

## 43. Idol activation sync (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Problem: an idol is "lit" by an entry in `NS_SPRJ::FrpgNetBonfireDb` (`*(base+0x3D5A6A8)+0xBC0`), not by its unlock event flag. A friend's synced flag (or one copied raw by the join snapshot / own-world restore) never creates the entry, so the idol stayed unlit and resting there played first-light again. `idols.rs`:
- `ensure_lit(entity)`: guards (DB owner and `+0xBC0` non-zero, flag manager ready, no load pending), `IS_LIT 0x76E080`; if unlit `LIGHT 0x76EE60(entity, false)` (no packet), refresh byte `base+0x3D5A1B4 = 1`, re-check. Still unlit: fallback A (entity into `base+0x3AFEB88` if that slot is 0; works only if the idol is loaded; the block is not checked, there is no entity-to-block-index map) and an error log. Fallback B (`0x76F8D0`) is not wired.
- Feeders, drained in `World::on_frame` -> `idols::tick` (game thread): `apply_flag` queues the idol when a friend's unlock flag goes ON (`note_flag_on`); reconcile every 5 s, once after every load (in-world timer reset), and on `request_reconcile()` (called after the join snapshot apply). An own-world restore ends in a reload, which the load edge covers. Reconcile lights flag-on-and-unlit idols, at most 20 per tick. Nothing runs before 2 s in the world.
- Rows (entity, flag) are re-read from BonfireWarpParam in `idols.rs` (same filter as ownworld's `idol_rows`, which is private).
- Logs: `idols: lit idol E (flag F) from another player's unlock (lit = true)`, `idols: reconcile lit N idols [..]`, `idols: E still unlit after the call`, `idols: DB not ready, retry` (throttled to 5 s).
- Pure fns with tests: `to_light` (no row, flag off, already lit, cap), `guard`.

**Risks.** All offsets and the call convention are from IDA, never run live: a wrong signature crashes in `ensure_lit`. Reconcile also lights idols whose flag came from our own save but whose entry is missing (intended). The 2 s gate and 5 s period are guesses. Fallback A may fire for an unloaded idol and be consumed later at the wrong time.

## 44. Story phases and dragonrot on NPCs (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

**A. Story phases.** [VERIFIED in code] Every story-phase flag is group 0, k = 8: `should_sync` was false and `shared_ks(0)` = 0/1/5/9 never dumped them, so each machine ran its own phase. `world.rs`:
- `STORY_PHASE = [8301, 8302, 8304, 8306, 8310]` (8301 Interior Ministry invasion, set by common event 910; 8302 Ashina burning / 2nd invasion; 8304 Hirata 2nd memory; 8306 prologue; 8310 castle enemies of the 8302 phase [INFERRED]). `should_sync` is true for them. 8303, 8305, 8307, 8330, 8335, 9530 and the endings `ENDINGS = [6830..6833]` stay local (endings: log and doctor only).
- Host authoritative, any N peers: `send_allowed(id, is_host)` is false for a phase id on a client (the ring entry is dropped, logged `world: phase flag N from our script held (host authoritative)`); the host ignores a phase EV_FLAG from a client before relaying it; `apply_flag` takes phase ids only on a client (so only from the host). A client keeps `host_phase`, which host flags update.
- Join: `BULK_PHASE = 8` (free: 1, 5, 6, 7, 20-23, 30, 41-45, 50, 60, 0x4E, roster 0x52 are taken), u32 LE mask, bit i = `STORY_PHASE[i]`. `host_dump` sends it to each joiner right after the records. The client's `apply_dump` waits up to 3 s for it (only when the dump status is OK), then `apply_phase` writes all five flags with SetEventFlag in one frame, after `known.clear()` and before `ownworld::after_join_snapshot` and the join warp. Log `world: story phase <names> applied (was <names>)`.
- Repair: once a second in the world (settled, flag manager ready, dump done) a client whose mask differs from `host_phase` re-applies the whole host mask (`... repaired`). The host re-sends `BULK_PHASE` to all clients when its own mask changes (`phase_sent`). A fresh `BULK_PHASE` makes the client compare at once.
- `ownworld.rs` file VERSION 4 (`own_phase` captured in `before_join_snapshot` on the first snapshot, `base_phase` after the phase is applied in `after_join_snapshot`; a v3 file reads with none and the phase is then left alone). `phase_at_restore(own, base, live, mirror)`: mirror -> live; live != base -> live; else own. Merge restore writes the five flags (on and off) and logs `own world: story phase taken from session|kept own: ...`. Mirror writes nothing.
- `doctor.rs`: keys `phase` (hex mask), `endings`. Phase differs -> log + notice "Story phase differs (host X, you Y): host's world applies." (on the host: "companion"); endings log only. `?` or a missing key (older build) is no mismatch.

**B. Dragonrot on NPCs.**
1. `PER_PLAYER_STATE` gains 627 and 70..=75 (Dragon's Blood Droplet cure: common event 626 waits for 627, resets the counter, sets 70-75; group 0, k = 0, so it was shared and a client's cure cured the host's world). They are kept across the join dump (`restore_death_flags`) and, new, across the own-world merge restore (`keep`). `world::per_player_state(id)` is used by ownworld's `per_player`. 626, 76, 69 stay shared.
2. `keyitems.rs`: `classify` returns Never for goods 9500..=9526 (Rot Essences, non-consumed key items). `build_index` filters through `classify`, so the key item list (`BULK_KEY_ITEMS`), `owned_keys`, the repair and every grant path leave them out. 9560 (Dragonrot Blood Sample) stays shareable.
3. `doctor.rs`: key `rot` = Rot Essences held (`keyitems::owned_rot_essences`, same `owns` reader as `owned_keys`); differs -> log + notice "Dragonrot differs: NPCs show your own sickness."

Tests: `story_phase_flags_sync`, `client_phase_write_not_sent`, `phase_mask_round_trip`, `dragonrot_cure_flags_stay_local` (world); `phase_taken_whole_when_session_changed`, `phase_own_kept_when_unchanged`, `phase_mirror_keeps_live`, `file_v3_reads_without_phase` (ownworld); `phase_mismatch_warns` (doctor); `rot_essences_never_shared` (keyitems).

**Risks.** Never run live. The rot essence id range and the flag meanings (esp. 8310) come from the spec. A client's script that legitimately advances a phase (e.g. 8301 on entering the Ministry first) is undone within a second by the repair until the host gets there; intended. If the host build has no `BULK_PHASE` (older), the client waits 3 s for it, then keeps its own phase. In the own-world merge fallback that writes before the warp, a rollback restores the records but not the phase flags. The ring-drop of a client's phase write is a log only; the local value stays until the repair runs.

## 45. Inventory guard and backup retention (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Trigger: a friend's 200 Spirit Emblems (goods 1000, `0x400003E8`) vanished in a co-op session; they were in STORAGE (save slot offset ~0x987B4, the list after the key-item list; carried amount ~0x8F950). No mod code writes the inventory (checked), cause unknown.

**A. `invguard.rs` (watch only, never writes game memory).** `checkpoint(why)` reads the carried emblem amount (`audit::read_inventory`, now `pub(crate)`) and sen (`PlayerGameData.player_info.sen`, offset from the vendor struct) and logs `invguard: emblems carried C storage S|unknown, sen M (<why>)`. Called: join and leave edge and every 60 s (`tick`, from `ownworld::on_frame` next to `backups::tick`), before and after each own-world restore (`restore` wraps `restore_inner`), before each mod-called rest (`restsync::note`, which also calls `note_rest` so a drop after a rest is not flagged). A total drop of more than 20 with no rest and no item use since the last sample logs `audit: SUSPECT inventory: Spirit Emblems total a -> b (<why>)` and an overlay notice (`bossrules::notice`). Item use = the carried amount fell by 1..=20 and the total fell by at most that plus 20 (`explained_by_use`). Pure `storage_drop(before, after, rested, used)` with table tests.
- **[ARV] Storage is not located in memory.** The vendor `EquipInventoryData` has only the normal and key lists; no storage box offset found in the vendor crate, Cheat Engine tables or our docs. So the guard watches carried + sen only and logs `storage unknown` (once: `storage box list not located in memory [ARV]`). `Sample.storage` is already `Option` so the reader can be added with one change. Consequence: a 200 loss from storage alone is NOT detected yet; a loss of carried emblems is.

**B. Backup retention (`backups.rs`).** `to_prune(names, keep, now)` is still the newest-`KEEP` rotation but never returns: a backup younger than 2 h; the last backup of each of the 3 days before today; the first backup of each of today and the 2 days before. The total can exceed `KEEP` (about 3 + 3 + KEEP at most). Previous days older than 3 days fall back to plain rotation (a literal "never delete the last of any past day" would grow without bound). Days are local time from the file names. Tests with fixed names: `retention_protects_young_last_of_day_and_first_of_day`, `retention_still_rotates_when_nothing_is_protected`, `day_numbers_cross_month_and_year`.

## 46. Enemy-ownership desync: hysteresis, double claims, paused world update (2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Live 2026-10-24 14:13-14:17: "claimed by both machines" in 13 of the friend's summaries (up to 6 enemies); "taken 127, given back 112" in one engagement (enemies 40-48 m away); "ownerless 396/397" for 232 enemies; our world update stopped (menu) so what we own stood still on every screen. All in `nsession.rs`:

1. **Ownfill hysteresis.** Take at <= `NEAR_M` (40 m) as before; `keep` now fails only beyond `NEAR_M + GIVE_BACK_EXTRA_M` (48 m) or when another player is clearly nearer, and the enemy is given back only after `keep` has been false for `GIVE_BACK_AFTER` (5 s) without a break (`give_back_due`, 4th tuple field of `Fill::ours`). Alternating 39/41 m: one take, no give-back.
2. **Double claims.** Client side: a claim of ours (`claims_raw`) on an enemy that a lower slot (the host first) also lists as owned, while we own it too for `DOUBLE_CLAIM_AFTER` (2.5 s, longer than the 2 s Owned period), is dropped (`double_claim_drop`, `DROPPED_CLAIMS`, filtered out of `claims_now`); logged once per enemy per engagement. The host never drops. The dropped list clears when the claim ends or the engagement tears down.
3. **Ownerless counter.** `OwnerTally` counts each netId once (an enemy can sit in several chr sets).
4. **Paused world update.** When `updates_at` is older than `WORLD_PAUSED_AFTER` (1.5 s) while engaged (`PauseTracker`), the enemies we own that another unpaused player would take under the ownfill rule (`handover_targets`) are left out of our Owned list, which also carries the marker netId `0xFFFE` (sorts last, older builds ignore it). Peers exclude marked peers from the ownfill competition, so the nearest one takes them within one Owned period (and our own ownfill is off while paused). Log: `nsession: our world update paused: N enemies handed to peer P`. When the update runs again (stalled < 0.5 s) the full list returns at once and the existing rules settle it (higher slot drops at once, the lowest after 3 s; the dwell above stops flapping). No protocol change, `PROTO` unchanged.

## 47. Cutscene state and NPC talk lock (v8.3, 2026-10-24, NOT verified live)

Evidence tags: [VAR] static IDA (`F:\Temp\ida_102`, headless re-check 2026-10-24), [ARV] live logs, [I] inferred. Nothing here has run in the game.

**A. Cutscene state** (`cutscene.rs`).
- Detection [VAR]: `SprjRemoImp* = *(base+0x3D8DC08)`, `RemoMan = *(SprjRemoImp+0x8)`; playing = `*(u32)(RemoMan+0x144) & 1`; finished = `step == -1 || (step == 1 && pending == 0)` with `step = *(i32)(RemoMan+0x40)`, `pending = *(u64)(RemoMan+0xD0)`; active = playing and not finished. `active()` is safe reads only. `remo_id()` calls `base+0xED1980(SprjRemoImp)` once per cutscene (at the start edge, only while active), for the log.
- Log: `cutscene: start id X`, `cutscene: end after N s`, `cutscene: peers in a cutscene: [slots]`, `cutscene: re-placed to [..] in block B (reason; write ok = ..)`. `statelog.rs` Snap gained `remo (flags, step, pending)` (full line and diffs of the `state:` lines).
- Protocol **VERSION 12 -> 13**: the snapshot's hidden byte is bits (bit 0 hidden, bit 1 in a cutscene), `Snapshot.cutscene: bool`. net.rs sends `hidden = true, cutscene = true` while our `active()` holds (player present), so the other side hides our puppet while the script moves us. A cutscene snapshot is hidden but is NOT a loading screen: `PeerState::on_snapshot` leaves `loading_at` and `dead_since` alone for it (else `loaded_since` would end a peer's boss fight at every mid-fight cutscene). `PeerState::in_cutscene()` (fresh snapshot, hidden ones included) feeds `PeerFrame.cutscene`.
- `party_state` = 3 ("(in cutscene)", `ui::state_tag`) before the away check; the tag also shows next to the head-mode HP bar. A peer in a cutscene has no nameplate (it is hidden), so the tag is seen in the party list; a nameplate "(in cutscene)" would need a nameplate for a hidden peer and was not added.
- `any_in_cutscene()` = ours or `PEERS_IN` (set every frame from all peers' latest snapshots). Users: `game::safe_to_warp` returns `Err("cutscene")` for OUR cutscene; `WarpWait::poll_at(now, paused, ..)` shifts its start by the frame delta (capped at 1 s) while anybody is in one, so the 20 s give-up does not run; `bossgate::tick_peer` freezes that peer's `pre` list (`freeze_pre`: PRE_HOLD 4 s and PRE_HOLD_LEARNED 90 s count only outside its cutscene); `bossrules.rs`: no expel start (`EXPEL_REQ` stays up), `postpone_release` holds a spectate release (`held_release`, retried every frame, at most `POSTPONE_MAX` 60 s), `spec_since` (SPECTATE_MAX) and `dead_idle_since` (DEAD_IDLE_MAX) are shifted by the frame delta, and a watched peer in a cutscene is neither "gone" nor "dead" (its snapshot reads hidden).
- Placement (`Tracker`): at the start edge we keep P0 (position, angle) and the block; an end needs 300 ms of "not active". Two seconds after the end: Y more than 10 m below P0.y, or the block is not P0's and no peer is in it -> back to P0; the death latch (`base+0x3EA0D74`) rising within 5 s -> back to P0. Written with `Chr::set_position` plus the UpdateCoords hook (`hook::set_target(native::pin_slot(me), ..)`: our own id's pin slot is free; held 30 frames). A player whose start position was unreadable is never moved.
